# GalacticAgents — Technology Recommendations

**Status:** Proposal v0.3 (§9 Deliverables added 2026-10-07 for Phase 9) · **Date:** 2026-09-30 · **Client decisions:** React + React Three Fiber confirmed (Flutter set aside for the 3D client; a Flutter companion app for the side panel remains a possible later phase). Review decisions of 2026-09-30 incorporated: WebGL-only renderer for v1 (SPEC decision 20), Agent SDK adapter as the day-one Claude transport, brains run through that adapter, subscription login without `--bare` (decision 17), hard caps from Phase 1 (decision 18), simplified monorepo.

This document recommends a technology stack for the system described in `SPEC.md`, explains the reasoning, and lists the alternatives that were considered. The two constraints that shaped every choice:

1. **The agents are mixed CLIs** (Claude Code today; Codex, Gemini CLI, custom scripts later) running on the user's computer.
2. **The UI must eventually be reachable from a phone** while the computer runs the agents.

Constraint 2 rules out a "desktop app with everything inside it" design. The right shape is a **local server that owns the agents, plus a web UI that can be opened locally today and remotely later**.

The domain and event vocabulary used below is defined once, in `packages/protocol` (§7). Where this document and `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` name fields or events, they reference that package rather than restate it.

---

## 1. Architecture at a glance

```
┌──────────────────────── user's computer ────────────────────────┐
│                                                                  │
│   ┌───────────────┐  Agent SDK (TS)    ┌──────────────────┐     │
│   │  Claude Code  │◄──────────────────►│                  │     │
│   │  (Stewards)   │  claude-sdk adapter│   Mission        │     │
│   ├───────────────┤                    │   Control        │     │
│   │  Harmonizer / │  Agent SDK (TS)    │   server         │     │
│   │  Waykeepers   │◄──────────────────►│   (Node/TS)      │     │
│   │  (brains)     │  same adapter +    │                  │     │
│   ├───────────────┤  domain tools      │  • agent runtime │     │
│   │  Codex CLI    │  JSON-RPC / stdio  │  • adapters      │     │
│   │  Gemini CLI   │◄ - - - - - - - - ─►│  • brain mailbox │     │
│   │  (later, ACP) │                    │  • status store  │     │
│   ├───────────────┤  PTY / stream      │  • SQLite        │     │
│   │  other CLIs   │◄ - - - - - - - - ─►│  • WebSocket API │     │
│   │  (later)      │                    │                  │     │
│   └───────────────┘                    └────────┬─────────┘     │
│                                                 │ ws:// + https  │
│              ┌──────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┐  │
│              │  Web UI (React + React Three Fiber, WebGL)     │  │
│              │  opened in a browser / Tauri shell / PWA       │  │
│              └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
└──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┘
                               │ Tailscale Serve (private HTTPS)
                        ┌──────┴──────┐
                        │  phone PWA  │
                        └─────────────┘
```

Solid arrows are v1; dashed arrows are deferred adapters behind the same `AgentAdapter` interface.

---

## 2. Recommended stack

### 2.1 Frontend: React 19 + React Three Fiber v9 + Three.js (WebGL)

**Recommendation:** React 19, [React Three Fiber v9](https://r3f.docs.pmnd.rs/tutorials/v9-migration-guide), `@react-three/drei`, `@react-three/postprocessing`, Three.js r17x with the classic **`WebGLRenderer`**, Zustand for state, Vite for tooling, TypeScript throughout.

Why this over Angular + Three.js (your daily stack): the "spectacular solar system" is the heart of this product, and the React 3D ecosystem is an order of magnitude larger. R3F gives declarative scene graphs that map naturally onto your domain (`<Planet>`, `<Satellite agent={…}>`, `<AsteroidBelt tasks={…}>`), drei provides camera controls, orbit helpers, HTML labels-in-3D, instancing helpers and loaders out of the box, and there are many open-source solar-system scenes to borrow from. Angular can host Three.js, but you would write all of that plumbing yourself, and Angular's change detection fights against a 60 fps render loop.

Rendering notes:

- **WebGL only in v1 (decision 20).** Three.js also ships a `WebGPURenderer` (with WebGL2 fallback), but it uses a different material/shader system (TSL node materials), and `@react-three/postprocessing` plus several drei helpers target the classic renderer. v1 needs nothing WebGPU offers (no compute shaders, no scene dense enough to need it), so one renderer, one quality matrix and the full helper ecosystem win. Keep hand-written GLSL to a minimum so a later move to WebGPU (GPU-driven belt simulation, say) stays feasible.
- The asteroid belt must be **instanced** (one draw call for thousands of rocks). Only the asteroids that host tasks get individual meshes/labels.
- Use `three-mesh-bvh` for fast raycasting/picking once the scene is dense.
- Level of detail (LOD) is mandatory: low-poly spheres at solar-system scale, textured spheres with bump/roughness detail maps when zoomed, and a separate "planet detail" sub-scene for surface/cutaway views. Do not try to render surface robots at solar-system zoom.
- Scale is fake: real orbital distances are unusable on a screen. Use logarithmic or hand-tuned spacing and keep a "true scale" toggle as a fun extra.
- Postprocessing: bloom for the sun and scanning beams, selective outline for the selected body. Keep a "reduced effects" quality tier for mobile.
- **Quality tiers** (P3-01, `apps/web/src/scene/quality.ts`, one table): `high` = DPR ≤ 2, MSAA, bloom 1.4, 9000 stars, shadows allowed; `balanced` = DPR ≤ 1.5, MSAA, bloom 1.0, 5000 stars, no shadows; `mobile` = DPR 1, no MSAA, no composer (a gradient sprite fakes the Sun's glow), 2000 stars, no shadows; belt rocks 6000 / 3500 / 1200 (P3-03). Bloom's luminance threshold is 1.0 (linear, before tone mapping): the Sun's emissive (≈ 1.95) blooms, the stars (capped at 0.85) never do. The tier comes from the server's settings (`qualityTier`, SPEC decision 51) and applies at once in this browser (another device's change after a reload); only a change of the context's antialiasing recreates the GL context. MSAA lives in the composer where there is one (`multisampling` 4); the context itself is multisampled only on a tier with MSAA and no composer (`contextAntialias`, P3-08: a multisampled canvas under a composer only resolved a full-screen quad).
- **Bodies and scale** (P3-02, `apps/web/src/scene/bodies/`, `scale.ts`, `motion.ts`; SPEC decision 63). Planets and moons are rendered from `world.bodies` (catalogue order `meta.orbit`, moons by `parentId`); a planet or moon made outside the catalogue (no `meta.orbit`: the early `/dev/seed` "Demo planet n"; since P7-07 a demo planet is never reused and is removed from the world by `body.retired` once its project is archived) is drawn only while it hosts work or shows a "moved to …" marker, or one of its moons is drawn (`layout.ts` `drawn`, P4-13), so a free one has no anchor, label or pick target; dressed by a static per-name appearance table (style, palette, real radius and tilt, rings for Saturn and Uranus); unknown bodies get a default grey look. Surfaces are procedural `DataTexture`s painted at runtime from seeded 3D value noise: no image assets. Earth alone also has its real coastlines (P3-09, `earth-mask.ts`): a 1024 × 512 land/sea mask rasterised from Natural Earth's public-domain 1:50m land polygons by `apps/web/scripts/earth-mask.mjs` and committed as a run-length module (11 kB, in the scene chunk and the paint worker only). The painter samples it bilinearly from a small pyramid of averaged levels matching the texture size, with a noisy threshold along the coasts, then adds latitude biomes, a short table of arid regions (the deserts), ice on Antarctica, Greenland and the high Arctic, and shallow coastal seas. Equirectangular u = 0 is 180°W growing east and v = 0 the south pole, so on three's `SphereGeometry` the prime meridian faces local +X and the map is not mirrored. Earth's texture key carries the mask version (`EARTH_MASK_VERSION`). Fake scale is a monotonic AU → scene knot table (Mercury 16 … Neptune 192, so the belt at 2.2–3.3 AU lands at ≈ 52–66, between Mars's moons and Jupiter's); true scale is 35 units/AU with sizes and moon orbits ×≈85. The star-field shell (1200–2400) is centred on the camera just before it is drawn (`onBeforeRender`), so it is a real backdrop at any zoom and scale (the true-scale overview sits ≈ 2550 from the Sun on a landscape view). One orbit convention for every body (`scene/orbit.ts`): angle 0 on +x, counter-clockwise seen from +y, inclination lifts toward +y; placement writes into the object's position (no per-frame allocation). The frame loop is `always` while decorative motion runs and `demand` under `prefers-reduced-motion` (one shared `useReducedMotion` in `scene/motion.ts`). Shadows (P3-10, `bodies/eclipse.ts`, tiers with `shadows`) are **eclipse shading, not a shadow map**: each planet, moon and ring system is darkened in its own fragment shader by the casters of its **own planet system** only, the planet and its moons of real radius ≥ 1000 km (the Moon, the Galilean moons, Titan, Triton; at most 6 per system, largest first). The shader tests the ray from the fragment to the Sun against those spheres (centre and radius as uniforms, written in each receiving mesh's `onBeforeRender`, when every world matrix is current) and scales only the Sun's direct light, with a soft edge of `t × R_sun / d_sun` capped at a quarter of the caster's radius. So a moon's shadow is a spot its own size on its planet, the planet shades its rings and eclipses its moons, and a planet never eclipses another. The Sun's point-light cube shadow it replaces magnified every caster by d_receiver / d_caster; in the readable layout Mercury, Venus, Earth and Mars threw shadows wider than Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus and Neptune whenever they lined up, about 13 % of the time. The patch is one `onBeforeCompile` shared by every body material (one program, `customProgramCacheKey`), anchored on three's `common`, `project_vertex` and `lights_fragment_end` chunks; a test fails if three renames them. Rings cast nothing.
- **Asteroid belt** (P3-03, `apps/web/src/scene/belt/`). The asteroid generator (mulberry32, `BELT_SEED`, `BELT_ORBIT` = 2.2–3.3 AU and ±0.15 rad, `asteroidSpec(n)`) lives in `packages/protocol` (`belt.ts`): the server's catalogue names and places task asteroids with it, the client draws the decorative belt from the same seed and ranges. The background belt is one `InstancedMesh` (one jittered-icosahedron geometry, one material, per-instance scale, rotation and colour, a bounding sphere over all instances): one draw call whatever the count. Every rock takes a fixed number of PRNG draws, so a lighter tier draws a prefix of the same belt. Only asteroids that host work get individual meshes (rock, additive halo, an invisible pick sphere carrying `userData {bodyId, kind, hostId}`; their labels are the scene's domain labels, P3-07), and each registers its anchor. The belt's `phase` follows the scene's orbit convention (counter-clockwise seen from +y, like the planets); the protocol's phase is just a uniform angle. The rock count comes in as a prop from `SolarScene`'s quality object. The belt spins at 0.004 rad/s from `useFrame` delta.
- **Anchors** (Phase 3 wave 2, `scene/anchors.ts`): every rendered body registers the `Object3D` carrying its world position under its `Body.id` (the Sun under `b-sun`), with its visual radius at the current scale and a stable `order` within its kind (planets by catalogue orbit, moons by planet orbit × 1000 + rank, asteroids by `meta.number`); the camera, picking and labels find bodies only there. One shared binding (`scene/anchor-binding.ts`: `attachAnchor`, `useBodyAnchor`) registers them all.
- **Camera** (P3-04, `apps/web/src/scene/camera/`; SPEC decision 64): drei `OrbitControls` (damping 0.08) driven by a three-free `CameraDirector` (free / flying / following) that reads bodies only through the anchors. Requests go through a zustand `useCameraStore` (`flyTo(bodyId)`, `back()`, `next()`, `previous()`, `focusedId`), usable outside the canvas. Framing distance = radius / sin(0.3 × the narrower half FOV); flights are eased over 0.8–1.5 s; the approach keeps the current direction unless its line of sight passes within 1.5 Sun radii, else it turns to cos ≥ 0.3 away from the Sun; flights keep 2 Sun radii from the Sun and the camera 1.3. Next / previous go Sun, planets, moons, asteroids, each by the anchors' stable `order` (never by live distance). The overview frames the planets' orbits: the farthest non-moon anchor's distance plus its radius (× 3 for a planet); moons are ignored, so Nereid does not zoom true scale out. The rig reads anchors lazily: a free camera reads none per frame, a flight or a follow reads its one body, and the Sun radius and extent are cached until the anchors or the scale change. A released camera drops its pending request and a newly mounted rig ignores the request already in the store, so hiding and showing the scene or a tier switch never replays an old flight. The rig calls `invalidate()` during flights (needed when the loop is `demand`), raises `camera.far` with the zoom-out limit, and treats `prefers-reduced-motion` (the shared `scene/motion.ts` helper) as instant.
- **Picking** (`apps/web/src/scene/picking/`): ray vs the anchors' bounding spheres (centre + visual radius) with screen-space sizes measured at each body's depth — a minimum 6 px target, a 6 px cluster slop, and a 12 px "clear target" threshold below which several candidates open the "what's here" list. No mesh raycast and no `three-mesh-bvh`: about 40 spheres per pick, independent of LOD meshes; the instanced belt is not pickable. Pointer events are read on the canvas element (a release within 4 px of its press is a click; 10 px for touch and pen), hover is picked at most once per frame, on pointer moves and on camera moves. The selection outline is postprocessing's `Outline` (the composer runs with `autoClear={false}`) over the selected anchor's meshes, re-collected per frame (LOD-safe); it stays mounted, and its selection is empty for the default Harmonizer selection (Earth gets the ring instead), because a non-empty selection costs a depth pass over the whole scene every frame (about +70 draw calls at high). The mobile tier, without a composer, uses a camera-facing ring. Panel → scene sync flies only on user selection changes (`SelectionSync`). **Selection both ways** (P5-01; audit in `docs/tasks/notes/P5-01-selection.md`): every selection — navigation rows, card and history links, notifications, back/forward, the scene's picks — goes through the one `navigate` of the app store, so the URL follows every path; the sync flies for any change not made by the scene (`selectFromScene`) except to the Harmonizer. A selection that arrives together with a switch back to the panel's main view from a page without the scene (settings, history) is kept like a deep link (a module-level store subscription in `picking/sync.ts`) and flies once the scene mounts and the body is drawn; a change made in list mode does not fly when the scene is shown later. During a replay (P4-07) flights and picks use `sceneWorld` (the replayed world) while the panel shows the live agent; a panel selection whose agent has no body in the replayed world is dropped, not kept waiting (the camera never jumps mid-playback). Deselecting from the scene (`deselectFromScene`): a click release on empty space with no popover open (with one open, the click only closes it), or `Escape` once there is no popover and the camera is on no body — Escape unwinds popover → camera body → selection → whole-system framing; `Home` stays camera-only; the camera never moves on a deselect. **Satellites are pick targets** (P4-08, `picking/scene-pick.ts`; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P4-08-satellite-hover.md`): each visible, present frame of `satellites/registry.ts` is one more sphere (its live `position`, half its `size`) with the same 6 px minimum target and no cluster slop; a satellite counts only when the ray passes through its target and its line of sight is not cut by a body's drawn sphere (a satellite behind its planet is hidden by it). A satellite under the pointer wins over the bodies that are clear targets (≥ 12 px, the planet it orbits seen up close) and over labels; of several, the nearest to the pointer on screen; when a speck of a body is under the pointer too (the system view), everything goes to the "what's here" list, satellites first in a stable order (role, then agent id — never per-frame screen distance), and the hover names the body with "+n", not a satellite that would change every frame. Hovering one shows its agent's card in place of the hover label (still one DOM element: `SatelliteHoverCard`, content from `satellite-card.ts` — the panel's `agentLabel`, the state pill, `StatusRecord.currentActivity` on one line cut at a word near 90 characters, the body and the mode when not plain orbiting); the card is positioned at the satellite's projected point and follows it: while any satellite is drawn (or one is hovered) and the pointer rests on the canvas the hover is re-picked once per frame, inside R3F's frame after the satellites' director wrote their positions (no one-frame lag); the hover's position is not in the picking store's React-selected state but in a separate channel (`moveHover` / `onHoverMove`) from which the card and the body label write their own `transform`, and the store's `hover` changes only when what is hovered (or its mode or "+n") does, so the overlay does not re-render at frame rate; the frame loop is invalidated only when the hover actually changed, so the `demand` loop still rests. **Hover grace** (`picking/hover-grace.ts`): a satellite picked by the rule above stays hovered (card and ring following it) while the pointer stays within 6 px of where it was picked, then, once the pointer has moved further, while the pointer is within max(the satellite's screen radius, 14 px) of its projected centre; it is released when the satellite is occluded by a body, off screen or gone (not visible / presence 0), when the popover opens, when the pointer leaves the canvas, or 3 s after the pointer last moved; off under reduced motion. The hovered satellite gets the faint camera-facing ring. A click on a satellite selects its agent (`waykeeper:<id>` / `steward:<id>`) and flies to its body (`activateSatellite`): the satellite hovered (or kept) at the press is activated on a click release without re-picking, as long as it is still visible and not occluded (it may have orbited out from under the pointer); the click-vs-drag rule is unchanged. Per-frame cost: the pick spheres, the ray and the anchors' positions live in scratch buffers reused from pick to pick (`anchorList()` / `satelliteFrames()` return the registries' own arrays, rebuilt only on register / publish), and the canvas's page rectangle is cached (dropped on resize, scroll, pointer re-entry and canvas size changes).
- **LOD** (P3-06, `scene/bodies/lod.ts`, `textures.ts`, `painter.ts`):
  - Each planet and moon picks a level from the tier's `lod` table by the camera's distance in body radii (so it works in both scales), with 15 % hysteresis.
  - A level swaps only the mesh's geometry (unit spheres per level, shared by all bodies, scaled by the mesh) and its material's maps. The anchor group is never replaced and its binding does not depend on the level.
  - The far map (256/128) is painted at once per body. Nearer maps are painted lazily in a Web Worker, one job at a time, and dropped if no longer wanted (the running one by terminating the worker). On a level change the body keeps its current maps until the new level's are ready. Without a worker, near maps are capped at the middle level's size with no detail map (no 2 s main-thread paints). Rocky and Earth-like surfaces also get an RG detail map from the same noise, used as `bumpMap` (R) and `roughnessMap` (G: glossy oceans; on Earth both follow the mask: relief only on land, gloss only on the sea).
  - Near maps live in a reference-counted cache keyed by content and size, kept idle within the tier's budget (96/32/8 MB, least recently used out) and dropped on a tier change. Not three's `LOD` / drei `<Detailed>`: those swap child objects, while a level here also means a lazily painted texture, and the anchor must stay put.
- **Quality tiers** add: LOD levels high 3 (2048 + detail near 16 radii, 1024 to 32), balanced 3 (1024 + detail near 12, 512 to 24), mobile 2 (512 near 12); ring segments 128/96/64; orbit segments 192·96 / 128·64 / 96·48; `labelHz` 30/30/15 (P3-07); `maxFps` 30 on mobile while motion runs: the loop switches to `never` and a rAF loop calls `advance()`, so even a camera follow is capped; the fps meter lifts the cap. Orbits run on their own clock (`motionSeconds`), since R3F's restarts on every loop change.
- **Domain labels** (P3-07, `apps/web/src/scene/labels/`; product text proposed in `docs/tasks/notes/P3-07-labels.md`). One DOM layer over the canvas, not a drei `Html` per body (each of those projects and writes its transform every frame). `label-model.ts` turns the world into labels with no three.js: a body hosting a project or task shows the work's name and its server-derived state (P2-15) as a pill, a planet or moon with only `movedTo` shows "moved to <body>" (an asteroid whose task moved away rejoins the belt, decision 63, and has no label), reserved (`star`, `meta.reserved`) and free bodies show nothing; a moon hosting a project is `sub-project` (the project has `parentProjectId`: "↳ part of <parent>") or `independent` (an "own project" badge, and `MoonMarkers.tsx` draws a teal camera-facing ring around it). `LabelLayer.tsx` (panel chunk) renders the content from the store, so a state change shows on the next React render; positions never go through React. `LabelProjector.tsx` (inside the canvas) runs in `useFrame` at most `labelHz` times a second (30 high/balanced, 15 mobile, never above the capped loop): it projects each label's anchor with one view-projection matrix, drops labels behind the camera, off screen (a label pushed above the viewport is hidden, except the selected and the camera-focused body's, which is clamped to the top edge), or whose line of sight passes through the Sun or a planet, de-clutters overlaps greedily by priority (selected > blocked on user > in progress > other open work > finished/moved; then nearer, then id), and writes a label's `transform` only when it moved more than 0.5 px and its `visibility` only when it changed. Content changes (a label is re-measured only when its title, state, moon role, parent, priority or selection changes), new labels, new or removed anchors (a label may register before its body's anchor) and resizes force the next pass; on the `demand` loop a skipped frame schedules one render for when the next pass is due. The layer and the projector meet in `registry.ts` (element, priority, size, last written position). The labels take no pointer events, so wheel, drag and click reach the canvas (OrbitControls, the click-vs-drag threshold); picking resolves label hits itself (`labelUnder`): a body the pointer's ray hits (its sphere or minimum target) wins, otherwise a shown label's rectangle under the pointer (`registry.labelAt`) means that label's body, over near misses — so a moon under its planet's label stays clickable. Stacking inside the scene area (custom properties on `.pnl-scene`, `scene.css`): canvas < labels < scene chrome (fps meter, breadcrumb, scale toggle) < hover label < popover. The layer is `aria-hidden`: labels are pointer-only extras, the panel carries the same information, the scene's keyboard path is `]` / `[` + `Enter`. Measured (headless Chrome, M3 Pro, 1440×900 at DPR 2, 41 labels): 60 fps on every tier with and without labels; ≈ 15 DOM writes/s at the overview, ≈ 90–150/s during camera flights (drei `Html` would have been ≈ 1 800/s).
- **Performance pass** (P3-08, numbers and the device-run steps in `docs/tasks/notes/P3-08-perf.md`). Decorations (orbit lines, the star field, task-asteroid halos, the highlight and own-project rings) live on their own layer; the belt's background rocks stay on layer 0 because they really hide task asteroids, and an outline is no longer hidden behind a halo (review) (`scene/layers.ts`, `DECOR_LAYER` = 1, enabled on the canvas camera at creation); the selection outline's depth pass, which re-renders the scene every frame a body is outlined, is wrapped to render without that layer, and the outline's selection is emptied while the selected body (its anchor sphere × 2.5, rings included) is outside the view frustum. Measured with 11 projects, 30 task asteroids and 42 labels (headless Chrome, ANGLE Metal, M3 Pro, 1440×900 at DPR 2): high with a Waykeeper selected 291 → 226 draw calls and 407k → 264k triangles at the system view, 82 → 57 with the selected body off screen; 60 fps (vsync) on high and balanced in every view, a steady 30 on capped mobile, no main-thread task over 50 ms, label passes invisible in the CPU profile. At high the GPU cost is pixels (DPR 2 × 4× MSAA HalfFloat composer, bloom, the cube shadow, since replaced by the cheaper eclipse shading of P3-10), not draw calls: DPR 1.5, fewer MSAA samples or no shadows are the levers if a Mac misses 60 (proposed, not applied). Each task asteroid costs about 2.5 draw calls; instancing them is the step if task counts pass about 100. Reaching the dev or preview server from a phone takes two per-run opt-ins: Vite's `--host` and `GALACTIC_ALLOWED_HOSTS=<the Mac's LAN IP>` (the server itself stays on `127.0.0.1`).
- **Renderer setup** (P3-01). The canvas's `gl` factory builds a `WebGLRenderer` explicitly (WebGL2: three 0.186 has no WebGL 1 path, so the up-front probe asks for `webgl2` only, and only when the scene is to be shown — list mode never probes); output is sRGB with ACES filmic tone mapping, which are R3F's own defaults and not set again. A `webglcontextlost` on the canvas is `preventDefault`ed and reported: the panel drops to list mode with a "Retry" that mounts a fresh canvas. The postprocessing composer forces `NoToneMapping` on the renderer, so with it ACES is the composer's last `ToneMapping` effect. The frame loop is `demand` until something moves; `?show_fps=true` on any panel URL shows a dev frame-rate meter at the scene's top-left (remembered per browser, `?show_fps=false` clears it) and switches the loop to `always`. The flag is taken when the app starts (`captureFpsFlag`, before the URL sync), and the panel's canonical URL rewrite (`navigate(…, { replace: true })`) keeps the query and hash: the scene mounts later, after the settings load (P3-11).
- **Bundle** (P3-01). three.js, R3F and postprocessing live in a lazily loaded `SolarScene` chunk (≈ 1 MB, 266 kB gzip); the panel's first chunk carries none of them, so list mode and a WebGL-less browser never download them. Versions: three 0.186 (postprocessing's peer range stops before 0.187), @react-three/fiber 9.8, @react-three/drei 10.7, @react-three/postprocessing 3.1, postprocessing 6.39.
- Satellites are animated actors with a small state machine (orbiting / scanning / gathering / transporting / building / flying-to-new-body / landing / landed), driven by the status store, so "work moved to Europa" and "task completed" are animations, not just label changes.
- **Satellites** (P4-01, `apps/web/src/scene/satellites/`; SPEC decision 68; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P4-01-satellite.md`). One satellite per **live** Waykeeper and Steward: a status record whose state is not in `ENDED_STATES` — one predicate, `showsSatellite(world, id, { keepFailed, keepLanded })` in `satellite-model.ts`, both options off by default; the scene's choice is `SCENE_SATELLITES` (P4-02 turned on `keepFailed`: a failed agent keeps its satellite in its failure colour, see the next bullet; P4-04 turned on `keepLanded`: completed work's newest Steward or Waykeeper, no longer live, stays as `landed` until the work is archived or reopened — see the body transitions bullet); its body is the work's body (the project's for a Waykeeper, the task's for a Steward, falling back to `agent.bodyId`), so a move (`body.moved` + the server's `task/project/agent.updated`) changes it and the satellite flies. The Harmonizer's HQ station is a list option (`harmonizer: true`, on `b-earth`; on in the scene since P4-02). The actor is a pure state machine (`machine.ts`): `orbiting | scanning | gathering | transporting | building | flying | landing | landed`, a transition table that `advance(actor, input, now)` never leaves, and every timed step stored as absolute times on the motion clock (`motionSeconds`), so a flight (3 s) or landing (1.6 s) started while no frame is drawn has ended by the next frame; the director applies a world change at once (`setSpecs`), not at the next drawn frame. A flight runs to completion (only a new destination retargets it); landing and archetypes wait for the arrival. Inputs: `bodyId` and `state` (P4-01: a `working` agent scans every 5–10 s for 2.4 s, seeded by the agent id), `archetype` (P4-03), `landed` (P4-04); colour and brightness come from `look.ts` (P4-02). Orbits (`orbits.ts`): per body, **sticky** slots — a satellite keeps its slot while it lives on that body; an arrival (new agent or flight) takes the lowest free slot, a Waykeeper slot 0 when it is free — at 1.8 + 0.15 × slot body radii with golden-ratio inclinations and nodes, phase from the agent id; Waykeepers turn in 28 s, Stewards 16 s (× √(r / 1.8)); an in-place orbit change (a role change) blends radius, inclination and node over 0.6 s. Satellites grow in over 0.4 s when their agent starts and shrink out over 0.4 s when it ends (kept in the registry meanwhile, `presence` on the frame). The director (`director.ts`, three-free) turns actors, anchors (read live through the registry) and the camera into frames: position (a flight arcs from the take-off point to the moving destination orbit), size (body radii, with a pixel floor and a ceiling), beam strength and sweep; frames are published in `satellites/registry.ts` (like the anchors) for P4-02–05 and for satellite picking (P4-08: hover card and click, in the Picking bullet above). Rendering (`Satellites.tsx`, `satellite-mesh.ts`): unlit vertex-coloured satellites and an additive open cone per beam (front faces, 12 rings with a cubic falloff from the apex: HDR only at the apex so it blooms only there, ≤ 0.155 luminance added over the body so bright planets are not washed out; ×0.35 without a composer; a beam cut short fades out over 0.3 s), all on `DECOR_LAYER`; a mesh pair per satellite up to the tier's `satellites.instanceAbove` (12 / 12 / 8), above it one `InstancedMesh` for bodies and one for beams (capacity in steps of 16; the beam mesh holds only the visible, scanning satellites' beams and is hidden when none scans). `satellites.maxAnimated` (16 / 12 / 6) caps scans and archetypes at once; flights and landings always animate and count toward it; satellites without an anchor take none; the focused body's satellites, then those already animating (no pre-emption), then Waykeepers, get the budget first. Bodies are placed at `useFrame` priority −1 (`scene/frame-order.ts`: `useOrbit`, the belt spin) so every reader of an anchor (satellites, labels, picking) sees this frame's position; the satellites' director runs at −0.5 and the camera rig at −0.25, so the readers at the default 0 (beacons, picking, labels) see this frame's satellites and camera whatever their mount order. Reduced motion: orbits frozen at their phase, no scans, instant flights, landings and fades (a running flight or landing ends at once), `invalidate()` on list, anchor and option changes. Measured with 10 Waykeepers + 30 Stewards (40 satellites, M3 Pro, ANGLE Metal, 1440×900 at DPR 2): +2 draw calls on every tier and view instanced (+49 at the system view per-mesh), 60 fps vsync at high and balanced, 30 capped on mobile, no-vsync headroom within run-to-run noise.
- **Satellite state look and HQ station** (P4-02, `satellites/look.ts`, `satellites/hq/`, `scene/legend/`; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P4-02-state-hq.md`). One table, `STATE_LOOK: Record<AgentState, …>` (every state, tested total), gives each agent state a tint (the role's own colour for `working` / `starting` / `finished`; amber `#ffb547` — the panel's beacon — only for `waiting_for_input`; grey idle, violet paused, orange stalled, red failed), a pulse (`breathe` 3.2 s de-synchronised per agent for working, `blink` 1.6 s synchronised for waiting, `stutter` for stalled, seeded `flicker` for failed, `steady` otherwise), a size multiplier (waiting ×1.35, stalled ×0.9, failed ×0.8), an orbit rate (paused 0 — holds still where it is —, failed 0.3 — drifts —, others 1) and a reduced-motion brightness (`still`: no pulse, size and level still tell the problem states apart — each of waiting, stalled, failed and paused differs from working by ≥ 0.2 in still brightness or ≥ 0.1 in size). The director reads it every frame (`satelliteTint`, `satelliteBrightness(spec, view, now, reducedMotion)`, `satelliteScale`, `beamTint`) and runs a per-satellite orbit clock at the state's rate, advanced up to the change at the old rate so a pause or resume never jumps (held while landing, which keeps the landing angle). Colour goes in the material colour (per-satellite meshes) or `instanceColor` (instanced): no extra draw call. A beam fading out after a state change takes the state's colour at the cyan's luminance (`MAX_BODY_LUMINANCE` still holds). The scene's list options are one constant, `SCENE_SATELLITES = { keepFailed: true, keepLanded: true, harmonizer: true }` (`satellite-model.ts`; `keepLanded` since P4-04): a failed agent keeps its satellite until a newer agent of its role (by `createdAt`) has the same task or project, live or not (`isSuperseded`: the old failure never comes back when the replacement ends), or until its work is over — task completed, archived or moved; project closed, archived, moved or cancelled (`isWorkOver`). The Harmonizer's HQ station is its ordinary satellite entry (same actor, frame, registry, picking: hovering shows its card, clicking selects the Harmonizer) on `b-earth`, the largest look (0.42 R, ≥ 7 px), drawn outside the instancing by `hq/HqStation.tsx` with a procedural model (`hq-mesh.ts`: hub, top module, dish toward Earth, truss and two wings, beacon; a segmented habitat ring with spokes as a second mesh) whose ring turns at 0.22 rad/s idle and 1.6 rad/s while it thinks (`working`), integrated so speed changes never jump, still under reduced motion; written in the satellites' own frame loop right after the director (no lag), ≤ 3 draw calls (core, ring, beam while scanning). The legend (`scene/legend/Legend.tsx`, panel chunk, no three.js) sits top-right in the scene chrome layer, draws the same table as CSS swatches with matching animations (none under reduced motion), and collapses to a "Legend" button; open/closed is remembered per browser (`localStorage['galactic.scene.legend.open']`), default open above the 1100 px stacked breakpoint, closed below. Measured (40 satellites + HQ, M3 Pro, ANGLE Metal, 1440×900 DPR 2): +4–5 draw calls over no agents at high and mobile in both views, 60 fps vsync at high, 30 capped on mobile; mixed states cost the same as all working.
- **Steward activity archetypes** (P4-03, `apps/web/src/scene/satellites/archetype.ts` and `satellites/activity/`; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P4-03-archetypes.md`). A working Steward's archetype is derived from `StatusRecord.currentActivity` — the structured part first (the tool name of `describeTool`'s `"<Tool>: <detail>"`, then the shell command of `Bash: …`, each part of a compound command alone with the strongest winning; an MCP tool by its name after the last `__`), then keywords in free text (progress summaries; a future summariser's sentences go through the same `TEXT_RULES`): reading and searching → `gathering`; edits, commits, pushes, PRs, reports → `transporting`; builds, tests, linters, installs, "editing N files" → `building`; anything unclear (TodoWrite, a sub-agent, a question) → none (plain orbit and scan). Waykeepers have no archetype. `deriveArchetype` is the stateless hook in `satelliteList`; `ArchetypeHold` (via `useHeldArchetypes` in `Satellites.tsx`, on the wall clock with a timer for due switches) adds hysteresis — a new archetype must stay the latest 0.4 s, the shown one stays at least 1 s (so a steady change always shows within 1 s), dropping to none waits 1.5 s, a non-working agent drops it at once — and escalates edits to 3 distinct files within 90 s to `building`. The visuals are one three-free layer (`activity-model.ts`) writing two instance lists (solid: robots, platform struts, shuttle hulls; glow: drones, sparks, the shuttle's trail) that `Activities.tsx` copies into two instanced meshes on `DECOR_LAYER`: two draw calls whatever the count, none when nothing is drawn; capacity is fixed by the tier's `maxAnimated` (only satellites in the animation budget draw an archetype). Gathering: drones rise from the surface under the satellite to it. Transporting: a shuttle runs between the Steward and its Waykeeper's satellite when both are on the same body, otherwise down to the surface and back. Building: robots at a fixed work site on a body with a surface (`footingOf`: rocky, Earth-like and cloudy planets, every moon and asteroid); an orbital platform assembled strut by strut under the satellite on gas and ice giants. Strength follows the actor's `view.blend`, the outgoing archetype shrinks out meanwhile, and losing the budget fades it over 0.4 s; reduced motion freezes it (clock at 0) and shows it at once; the mobile tier draws fewer pieces, no sparks or trail (`ACTIVITY_QUALITY`). The satellites' director now runs at `useFrame` priority `PLACE_SATELLITES` (−0.5, `frame-order.ts`), after the bodies and before every reader of the satellite registry. The raw activity text is untouched (corner case 32).
- **Body transitions: fly, land, colonise** (P4-04, `scene/satellites/`, `scene/colonies/`; SPEC §3.4, §4.1, decisions 4, 9, 15, 63, 66, 68; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P4-04-transitions.md`). **Flights**: nothing new on the event side — a move or promotion arrives as the server's `task.updated` / `project.updated` (`bodyId`, and `projectId` for a task joining a project), `body.moved` (the old body's marker) and `agent.updated`; the satellite's work body (`workBody`) changes, `setSpecs` starts the 3 s flight at the event, for every satellite of that work (a task's Steward; a project's Waykeeper and the Stewards of its tasks). The flight's origin is where the satellite was drawn on the last frame when that is at most `STALE_ORIGIN` (2 s) old — this already covers an old body that stops being drawn in the same render that starts the flight (a standalone task's asteroid folds into the belt, decision 63) — else its orbit round the old body's live anchor (the satellite was hidden on the last frame, or its last drawn position is stale after a long gap without frames, such as a hidden tab), else a **ghost** — where it was last drawn on the old body (`Entry.lastDrawn`), which only runs after a frame where the satellite itself was hidden and when the old body is not drawn either; a frame where the destination is not drawn keeps the flight's origin. A **landed** satellite whose body moves (a promotion carrying a project's completed tasks) is re-created landed on the new body — no flight, no second landing; it grows in there over 0.4 s (`machine.ts`'s `landed → landed` edge). **Landing**: `SCENE_SATELLITES.keepLanded` is on; `hasLanded`: a Steward whose task is `completed`, or a Waykeeper whose project is `completed`, that is not live (finished, or stopped by the close — `control.close` stops the work's live agents — or even failed), is the newest of its role on that work that ran (`isSuperseded`, through `newestAgents`: an index by (role, work) built once per `satelliteList`, so the list is linear in the agents; a newer agent that never ran hides nothing), and ran itself (a status record or a `sessionRef`). At most `LANDED_PER_BODY` (6) landed satellites per body are drawn, the newest agents'; the others are not (the colonised lights and the labels still tell the story). It needs no status record (the server drops ended records after 24 h, retention is longer, decision 4); its spec takes the `finished` look whatever the record says, no activity, no archetype. It lands (1.6 s, always animated) from the orbit it had, then sits at 1.03 R, dim, no beam, no animation budget, until the work is archived (`task/project.state_changed → archived` → not completed → it shrinks out over 0.4 s) or reopened. Landed satellites hold no orbit slot (`orbitSlots` runs over the live ones; one that appears already landed gets a slot among its body's landed satellites, which spreads them over the surface), so a project body collecting finished Stewards never pushes its live satellites outwards. The hover card says "work completed · landed · <body>". Picking (`pickScene`) leaves landed satellites out of the candidates and the "+n" count — a click on a completed project's planet selects its Waykeeper (decision 65), never a list of its landed Stewards — except a direct hit at their drawn size (no `MIN_TARGET_PX` floor) while their body is at least `CLEAR_TARGET_PX` on screen. **Colonised**: `colonies/colony-model.ts` — a body whose **host** is `completed` (a project's planet or moon, a standalone task's asteroid); a completed project task lands its Steward on the project's body without colonising it until the project completes; archival (`body.freed`), a reopen or a move clears it. `Colonies.tsx` draws 5 clusters × 4 warm-white lights (`#ffe6b8`, not the waiting amber) on the surface, seeded by the body id: **one** `Points` for every colony (2.5 px, not attenuated, depth-tested so the far side hides, `DECOR_LAYER`), its world positions rewritten at `READ_BODIES` from each anchor (`writeColony`; an asteroid's through its rock's own matrix, so the lights follow its squash and rotation, at 1.1 of its local radius), with a per-light opacity by the Sun's angle (full on the night side, 0.35 on the lit side: dimmed, not hidden); still (nothing to do under reduced motion), a frame asked only when the list or the anchors change. **Markers**: unchanged — a planet's or moon's label says "moved to <body>" until `body.freed` (decision 66), an asteroid folds into the belt (decision 63). Cost: +1 draw call for all colonised bodies together (20 vertices each, rewritten per drawn frame); landed satellites (≤ 6 per body) go through the existing per-mesh / instanced paths.
- **Question beacons** (P4-05, P4-13, `apps/web/src/scene/beacons/`; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P4-05-beacons.md` and `P4-13-beacons-per-question.md`). `beaconList(world)` (`beacon-model.ts`, no three.js): **one beacon per question** that is `open` and held by the user (`holder.type === 'user'`, the inbox's "Waiting for you"), keyed by the question id; the body is the asking agent's work body (Steward → its task's body, Waykeeper → its project's body, Harmonizer → `headquartersBody`, i.e. Earth; then the agent's `bodyId`, then `context.origin`); its kind is the question's. Several on one body form a row (`slot` of `slots`, in the inbox order: permissions, then clarifications, oldest first), centred above the body, 40 px apart (`BEACON.spacingPx`, more than the 36 px click target), their pillars fanning out from the body's top; at most `MAX_BEACONS_PER_BODY` (6) per body, the last carrying a "+n" badge for the rest. Rendered as a camera-facing group scaled to world units per pixel at the body's depth, so the parts are laid out in screen pixels: an additive amber pillar from the body's top, the glyph 64 px above it (a padlock diamond or a "?" disc, `#ffb547`, canvas-drawn textures cached per kind and badge), a pulsing halo (steady under reduced motion); clamped inside the viewport's top when the body is big. No depth test, `renderOrder` 20–22, `DECOR_LAYER`, 3 draw calls per beacon on screen: a beacon whose glyph (with its widest halo) is outside the viewport is hidden and not clickable (`glyphInView`), and all beacons share one plane and one ring geometry (`BeaconShapes`). Instancing (3 draw calls in all, a glyph atlas with a per-instance UV offset) was weighed and left out: one beacon per user-held question is a handful at most, not worth a custom shader and the loss of per-beacon render order. The list never reads the status records (headquarters' body comes from a string selector, `beaconHq`) and is kept by reference while it draws the same (`sameBeacons`), so status flushes neither rebuild, republish nor re-render it; rebuilt frames carry each surviving beacon's placement over (the hover does not flicker). A beacon is laid out in this frame's camera pixels: the camera rig runs at `PLACE_CAMERA` (−0.25), before the beacons' default priority, whatever the mount order. Picking tests `beaconUnder(ray)` (glyph centre within 18 px on screen, from `beacons/registry.ts`; one frame per question) before satellites, bodies and labels; hover → `{ kind: 'beacon', questionId, bodyId }`, that question's card; click → `activateBeacon(questionId)`: selects the asking agent (`satelliteSelection`: `waykeeper:<id>` / `steward:<id>` / `harmonizer`, through `selectFromScene` so the panel → scene sync does not fly twice), flies the camera to the beacon's body (`flyTo`) and `revealQuestion` (`panel/inbox-reveal.ts`: scroll the card `[data-question-id]` inside `#pnl-inbox` into view, focus it with `tabindex=-1`, amber ring 1.6 s; falls back to revealing the inbox). An asker that is not loaded leaves the selection alone; a question that no longer waits does nothing.
- **Timeline replay** (P4-07, `apps/web/src/replay/`; notes and the proposed SPEC decision in `docs/tasks/notes/P4-07-scrubber.md`). **One switch**: `AppState.replayWorld` holds the replayed world while a replay is open, and `sceneWorld(s) = s.replayWorld ?? s.world` is how every scene component reads its rows (a source test forbids `s.world` / `getState().world` under `scene/`); the panel keeps reading `world`, and live events keep folding into it. **The fold** (`replay-model.ts`, no three.js): the base is the client's bodies with no host and no marker and no work (`replayBase`); the project's events (`GET /api/history/events`, §6.3) fold with the page's own `applyEvent` plus `foldReplayStatus`, a replay of the log's `agent.*` events into status records (state, `describeTool` activity — the protocol's, shared with the status store — and the open question; no stall timer, no output, no cap usage). It is deliberately not the status projection: live records stay server-owned (§4); a replayed agent without `agent.spawned` has no record and no satellite. `createReplayTimeline` keeps a world every 64 events, so any seek folds at most 64 (50 000 events: 21 ms to build, ~10 µs per random seek). A conformance test (`replay-conformance.test.ts`) runs real commands and agents and checks, at every commit, that the replay holds exactly the server's rows for the project's entities and nothing of other projects, and that the final replayed agent states are the status store's. **The bar** (`ReplayBar.tsx`, plain React in the panel chunk) docks between the scene and the conversation (a flex item that never makes the page scroll); `useReplayStore` reads every page until `hasMore` is false (no page cap; a progress line meanwhile; the first page's `membership` token goes back with each page; a non-advancing `seq` is an error), owns the time, ticks (assignments, moves, completions, questions), stepping between ticks and playback (`requestAnimationFrame` × speed; with reduced motion a 1 s interval, so only the replayed state changes). The replay is the history as it was when opened; a snapshot of another log closes it. Escape exits from anywhere on the page while a replay is open, after any handler that took it (`defaultPrevented`), a text field, focus in a dialog / menu, or an open page-wide layer marked `data-escape-layer`. Opening or exiting a replay swaps the whole world, so the satellite director is told to `snap` (no flight, no landing for a satellite whose body differs between the two worlds); scrubbing within a replay still flies.
- **Event toasts and the scene harness** (P4-06, `apps/web/src/scene/toasts/`, `apps/web/src/console/SceneHarness.tsx`, `apps/server/src/runtime/scene-harness.ts`; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P4-06-harness-toasts.md`). **Toasts**: `toastOf(envelope, world)` (`toast-model.ts`, no three.js) — `task/project.state_changed → completed` flares the work's body (warm white `#ffe6b8`, the colonised lights' colour), a question that starts waiting for the user (`question.opened`, or escalated / reassigned to the user, as `waitsForUser`) pings its beacon's body (`questionBody`, amber). The feed subscribes to the store's event log (newest first, empty after a snapshot) from the last `seq` it saw, so a snapshot never toasts, and drops events whose `at` is more than 5 s old on the server's clock (`Date.now() + serverOffsetMs`, the offset taken from the snapshot's / `caught_up`'s `serverTime`, so a skewed client clock does not decide; a reconnect's catch-up is history); while a timeline replay is open (`replayWorld`) live events do not flare. One toast per body and kind at a time (a burst restarts it), at most `TOAST.max` (4) at once, the oldest dropped. Drawn as a fixed pool of 4 camera-facing additive rings (one shared `RingGeometry`, a material each, no depth write, `DECOR_LAYER`, `READ_BODIES`): 1.15 → 2.4 body radii over 1.6 s with a fast rise and a quadratic fade, never under 22 px on screen; invisible rings cost no draw call. Reduced motion: a still ring (1.5 R, dimmer) for 1.2 s, the on-demand loop asked for a frame at the start and once after the end. **Harness**: development-only routes `/dev/harness/*` (§8) drive the scene through the real event log — fake agents (`meta.harness`, runtime `fake`, no session, no process) made as the runtime makes rows (`checkPlacement`, `createAgent`, `recordSpawned`); states and activities as the runtime's agent events through `Commands.recordAgentEvent` (an ended agent is re-`spawned` first; `waiting_for_input` asks a permission question; moving on withdraws a fake agent's open questions); moves, closes, questions and answers through `Commands` (a close is `Commands.closeTask` / `closeProject`, never `control.close`). **The harness only ever touches its own rows**: an agent, task, project or question (by its origin agent) without `meta.harness` is refused (409 `not_harness`, nothing written) — so a real agent is never given a fake state (which `recover()` would resume from its real session), a real question never answered, real work never moved, closed or given a fake agent. A harness project's Waykeeper and its asteroid Stewards answer to a dormant harness Harmonizer row (never spawned, `createdAt` at the epoch so it is never "the newest Harmonizer"; the panel's `currentHarmonizer` skips it), a harness project task's Steward to the project's harness Waykeeper, and `concernedSupervisors` concerns no one for a harness target — so no real brain wakes on harness events. The status store's stall threshold takes the agent id; the composition makes it infinite for fake agents (they stall only when told), and recovery ends a fake agent `stopped` instead of reopening it. Bodies and replies are `packages/protocol/src/dev.ts`.

- **Planet detail view** (P6-01, `apps/web/src/scene/detail/`; notes and the proposed SPEC decision in `docs/tasks/notes/P6-01-detail-scene.md`). **A separate sub-scene, not the system meshes at a higher LOD**: `DetailScene` (one component per kind — `RockyPlanetDetail`, `GiantPlanetDetail`, `MoonDetail`, `AsteroidDetail` — plus the `DETAIL_PARTS` slots of `parts.ts` for P6-03/04/05) is mounted while a body is shown in detail and unmounted (every geometry, material and texture disposed) when it is left. It draws in the body's own frame — a group that copies the body's anchor matrix every frame at `PLACE_DETAIL` (−0.75, after the bodies move) — at the anchor's size, from the same layout the system uses (`resolveDetailBody` over `buildRenderList` / `taskAsteroids`); a planet's moons on their orbits with the system's clock, eclipse shading among them (a moon's view takes its planet's and siblings' anchors as casters), the nearest LOD level's spheres and maps (cache-shared with the system body). So **the anchors stay the system's**: camera, picking, labels, satellites, beacons and colonies keep working around the big body unchanged, and the swap is seamless. The system hides only its own meshes of the shown body (`shown`; a planet's tilt group with its moons, a moon's mesh, an asteroid's rock and halo; the body's own orbit line fades out with the mix instead of popping); the sub-scene's groups register as **stand-ins** (`stand-ins.ts`) so the selection outline collects their meshes. The anchor registry now stacks registrations of one id (newest wins, removing it restores the one below; covered by `mode.test.ts`), reserved for a later P6-0x view that draws a body elsewhere; P6-01 registers none. **Mode** (`mode.ts`, zustand, three-free, panel chunk): `system | detail{bodyId}`, not in the URL (a way of looking, like the camera focus). Enter by zoom — focused on a planet, moon or asteroid, not flying, closer than `ENTER_RADII` (3 **framed radii**: the anchor's visual radius, never below the camera's `MIN_FRAMED_RADIUS` 0.05 — `framedRadius` in `flight.ts`, the one unit of the camera's framing, the detail flight's `radii`, the watcher's distance (`radiiFrom`) and the backdrop, so true scale's tiny moons stay in once the flight lands) —, by the breadcrumb's "Detail view" or by a second `Enter` that would change nothing (P5-01's Enter still selects first); an explicit enter flies to `DETAIL_FRAME_RADII` (2.85 since P6-07 — the disc and its glow, `DETAIL_DISC_REACH` 1.08 radii, fit the 45° vertical field at any aspect from 1:1 up, still inside `ENTER_RADII`; 2.4 overfilled a square canvas; the system framing is ≈ 8.5). Leave past `EXIT_RADII` (4.5, hysteresis; never during a flight), by `Escape` (after the popover, before the camera's body: popover → detail → body → selection → system), by the body's crumb (back to its system framing), or when the camera is sent elsewhere (`Home`, "Solar system", `]`/`[`, a pick of another body, a panel selection elsewhere: `bindCameraToDetail`); an explicit exit disarms zoom entry until the camera has been past `EXIT_RADII`. Any flight to the shown body keeps the detail framing (`setFocusFraming` in the camera store; `CameraRequest.radii`), so a click on it, its agent's selection or the scale toggle never fly out; the zoom limits hold the body when the user pans off it, and `CameraDirector.step(…, holdId)` keeps the camera ≥ `BODY_KEEP_OUT` (1.3) radii from the held or followed body's centre (OrbitControls' `minDistance` only bounds the distance to the target, which a pan moves). **Transition**: the camera flight plus a cross-fade (`detailMix`, smoothstep, 0.9 s in / 0.7 s out, reversible mid-way; instant under reduced motion) over a **backdrop** (`backdrop.ts`): a dark sphere about the body (≥ 40 radii, beyond the view's bodies seen from `EXIT_RADII`) that is blended over the system while it fades (transparent, z-sorted) and, once settled (`covered`: the fade has ended **and** the camera is in place — not flying, within `EXIT_RADII`, `settleDue` — so an explicit enter mid-flight, whose flight outlasts the fade, or reduced motion's instant switch never covers before the camera arrives), an opaque copy drawn first (`renderOrder` −1000) that writes depth, so what lies beyond it is hidden by depth; the system then skips only what is **wholly outside** the dome (`dome.ts`: planets with their systems, task asteroids, Sun-centred orbit lines, the belt as a shell; tested per frame against the dome the backdrop publishes, so a body added or moved while settled is right), while anything that may be inside it — belt rocks and other task asteroids around an asteroid's view, a moon's planet, an orbit line through it — stays drawn and nothing pops when the fade ends; the system's Sun and sky are not drawn; **system decorations that may be inside the dome but do not belong to the view fade out with the cross-fade instead** (P6-07: the backdrop is ≥ 40 framed radii, which in the readable layout reaches the neighbours and the belt — Earth's is 68 units about a planet 19 units from the belt — so they were drawn over the dome in the close-up): every Sun-centred orbit line (`orbitLineFactor(own, mix, aboutSun)`), the hover/selection highlight ring around the shown body (`ownRingFactor`; it was a light-blue band at ≈ 1.15 R), and in a planet's or moon's view the belt's rocks and the other task asteroids (`beltFactor` via `useBeltFade`: their material colour, emissive and halo opacity scaled to black, then `material.visible = false`; an asteroid's view keeps them); the neighbours' planet meshes inside the dome are still drawn (small discs, not seen in the checks; a follow-up if they show); the sky's own stars (same seed, count and camera-centred placement as the system's, on a shell inside it, additive at the fade's opacity, so no star changes across the fade; first of the transparents once settled so rings and beams draw over them) and the Sun in its real direction at its system angular size. ≈ 4 draw calls; on `DECOR_LAYER`. **Scope** (`detailScope`: a planet and its moons; a moon, its planet and siblings; an asteroid alone): only these are pick targets, labelled, beaconed, ringed or outlined while the view is on (the beacons and rings draw over everything, the outline's x-ray would draw through the backdrop). The scope follows the mode, not the fade: out-of-scope bodies stop being clickable when the fade in starts and become clickable again when the fade out starts. **Texture budget:** the sub-scene puts the shown body and every moon of its system at the nearest LOD level (Saturn: the planet, its rings and six moons' near maps at once); on low tiers this is the largest near-texture working set of the scene — a risk to watch, to be capped per tier (e.g. the moons one level coarser) if the low tier's memory proves tight. Measured (headless Chrome, ANGLE Metal, M3 Pro, 1440×900 DPR 2, high): every planet, two moons and a task asteroid enter and leave at 60 fps (p95 16.8 ms); one 33–100 ms frame at the first entries (the backdrop's programs compiling); settled detail views cost fewer draw calls than the system view they replace (e.g. Jupiter 38 → 29).
- **Earth as headquarters in the detail view** (P6-05, `apps/web/src/scene/detail/earth-hq/`; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P6-05-earth-hq.md`). The `'earth-hq'` entry of `DETAIL_PARTS` (rocky kind, `accepts` only `b-earth`) draws a detailed procedural HQ station (spine, habitation modules, top module, dish, docking ring and ports, radiators, four solar arrays, an eight-segment habitat ring, red/green navigation lights, a white strobe, a cyan "thinking" glow billboard; the mobile tier a simpler model without the glow). **It is the Harmonizer's satellite frame drawn with another model**: while mounted it claims the HQ (`claimHqStation()` in `satellites/hq/hq-model.ts`), so `writeHq` hides the system station's core and ring (never two HQs) but keeps drawing its beam, and the frame stays published — picking, the hover card and the click are the system HQ's unchanged. Each frame (`READ_BODIES`, after the satellites' loop) `writeEarthHq` places it at `inverse(anchor) × bodyMatrix(frame)` in Earth's frame, tints hull and ring with `bodyColor(frame)` (the state look: amber blink while waiting) and applies `stationLook(state, reducedMotion)`: ring spin `hqRingSpeed` (fast thinking, slow idle/waiting, 0 halted), lights blink (steady under reduced motion and when paused, off when failed), glow breathing while thinking (steady under reduced motion). With no Harmonizer there is no frame: the station rests dark at `restingPose` (innermost shell), unlit and still, and is not a pick target (clicking it hits Earth or space, both of which land on the Harmonizer). Lights and glow fade in with the cross-fade. ≤ 5 draw calls. **Clicking the HQ** (any view) runs `activateSatellite` → select the Harmonizer, then `revealChat()` (`panel/dock-state.ts`): `requestDockOpen()` restores the mounted dock (a listener set that `useDockPrefs` registers; with none mounted the stored preference is set to open) and the dock shows Chat through a **transient override** (`useDockView.showTransient`: not stored, so the device's remembered Chat/Status choice of decision 80 is untouched; it ends when the selection changes — a watcher on the app store's selection — or the user picks a view with `setView`). **Viewing slot** (P6-06, SPEC decision 89, `earth-hq/hq-slot.ts`, three-free; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P6-06-07-detail-view.md`): at the detail framing the station's 1.8-radii orbit kept it on screen ≈ 9 % of the time in a square canvas and ≈ 57 % in a 2.1:1 one, so while the part is mounted it installs `overrideHqPose` (`satellites/hq/hq-model.ts`; `Satellites.tsx` calls `applyHqPose(frames, camera)` right after `director.update`, before `writeHq` and every reader), which moves the **Harmonizer's frame itself** — position and `size` — into a place fixed in the view: on the line of sight through NDC (0.7, 0.55), at the depth of Earth's centre if that is ≥ `clearance` (1.35 R) from it, else where that line first comes within 1.35 R (in front of the upper-right limb), drawn at the size it would have at Earth's depth (`hqSlot`'s factor ≤ 1); in a canvas so wide (≈ 2:1 and wider) that the point at Earth's depth would be more than `maxReach` (1.7 R) from Earth's centre, the slot's x is pulled in (its height kept) so the station (half-span 0.21 R) stays inside the Moon's orbit (2.21 R, radius 0.26 R); no slot when that point is < 0.9 R in front of the camera (the orbit is kept). `HqSlotter` blends orbit → slot by the cross-fade's mix and follows the slot with a 0.35 s lag (snaps under reduced motion), both moves along `swing` (great-circle turn plus linear height about Earth's centre, so never through the globe), and stops following while the view fades out. Picking, the hover card and ring, the click and the beam's apex follow the frame; the dark resting station takes the same slot (`restInSlot`). The camera is the previous frame's (the director runs before the camera rig); the lag hides it.
- **Cutaway layers** (P6-03, `apps/web/src/scene/detail/cutaway/`; layer sources, proposed SPEC text and the browser check in `docs/tasks/notes/P6-03-cutaway.md`). A detail part (`'cutaway'` in `DETAIL_PARTS`, kinds rocky/giant/moon, `accepts` only bodies with layer data). **Data** (`layers.ts`, no three.js): a table per catalogue body with a known interior, keyed by name and kind — Mercury, Venus, Earth, Mars, the Moon, Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus, Neptune — each layer with its outer radius as a fraction of the mean radius (surface first, strictly decreasing, bands summing to 1), an sRGB colour and a short note; thin layers are drawn at least `MIN_BAND` (4.5 %) thick (`displayRadii`), the rest to scale. **Visual** (`Cutaway.tsx`): a 90° wedge through both poles, centred 20° off the camera's azimuth about the body's spin axis (it follows the camera), is removed by two world-space clipping planes with `clipIntersection` on the body's own sphere materials — the meshes under the group whose `userData.bodyId` is the body's (a moon's subtree skipped) whose geometry is a `SphereGeometry` or that set `userData.cutaway = true` (`false` opts out; rings and other geometry are never cut) — so the outer surface keeps its maps and eclipse shading; the renderer's `localClippingEnabled` is on while the part is mounted. The two cut faces are half discs of vertex-coloured, unlit triangles (2 draw calls), turned to the planes' azimuths. Materials are re-scanned each frame (a body's material is swapped when its maps arrive; a swapped-out one gets its planes back). The planes stay on the materials while the part is mounted and a closed cut parks them where they clip nothing (`clipping.ts`: normal up, constant 10⁹), so opening, closing and the fade never change a program's clipping parameters (no recompile); they are removed when the view is left. The wedge opens over 0.6 s (instant with reduced motion) and only once the cross-fade is past 0.6 (`mixGate`), closing with it on the way out. **Labels**: DOM pills (swatch + name) over the canvas in the domain labels' z slot (`CutawayLabels`, `aria-hidden`), positioned per frame by the part through `label-registry.ts` at the middle of each band on the cut face drawn further right, spread vertically to 19 px apart. **Toggle**: "Cutaway" after the breadcrumb's trail in a detail view of a body with layer data (`CutawayToggle`, `aria-pressed`, in the tab order, `aria-describedby` a visually hidden sentence listing the layers); one session-wide switch in `sessionStorage` (`store.ts`). Only the DOM side (toggle, labels, layer table, store) is in the panel's chunk; three.js stays in the scene's.
- **High-detail visuals** (P6-02, `apps/web/src/scene/detail/visuals/`, with the painting shared by every view — `features.ts`, `rings.ts`, `paint-jobs.ts` — in `scene/bodies/`, so the system layer does not import the detail layer; perf table, proposed SPEC text and the browser check in `docs/tasks/notes/P6-02-visuals.md`). **Per-body features** (`features.ts`, no three.js; data in `BodyLook.features`, `appearance.ts`): band frequency/contrast for the giants (Jupiter bold, Saturn pale, Uranus barely), storms that swirl the bands and tint their core (Jupiter's Great Red Spot, Neptune's dark spot), maria (the Moon, Mars, Ganymede, Iapetus, Io, Triton), craters from cells on the sphere in up to three sizes (only sizes ≥ ~1.5 texels at the map's width are painted), lineae (Europa, Enceladus, Miranda, Triton) and Venus's chevrons — painted by the one surface painter at **every** size, so the system view shows them too and the detail level only adds resolution and relief (cache keys include the features). Rings have a measured profile (`rings.ts`, `RingLook.bands`: Saturn's C/B/A rings, Cassini division, Encke gap; Uranus's nine ringlets) used by the system's 256-texel strip and the detail view's wider one. **Detail level** (`budget.ts`, `detail-textures.ts`): the body shown in detail (only it: orbiting moons keep their nearest LOD level) gets, beyond the LOD table, a colour map of `DETAIL_TIERS[tier].planetTexture`/`moonTexture` (high 4096/2048, balanced 2048/1024, mobile 1024/512), a tangent-space **normal map** derived in the paint worker from the same unquantised relief (`paintSurfaceLayers(…, heights)`; central differences per radian, east-west scaled by cos latitude; Earth's relief, flat at sea, box-blurred first so the land mask's steps are not embossed) on high and balanced, and Earth's roughness map (glossy oceans); giants and Venus get no normal map. Painted in the worker (`runPaintJob` in `paint-jobs.ts` now dispatches surface / detail / clouds jobs; 2–8.5 s for a 4096 level, the nearest LOD level shows meanwhile; the painter's pending jobs are ordered by `paintPriority` — near levels and clouds before detail levels, FIFO within a priority, a running job never preempted — so the clouds and an orbiting moon's near maps do not wait behind a queued 4096 level), cached in `detailTextures` (reference-counted, idle levels kept within `budgetMB` 128/32/8 MB — high holds Earth's level and clouds, ≈ 118 MB, so re-entering does not repaint — all idle ones dropped on a tier change and when the canvas goes: the budget hook lives in `DetailLayer`, mounted for the canvas's lifetime, not in the view that unmounts on every exit; the budget caps live + idle ready bytes, so the detail cache holds at most max(budget, the live view) — ≤ 128 MB on high, ≈ 224 MB with the near cache's 96 MB), and each texture **frees its CPU copy after upload** (`onUpdate`), so a level costs its GPU size: high Earth 4096 × 2048 × (4 + 4 + 2 B) × 4/3 ≈ 112 MB. No worker → no detail level. The detail material (`DetailSurfaceMaterial`) is a standard material keyed on its maps, with the eclipse patch and shared program cache key, and accepts the cutaway's clipping planes. **Atmosphere** (`atmosphere-shader.ts`, the detail view's only hand-written shader, one program for all bodies): an additive shell `thickness` above the surface; the glow is the view ray's path through the shell outside the body (analytic, object space), normalised and raised to `power` — a soft limb ring with no hard edge — and only on the day side; colour/intensity per body (`looks.ts`: Earth blue, Venus yellow, Mars thin pink, the giants faint, Titan orange); `clipping: true` so the cutaway cuts it; no eclipse; fades in with the cross-fade; every tier. **Clouds** (Earth): a standard-material shell at 1.01 R with a procedural RG alpha map (domain-warped fbm, latitude climate: ITCZ and storm tracks cloudy, subtropics clear) painted in the worker at 2048/1024/512 per tier, turning with the surface plus a 4 % drift (still under reduced motion), eclipse-shaded with the planet's binding (in a planet's detail view its moons cast no shadow on it — surface and clouds — `detailEclipseReceivers`, P6-07: the readable layout puts the Moon 2.2 Earth radii out, so its shadow was a hard disc over a third of the globe; the planet still shades its moons and rings), cut by the cutaway, fading with the mix and, as the map may arrive after the cross-fade, also from its arrival over 1.2 s (`cloudFadeIn`, at once under reduced motion). **Moons** in a planet's view get planetshine: the material's emissive set per frame to the planet's mean colour × the lit fraction of the planet facing the moon × (R/d)², capped at 0.12 (no new light, so nothing recompiles) — a faint **uniform** glow over the whole moon, not a night-side-only light (emissive has no direction; on the day side the Sun swamps it). Measured (headless Chrome, ANGLE Metal, M3 Pro, 1440×900 DPR 2, dev build): every planet and three moons settle at 60 fps (p95 16.8 ms) on high and mobile, 23–37 draw calls on high; entering costs one 33–67 ms frame (programs compiling).
- **Project overlay in the detail view** (P6-04, SPEC decision 88, `apps/web/src/scene/detail/overlay/`; notes in `docs/tasks/notes/P6-04-overlay.md`). A detail part (`'project-overlay'`, planets and moons). **Markers:** a body's project tasks (`task.bodyId`, not archived or moved) are pins on a rocky planet's or moon's surface and buoys on three low rings (1.22–1.54 anchor radii, under the satellites' 1.8) around a gas or ice giant. **Placement is deterministic:** slot = `hash(task id) mod slots` with linear probing, tasks taken oldest first (`createdAt`, id), so a task's slot depends only on it and older tasks — stable across sessions, never moved by a newer task (`assignSlots`); surface slots are a 48-point Fibonacci lattice over ±55° latitude, riding the planet's tilt and spin like its terrain (`surfacePoint` reproduces the per-kind components' nesting); per-tier caps per body (48 / 36 / 24, open work before the newest completed). Five instanced solids (pins on the shown body, pins on sub-project moons, buoys, robots, drones) and two instanced additive glows (cut and uncut: the heads in the state colour with the satellites' pulses, still under reduced motion, and the stand-ins' lights), each hidden when empty; their capacities come from the marker list (`overlayCapacity`, in steps of 8, so a planet with several sub-project moons never overflows a list), and a marker whose instances would not fit is neither drawn nor pickable. Markers are kept between 7 and 44 px on screen and grow in with the cross-fade. **Picking:** `task-registry.ts` (like the beacons' registry; published and withdrawn in one layout effect, the withdrawal only of the overlay's own set, so when `DetailScene` swaps bodies in one commit the old overlay never empties the new one's registry): a marker under the pointer, not behind a body (its own body tested at its sphere radius), wins over labels and bodies, and over satellites unless one is nearer the pointer; hover shows the task's name and state (`TaskHover`), a click selects its Steward with the panel's `primaryAgent` rule (`activateTask`) or shows an info card when no Steward has started; the panel's Steward selection rings its marker. **Stewards:** `satellites/placement.ts` — a view registers a placement override (world position, `k`, span `scale`); the director applies it after placing an orbiting satellite (never a flight, landing or landed one), moving the frame by `k` (the cross-fade, eased) and setting `frame.claimed` from `k` ≥ 0.5, when the satellite renderer draws neither satellite nor beam and the overlay draws a tracked robot (surface) or a drone (giant) at the frame. Picking, the hover card and ring and the P4-03 activities read the frame, so they follow the stand-in. The robot is scaled by the frame's `presence` (an ended Steward's robot shrinks away). An override can be `hidden`: the overlay sets it for a robot whose place is on the cutaway's removed wedge, and the director then clears `frame.visible` once claimed, so the robot is neither picked nor ringed and its activities stop. Order: `PLACE_OVERLAY` (−0.6, markers and overrides, between `PLACE_DETAIL` and `PLACE_SATELLITES`), stand-ins and rings at `READ_BODIES`. **Moons:** in a planet's view a sub-project moon gets a faint tether from the planet and its tasks on its surface; a sub-project moon's own view tethers to its planet; independent moons keep `MoonMarkers`' teal ring. **Cutaway:** the cutaway publishes its two planes (`cutaway/cut-planes.ts`; on unmount `withdrawCutPlanes` withdraws only its own); surface pins, their heads and robots on the shown body use them (`clipIntersection`, assigned once — no recompile on open/close) and a cut marker is not a pick target; orbit pieces and moon pins are never cut.

UI chrome (side panel, chat, status lists) is ordinary React (Tailwind or CSS modules), rendered outside the canvas. Only the scene is in R3F.

- **Keyboard and accessibility of the panel** (P5-04, `apps/web/src/panel/a11y.tsx`; audit, deferred items and the axe summary in `docs/tasks/notes/P5-04-a11y.md`). Landmarks: the top bar is the banner and starts with a "Skip to main content" link (to the main area's `h1`); the navigation, one `main` per page (the dock, settings, history, the wizard) and the side column's `aside` are named. **Tab is never replaced**: every link and button stays a Tab stop, and arrow keys are added on top — the navigation follows a light version of the ARIA tree pattern (Down / Up / Home / End between rows, Right opens a row then enters it, Left goes up then closes; rows stay links with `aria-current`, no `role="tree"`, no roving tabindex), and the inbox's cards, the managed-agents cards and the history rows move with Down / Up / Home / End (`listKeys`: the item itself gets `tabindex="-1"` and the focus; fields and widgets that use the arrows keep them, and so does whatever is open in the list — a menu (`data-escape-layer`) or a pending confirmation — so nothing is left open behind the focus). **Focus is never dropped**: a route change puts it on the new page's `h1` when it was lost or in the top bar (`useRouteFocus`); a list whose focused item went away (a card answered, a row archived) gives it to the item now in its place, else the list's heading (`useFocusRescue`, a `MutationObserver`); in the main area a question answered (an inline card of the thread, the Steward view's card or clarification box) gives it to the next card, else the main area's title. Popovers, confirmations and edit forms take the focus (a confirmation on "Keep", the safe answer; Escape keeps and gives it back to what opened it, or "More"); disclosures (More, ⋯) keep it on their toggle, Tab enters them; the history inspector, the wizard's steps, the legend's toggle, a replay's exit (to what opened it, else the main area's title) and a column's "Hide" all hand it on. A visually hidden polite live region says when the "waiting for you" count changes (never on load); the connection state is not a live region (a named `role="img"`), so reconnect retries are not announced. Reduced motion: one `prefers-reduced-motion` rule turns off every animation and transition under `.pnl`, each animating stylesheet carries its own block, and code-driven smooth scrolling (`scrollBehavior()`) jumps instead. Checked by axe-core in jsdom (`apps/web/src/a11y/`, colour contrast excluded: no layout engine) over every main view, with zero serious or critical findings, violations and "needs review" alike; an `aria-label` goes only on an element whose role takes a name (`role="group"`, `"status"`, `"definition"` on a `dd`).

### 2.2 Backend: Node.js 22+ / TypeScript "Mission Control" server

**Recommendation:** Node.js (LTS) with TypeScript, Fastify for HTTP, `ws` for WebSockets, `better-sqlite3` for persistence (WAL mode), `zod` for shared schemas.

Why Node and not Rust/Python: the agent CLIs are all Node-friendly, the Claude Agent SDK is first-class in TypeScript, `node-pty` exists for wrapping arbitrary CLIs, and one language across server and UI lets you share the protocol types (`packages/protocol`). Bun is a reasonable substitute if you prefer it (`bun:sqlite`, fast startup), but `node-pty` and the SDK are better tested on Node.

Responsibilities:

- **Agent runtime**: spawn, supervise, pause, resume, restart and kill agent processes; own their stdio; enforce hard caps (§5.4).
- **Adapters** (see §3): translate each runtime's protocol into one internal event model. **The brains are agents too** and run through the same adapter (§5.1).
- **Status store**: the always-current, read-without-asking status of every agent (see §4) — a rebuildable projection of the event log, never the source of truth.
- **Domain model & persistence**: projects, tasks, bodies, workspaces/worktrees, proposals, questions, agent sessions, event log — in SQLite (§6).
- **Brain mailboxes**: the event-driven wake-up mechanism for the Harmonizer and Waykeepers (§5.2).
- **API**: WebSocket for live events with snapshot + resume (§6.3), plus a small REST surface for history. Authenticated with a single-user token (§8).
- **Process cleanup** (P4-14, SPEC decision 76; notes and the safety argument in `docs/tasks/notes/P4-14-process-cleanup.md`): a Steward's leftover processes — dev servers, watchers, headless browsers that outlive its work — are found by **attribution** (`runtime/process-attribution.ts`, pure) over a `ps` snapshot (`runtime/process-table.ts`: `ps -A -ww -o pid,ppid,pgid,uid,stat,lstart,command` on macOS and Linux; working directories from `/proc/<pid>/cwd` or `lsof -a -d cwd`; the agent marker from `/proc/<pid>/environ` or `ps -E -ww -o pid,command` — only what follows the known command line counts, and macOS hides the environment of platform binaries; listening ports from `lsof -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN`, or `/proc/net/tcp` + `/proc/<pid>/fd` on Linux without lsof). **The marker:** the supervisor puts `GALACTIC_AGENT_ID=<agentId>` last in every agent child's allow-listed environment (no spec can override it), so everything an agent starts inherits it.
