Since the phase review (P1-32, P1-37): the interrupt hook runs inside the grace window (a CLI that never answers the interrupt still reaches SIGTERM/SIGKILL); **whenever a group leader exits, for any reason, the rest of its group gets SIGTERM (and SIGCONT) then SIGKILL**, so dev servers and background shells do not outlive their agent, and `reconcile()` also kills the leftover group of a dead leader recorded in this boot; process identity is the basename of the whole `ps -o comm=` (macOS paths with spaces) plus a start-time check (`ps -o lstart=`, 5 s tolerance) against the row's `startedAt`, so a recycled pid — say the user's own `claude` — is never killed; `adopt()` refuses a restart policy (the SDK could not reconnect to a relaunched CLI). **Launch intents (P8-11; notes `docs/tasks/notes/P8-11-launch-intent.md`).** Every fork goes through `launch()` (`spawn`, `adopt`, restarts), which first writes a launch intent — agent, command, args, cwd, server run id, boot time, real-clock time — to `agent_launches` (migration 0013, runtime bookkeeping like `agent_processes`) and gives the child `GALACTIC_LAUNCH_ID=<intent id>` (a UUID) next to the agent marker; the PID-table row carries `meta.launchId` and its write removes the intent. If the intent cannot be written (a full disk) nothing is forked (`start_failed`); if the row cannot be written after the fork the intent stays. At startup, after `reconcile()` and before anything spawns, `reconcileLaunches()` handles the intents of earlier runs: one whose row exists completed; one of another boot is forgotten; for the rest, `launch-intents.ts` (pure) matches process groups from one `ps` snapshot — the leader alive, carrying the agent marker and the launch id, started within 5 s of the intent, orphaned, its cwd inside the launch's; or the leader gone and a member carrying both in that cwd — an unreadable environment never qualifies a group, and a group holding a user-facing app (or anything below one), another user's process or the server's own is left whole. Each group is re-checked, then SIGTERM + SIGCONT, SIGKILL after `termMs`; the launch is recorded as an interrupted session of the agent so process attribution's sweep finds what escaped its group. Fault injection: `testHooks.afterFork`.

---

## 4. Supervision without interruption (status store)

The spec's requirement — supervisors know statuses without interrupting — is solved by **never asking the agent**. Instead:

1. Each adapter's event stream is folded into a per-agent **status record**: `state ∈ {starting, working, waiting_for_input, idle, stalled, paused, interrupted, finished, failed, stopped}`, `current_activity` (last tool call or a rolling LLM-written one-liner), `last_event_at`, `pending_question`, `progress` (task-level checklist if the agent reports one), `cost_so_far`, `caps` (used vs. limit). `stalled` is set by a per-runtime silence threshold, never by killing; the runtime logs it as `agent.stalled` once per silence (P8-04: `StatusStoreOptions.onStalled` → `AgentRuntime.noteStalled`), so the supervisor is woken and the history shows it, and output brings the agent back to `working` without an event.
2. The status store is an in-memory **projection** of the event log, rebuildable from it on startup and pushed to the UI over WebSocket as batched diffs (≈10 Hz, never per token, and only for records that changed: the record carries `startedAt` and the client extrapolates the running wall clock from it rather than being sent a fresh `caps.wallClockMs.used` every second — P1-25). **The projection has one implementation, in the server** (P1-05): clients receive status records in the `snapshot` and as status messages afterwards and never fold `agent.*` events into records themselves — a second implementation in the web client would drift from the server's and make a resuming client disagree with a snapshotting one (decided with P0-18).
   Ended records are retained for a bounded time and count (default 24 h, 500) and then dropped; the drop is announced in the next `status` message (`removed`) so clients forget them too, and the log keeps the history (P1-16). After a replay the full set is sent with `replace: true` and the client swaps its map, so a record pruned while it was away disappears too (P1-38). `pendingQuestionId` is cleared by `question.cancelled`, `agent.idle` and `agent.interrupted` (P1-34).
3. The Harmonizer and Waykeepers get a `get_agent_status(agent_id)` / `list_supervised_agents()` **tool** that reads the store. Reading it costs nothing to the supervised agent.
4. Task and project states (SPEC §4.4) are derived in the server from agent states and open questions — no LLM call needed for the basic status views.
5. Optional cheap summariser: every N events (or on idle), a small model turns the recent event window into a human sentence ("Refactoring auth module, 3 files edited, running tests"). This drives both the panel text and the satellite animation choice (gathering / transporting / building). The raw last event is always shown next to the summary so a lying summary is visible.

---

The system summary counts (open questions, user-held questions, blocked tasks and projects) come from a pure function in `packages/protocol` (`summarize`, P2-15) over the domain rows: server and client compute them from the same state, so no summary message is sent and it cannot drift.

**Status views (P5-02).** The panel answers "the status of anything" (SPEC §4.3) without an LLM call: `apps/web/src/panel/status/status-model.ts` computes the system view (projects and tasks by work state, agents by state, a "needs attention" list — blocked on the user, failed, interrupted, stalled, close to a cap, in that order, oldest first within each — open questions by kind and holder, decision 58's waiting count, cost totals with the Harmonizer's own cost — P5-05: every other agent shows its own on its card, the Steward view or its project's view — and the global caps' usage), the project view (its tasks blocked first with each task's Steward state and summed cost, Stewards against `maxStewardsPerWaykeeper`, its questions, cost and sub-projects) and the task facts the Steward view adds, all as pure functions over the client world (folded log + status records) and the settings' caps. Costs sum `costSoFar` over the status records the server still holds (live, or ended within the 24 h / 500 retention), dollars where reported and tokens only for agents without dollars, and every cost fact says so; "agents active today (lifetime cost)" is the whole `costSoFar` of the records whose last event is after local midnight (a record has no per-day split). The system view leaves the scene harness's agents (`meta.harness`) out of attention, costs, agent counts and caps, and takes the Harmonizer from `currentHarmonizer`; a blocked task whose project is blocked folds into the project's attention entry ("· N blocked tasks"). Two counts are estimates: the per-Waykeeper cap meter counts the project's tasks' live Stewards (the enforcer counts by `supervisorId`), and spawns in the last hour come from the agents' `createdAt` (the enforcer keeps its own in-memory window) — their tooltips say so. The dock's "Chat | Status" switch (Harmonizer and Waykeepers, and the Harmonizer slot with no Harmonizer row, decision 44) is a per-browser preference (`galactic.panel.dock.view`); the conversation stays mounted under the status view. The only LLM path is the optional "Ask the Harmonizer for a narrative status" button, an ordinary chat message to the Harmonizer (which answers with `report_status`).

**Hierarchy resume (P2-16).** The composition registers the brain definitions (`defineBrains`) before `recover()`. `AgentRuntime.recover()` interrupts the live agents deepest first, then reopens the interrupted ones shallowest first; an agent whose role has a definition goes through the brain resumer (`BrainRunner.resume`), any other through `reopen` (`resumeSession(sessionRef)` with `RESUME_PROMPT`, falling back with `freshPrompt` to `adapter.spawn` on the same row). A brain's mailbox is rebuilt from the log (`brains/requeue.ts`): everything after its last delivered turn, minus its own effects and messages it already got. The reopen records how far its first prompt folded the log on `agent.spawned.foldedUpTo`; a later requeue cuts there once that session's first turn ended (`agent.idle`), so a second crash folds nothing twice and a session lost before its first turn folds its items again. Inside the brain's own turn an actor-less event is dropped as possibly its own effect, except another agent's `agent.*` lifecycle, a question event or a proposal decision not in the same command (one `at`) as an event the brain is the actor of. The runner marks the folded messages delivered when the first turn ends, not when the handle comes back. **A resume can fail late:** the SDK adapter returns the handle at once and a lost or corrupt session shows only as `agent.failed` / `agent.finished` when the CLI exits. A session resumed from its ref that ends before its first `agent.idle` (and was not being stopped) is therefore a failed resume: it is logged `agent.interrupted{resume failed: …}` and a brain gets the fresh session on the same row (`ReopenOptions.onFallback` tells its runner first); a plain agent that failed before doing anything stays interrupted. `endAgentQuestions(…, 'interrupted')` keeps a brain's command-sourced questions (`meta.source: command`) open, and the ones of that kind an interrupted holder holds stay with it; recovery re-routes what an agent it passed over or could not resume held. `refuseReopen` also refuses a second Steward per task and a second Waykeeper per project. While `recover()` reopens, the interrupted agents it is about to reopen (and a brain whose lost resume is being replaced) are reserved (`AgentRuntime.isReserved`): the brain queries report them `starting`, so the spawn guards of a supervisor resumed first do not replace them, and question routing counts them living. `composed.keepHarmonizer(start)`, armed by `index.ts` after the boot-time start, replaces a Harmonizer that ends (other than by the user's `control.stop`) when none runs or is reserved, at most 3 times in 10 minutes. The Harmonizer's start notes also withdraw the open `ask_user` / `ask_confirmation` questions of an ended Harmonizer and name them (their answer would wake only the asker, and `finished_work` accepts only its own confirmation). `POST /api/agents/:id/recover` takes the same path; one Harmonizer at a time holds on both. After recovery and `startHarmonizer`, `announceRecovery` adds the report line to the running Harmonizer (`BrainRunner.note`); `BrainDefinition.startNotes(ctx)` gives lines for a session's first turn (the Harmonizer's unrelayed closure requests). A tool call in a brain's first turn waits for its agent id. A resumed session's first message is `RESUME_PROMPT`, plus — when the interruption cancelled questions of the agent (decision 21) — one line per cancelled question and "ask it again" (`AgentRuntime.resumePromptFor`); without it real Claude went idle. Steward sessions load `['user', 'project', 'local']` settings (`ClaudeSdkAdapter.stewardSettingSources`; the real-Claude checks use `['project', 'local']`), brains none.

**Restarting the Harmonizer (P5-06; SPEC decision 82).** `POST /api/harmonizer/restart` (bearer-guarded like every `/api` route; any authenticated client may call it, like `POST /api/agents/:id/stop` and the panel's controls — a control action, not a settings write, so not local-only) runs `harmonizerRestarter` (`apps/server/src/brains/harmonizer/restart.ts`), wired in `index.ts`, under the Harmonizer lock (`withHarmonizerLock`: one start of the Harmonizer at a time per runtime, shared by the boot start, `keepHarmonizer` and the restart, so one Harmonizer is structural). The live Harmonizer is stopped first, with `by: user`, and nothing else happens unless it ended — a stop that fails leaves it running and driven by its runner (500). The stop ends the open `ask_user` / `ask_confirmation` questions it asked (`meta.source: command`, kind clarification, not a project's closure question; one still open is withdrawn as the user) and the new one is told about them; the stop is the user's, so `keepHarmonizer` does not start another one beside it (its closure question is cancelled with the stop and `closureNotes` relays the closure request again; the questions it held move on as on any stop, P2-03), and a fresh one starts on the same runtime and working directory — the stopped one's, else the newest Harmonizer row's (one the user stopped from the ops console, an interrupted or failed one), else the boot setting (`GALACTIC_HARMONIZER`); with none of these it refuses. `StartHarmonizerOptions.notes` puts lines first in the new one's first turn, once: "the user restarted the Harmonizer from the panel … tell the user in one line", then one line per withdrawn question ("ask again if it still matters"), then its usual start notes. A start that fails after the stop is retried once on the boot runtime (what `keepHarmonizer` would start), logged through `onError`, so the user is not left without a Harmonizer. When the last Harmonizer is interrupted (not live), the fresh one starts beside its row: its own tool-asked questions are withdrawn by the new one's start notes, and the questions it *held* (a Waykeeper's, escalated) move on to the user once the new one runs (`AgentRuntime.releaseInterruptedHarmonizers`, called by every `startHarmonizer` that starts one, so `keepHarmonizer` too; decision 45). The row stays `interrupted` (an ended status is a sink), like the Harmonizers startup recovery passes over, and is not reopened while the new one runs. **Restart window:** at most 3 restarts per 10 minutes (as `keepHarmonizer`'s); past it the reply is 429 with `Retry-After` and "try again in N min". A Harmonizer's spawn does not count toward the per-hour spawn window (`CapsEnforcer.noteSpawn` skips the role; decision 43), so restarts never block Steward or Waykeeper spawns. The reply is `RestartHarmonizerResponse {agentId, replaced?}`; a second restart while one is under way, an interrupted Harmonizer on its way back (`runtime.isReserved`), a missing runtime or a refused start answer 409 `refused` (redacted); anything unexpected is 500. The scene harness's dormant stand-in is never taken for the Harmonizer. In the panel the Harmonizer's dock bar has `HarmonizerControls` (`apps/web/src/panel/controls.tsx`): Pause / Resume through `POST /api/control` on the agent, "Resume session" (`POST /api/agents/:id/recover`) only while it is interrupted, and "Restart Harmonizer" behind `ConfirmBox` (the shared inline confirmation, focus on "Keep"); no Stop. The dock stays on the `harmonizer` selection, which resolves to the newest running Harmonizer (`currentHarmonizer`), so it follows the new id when the stream brings it.

## 5. The Harmonizer and Waykeepers as "tool-armed" agents

Do not make the hierarchy purely prompt-based. Build the Harmonizer and Waykeepers on the Claude Agent SDK with custom tools that mutate the domain model, so the important decisions are auditable and the UI reflects them immediately.

### 5.1 Brains are agents, run through the adapter

The Harmonizer and each Waykeeper are rows in the `Agent` table (`role`, `runtime: claude-sdk`, `sessionRef`, `supervisorId`, `bodyId`) and are spawned through the **same `claude-sdk` adapter** as Stewards, with `tools` and `systemPrompt` supplied. Consequently they emit `agent.*` events, appear in the status store with real statuses and cost, have a satellite/HQ presence on their body, and are resumed after a restart like any other agent. `packages/brains` (or `apps/server/src/brains`) contains only prompts, tool schemas and tool handlers written against a `DomainCommands` interface — never SDK or runtime imports. The implementation is `Commands` (`apps/server/src/domain/commands.ts`, P2-01), built by `composeRuntime`: domain commands run synchronously under the one write rule and refuse with a `DomainError{code: not_found | invalid_transition | conflict | stale | invalid}` that rolls everything back; agent commands (spawn, stop, pause, resume, answering a question a live session asked) go through the runtime. Task and project state transitions follow one table (`domain/states.ts`, SPEC §4.4).

### 5.2 Mailbox, one turn at a time

Brains are long-lived but **event-driven, never polling**:

- Each brain has a **mailbox**. Wake-worthy events are appended to it: a user message, `question.opened / answered`, `proposal.decided`, `agent.finished / failed / stalled`, task/project control actions from the panel, a subordinate's report. Never `agent.output` or `agent.tool_call`.
- **One turn at a time per brain.** A turn drains the whole mailbox into a single prompt ("since your last turn: Steward S3 finished task T7; the user answered Q12; the user paused T9"). Events arriving during a turn wait for the next one. Two Stewards finishing together produce one turn, not two, and a brain's own tool side effects never re-enter it mid-turn.
- **Tools that need the user are non-blocking.** `ask_user`, `escalate`, `propose`, `request_project_closure` create the corresponding object and end the turn; the answer arrives later as a mailbox event. A brain's turn is therefore always short, and a restart never loses an hours-long in-flight turn.
- **The database is the brain's memory.** Each turn is seeded with the current state it needs (`list_supervised_agents()`, its project's tasks and open questions) rather than relying on an ever-growing transcript; sessions are reset periodically (e.g. on each project milestone) with a summary carried over, so context never silently compacts away "knows the status of every task".

**Brain runner (P2-06).** `apps/server/src/brains/runner.ts` drives one brain: it spawns it through `DomainCommands.spawnAgent` with `tools` and `systemPrompt` and calls `AgentRuntime.attachMailbox`; from then on messages to the brain (the user's chat, a subordinate's report) are only logged as `message.sent`, folded into the next turn by the mailbox and marked `message.delivered` once that turn is delivered. A turn starts only when the brain is idle and ends at its `agent.idle`; its prompt is "Since your last turn: 1. … 2. …" plus the seed block (supervised agents with status, the project's tasks, the open clarifications it holds or its agents asked). Events committed together become one turn. The whitelist is one table, `WAKE_RULES` (`brains/wake.ts`): `message.sent` from the user or an agent below the brain (`WakeScope.below`: a direct report, or a sub-project's Waykeeper asking the Harmonizer for closure); `task.created` in a Waykeeper's project by someone else (not `proposed`: plan tasks wait for their card); `question.opened/escalated/reassigned` to the brain as new holder and `question.answered/cancelled` naming it in `holders` (clarifications only; a question opened `toUser` concerns no supervisor); `proposal.*` decisions on proposals it owns; `agent.finished/failed/stalled` of supervised agents; `control.*` aimed at the brain, a supervised agent, a task or project it runs, or concerning it as a supervisor (`concernedSupervisors`, P2-14: an agent target concerns its supervisor; a task its Stewards' supervisors and its project's Waykeeper, or the Harmonizer for a standalone task; a project its Waykeeper(s) and their supervisors, the parent project's Waykeeper and the Harmonizer; never the actor); `budget.set/warning/exceeded` to the brain that runs the work (a project's Waykeeper, or the task's project's; the Harmonizer only for an asteroid task — never for a Waykeeper's project, whose notice its chat already shows), while `budget.spawn_refused` wakes no one (the asker has the tool error; P8-01). Tool handlers receive a `DomainCommands` proxy: every event a tool call appended is taken out of the mailbox, so a brain's own actions never wake it. `ask_user`, `escalate`, `propose`, `request_project_closure` (or any tool marked `endsTurn`) end the turn: later tool calls in it are refused with `turn_ended`; a refused command reaches the model as `{error: <DomainError code>, message}`. Session reset (after N turns, or on demand) has the brain call `carry_summary`, then `AgentRuntime.renewSession` replaces the adapter session on the **same agent row** (`agent.interrupted{reason: "session reset"}` then `agent.spawned`), and the new session's first prompt carries the summary. A reset keeps the questions the brain holds (the agent lives on; only its own open questions are cancelled with the old session). A tool call's events, as the command surface returns them, are only what that call caused — its own transaction and its agent effects carrying its actor — so another actor's event logged meanwhile still wakes the brain. The mailbox is in memory; P2-16 restores it after a restart. **A user's slash command (P8-13)** — a user `message.sent` whose first word is `/name` and that the brain's session offers (`AgentRuntime.hasCommand`: one of `BRAIN_SLASH_COMMANDS`, as the runtime read them when the session opened, §3.4) — is not folded: it is delivered alone, as typed, as its own turn (no "since your last turn", no seed), so the runtime runs the command (`/compact`, a skill); what came before it in the mailbox is one turn first, what comes after it the next. A name the session does not offer, a path like `/tmp/x` and anything another agent sends stay lines of the turn prompt. A session's first prompt (a spawn, a reset) still folds everything.

**Placement (P2-20).** `checkPlacement` (`domain/agents.ts`) holds the hierarchy rules for `spawnAgent` and `updateAgent` (SPEC decision 32): the Harmonizer on Earth, one at a time, unsupervised; every other agent under its prescribed supervisor, matched by role and project. The Phase 1 ops console (`/api/agents` → `AgentRuntime.spawn`) is outside these rules. A brain is also woken when a clarification it asked through a tool (the Harmonizer's `ask_user`, a Waykeeper's own question — marked `meta.source: "command"` by the command surface) is answered or cancelled; a question its own session raised, and any permission question, is answered through the adapter and never enters its mailbox.

**Brain tests (P2-07).** Brains are tested without Claude through `apps/server/src/brains/testing/`: a `BrainScript` stands in for the model — turn rules match the turn prompt and their async bodies call the brain's real tools (through the runner, the gate and `DomainCommands`), chaining results; a `BrainTranscript` records every prompt, call, result and refusal, and `verify()` fails on an expected turn that never came, an unexpected turn or a refused call. The fake adapter's `script` step is the only seam. A brain is a non-Steward spawn with server tools: a Steward that carries the server's deliverable tools (P9-04) still runs its `scenario:<name>`, whose `script` steps may call them.

### 5.3 Tools

| Agent | Key tools |
|---|---|
| Harmonizer | `find_related_work(request)`, `size_request → {kind, rationale, suggestedName, proposedBody, workspace}`, `propose(proposal)`, `create_project`, `create_task`, `assign_body`, `spawn_waykeeper`, `spawn_steward` (asteroid tasks; the Harmonizer is their supervisor), `propose_promotion(task\|project, toKind)`, `close_task` (asteroid tasks), `stop_agent`, `pause_agent`, `resume_agent`, `get_agent_status`, `list_supervised_agents`, `tell_steward`, `list_steward_processes`, `stop_steward_processes` (asteroid Stewards), `ask_user`, `answer_question` (clarification kind only), `report_status`, `freePlanets()` / `freeMoons(planetId?)` (deterministic body catalogue checks), `ask_closure`, `ask_confirmation`, `close_project`, `finished_work` (archive, pin, unpin, reopen; P2-13), `control_work(target: task\|project, action: pause\|resume\|stop\|cancel\|edit, changes?, questionId?)` (P5-07, decision 83), `declare_deliverable`, `remove_deliverable`, `list_deliverables` (standalone tasks; P9, §9.3) |
| Waykeeper | `plan_tasks(plan)` (presented once for approval), `create_task`, `spawn_steward` (creates the worktree), `merge_task(taskId)`, `close_task`, `pause_agent`, `resume_agent`, `stop_agent`, `get_agent_status`, `list_supervised_agents`, `answer_question` (clarification kind only), `escalate(question)`, `request_promotion`, `request_project_closure`, `report_status`, `tell_steward`, `list_steward_processes`, `stop_steward_processes`, `declare_deliverable`, `remove_deliverable`, `list_deliverables` (its project and tasks; P9, §9.3) |
| Steward | Whatever the underlying runtime provides (files, shell, tests) inside its worktree; plus clarification questions and permission requests, which the adapter maps to `agent.needs_input`; on `claude-sdk`, the server's `declare_deliverable` / `list_deliverables` for its own task (P9, §9.3) |

Escalation is an **event with an owner**: `needs_input` creates a `Question{id, kind: clarification|permission, originAgentId, holder: {type: agent|user, id?}, status, context, answeredBy?, answer?}`. Holder routing follows `Agent.supervisorId` generically (Harmonizer for asteroid Stewards). A **permission** question is resolved only by the policy table or the user and is routed to the user's inbox without entering any brain's mailbox; a **clarification** question may be answered by its holding supervisor from context (logged with `answeredBy`) or escalated. When the user answers it anywhere in the UI, the server marks it answered and notifies every holder, so upper levels stop waiting. An orphaned question (holder died) moves to the next living supervisor or to the user — the server, not the agents, owns questions.

**Question service (P2-03, `domain/question-service.ts`).** `agent.needs_input` opens the question with holder = the origin's first living ancestor by `supervisorId` (permission → user; P2-04 puts the policy evaluation in front) and `context.origin = {role, taskId?, projectId?, bodyId?, recent: the agent's last 10 events}`. An agent's end (the runtime's terminal path, startup recovery, `Commands.recordAgentEvent`) cancels its own open questions and re-assigns the ones it held, in the same command (`question.reassigned{reason: "holder <id> <how>"}`). `question.answered` and `question.cancelled` carry `holders[]`, every agent that held the question. Liveness comes from the status store in the composed runtime, from the log's lifecycle events otherwise.

**Harmonizer (P2-08, `brains/harmonizer/`).** `harmonizerBrain(deps)` is the prompt plus its tools; deps are `projectsRoot`, `checkPath` (`Workspaces.checkPath`), `startWaykeeper` (wired in `index.ts` to start the Waykeeper brain) and `stewardPrompt` (P2-11). `find_related_work` is a deterministic word-overlap search; `size_request` informs and changes nothing (the body `chooseBody` would give, a suggested name and workspace, related work, free planets and moons). `propose` stores what the card asks for in `Proposal.meta.request` (and the `BodyChoice` in `meta.bodyChoice` when no planet or moon is free, for P2-09). `create_project` / `create_task` need an approved card and record `meta.proposalId`; a standalone task also records the card's `workspace` (and `relatedProjectId`), and `spawn_steward` runs its Steward there only (no workspace input). A project task's `create_task` says when the project has no running Waykeeper to pick it up. `spawn_waykeeper` for a sub-project needs a running parent Waykeeper. `Commands.spawnAgent` holds one live Harmonizer, one live Waykeeper per project and one live Steward per task with a synchronous slot, so parallel spawns cannot both pass; an asteroid Steward working inside a live project's workspace gets the strictest of that project's table and the global default. `index.ts` starts the Harmonizer after `listen` on `claude-sdk` by default, dev mode included (`GALACTIC_HARMONIZER=fake|off` to change it), cwd = the projects root, unless one already runs on that runtime — a live one on another runtime is stopped and replaced; tests build the app through `buildApp`, never `index.ts`.

**Waykeeper (P2-10, `brains/waykeeper/`).** The prompt and its tools; the supervision tools both brains share live in `brains/common-tools.ts` (`stop/pause/resume_agent`, `get_agent_status`, `list_supervised_agents`, `answer_question`, `report_status`; the Harmonizer uses them all but `report_status`, for which it has its own read-only system overview). Their reach is the caller's subtree (the `supervisorId` chain); a refusal is a `DomainError` returned to the model. Tools get `ctx.send(agentId, text)` (a `message.sent` from the brain that wakes only its addressee) and `ctx.workspaces` (built by `composeRuntime` from `ComposeOptions.workspaces`, which `index.ts` fills from `projectsRoot`/`dataDir`). A plan card is a `Proposal` with `meta.plan = {projectId, waykeeperId, taskIds}`, made with its proposed tasks in one transaction by `DomainCommands.proposePlan`; approving it approves its tasks in the same transaction; its decision wakes the project's Waykeeper (`WakeScope.ownsProposal`), never the Harmonizer. A spawn's result carries only the spawn's own events (row, body, session), so a Steward that finishes before the call returns still wakes its supervisor. A promotion request's card (`meta.promotion`) belongs to the Waykeeper of `fromProjectId` the same way. A merge-conflict question for the user is opened with `OpenQuestionInput.toUser` (held by the user from the start, no escalation hop); `merge_task{sendBack: true}` carries out the user's "send the Steward back" once they answered it (a respawned Steward gets the send-back, then the task and its first instructions, `meta.stewardInstructions`); `close_task` withdraws an open conflict question (`DomainCommands.withdrawQuestion`). `request_project_closure` reports to the running Harmonizer, also from a sub-project.

**Proposal flow (P2-09, `domain/parking.ts`, `domain/approval.ts`).** A card needs a body when `bodyRequestOf(ctx, p)` says so (a non-plan, non-asteroid card whose `meta.request` is `new_project` or `standalone_task` resized up, or a promotion card of either shape without `toProjectId` — `promotionBodyRequest`). `parkIfNoRoom` runs at creation and after an edit (`meta.parked = ProposalParking`, `proposal.parked` with the same fields); approval checks `roomFor` and either parks or emits `proposal.approved` and `carryOut` (the project or task, with `meta.proposalId`; for a promotion, the task's new project on the chosen body — `createPromotedProject` — or the project target's `moveProject`, so the body is the approval's; P2-12's flow does the rest). `release()` calls `unparkWaiting` with `ctx.choose` (`EventLog.choose`, the status store's running check, set by the composition), so `body.freed` and `proposal.unparked` commit together and choose as an approval does; a card `chooseBody` refuses outright stays parked. A reopen frees its old body with `release(ctx, body, false)` and un-parks after it holds the new one. Reject and edit run it too. Every decision but a park drops `meta.parked`; `foldProposalMeta` (protocol) is the one fold for server and clients.

**Stewards (P2-11).** Every Steward's first prompt comes from `brains/steward-prompt.ts` (the supervisor's instructions first, then the task and project context, then the etiquette: ask through the question tool, never ask permission in prose, end with "Done:" / "Not done:"). `AgentRuntime.onTurnEnded` reports each `agent.idle` with the turn's last text, whether it was cut short and the queued count; `runtime/steward-reports.ts` turns a Steward's completed, unqueued turn into a `message.sent` from the Steward to its current supervisor (when that supervisor has a mailbox) — the existing wake rule wakes only the supervisor. A Steward's `agent.finished` without a result gets its last text. `tell_steward {agentId, text}` is the supervisor's queued, non-interrupting reply. `list_steward_processes {agentId?}` and `stop_steward_processes {agentId, pids?}` (P4-14, `brains/steward-tools.ts`, through `ctx.processes`) reach the caller's own Stewards only (`supervisorId` = the caller, like `tell_steward`); which pids are a Steward's is the server's attribution (§2.2 "Process cleanup"), never the model's. Both brains' prompts make the check part of finishing a task (before `merge_task`/`close_task`) and of handling a stopped or failed Steward, with one exception: a server the user asked to keep running, which they mention in their report.

**Steward processes (P4-14, SPEC decision 76).** `list_steward_processes` / `stop_steward_processes` (`brains/steward-tools.ts`) reach a Steward when the caller is its recorded supervisor, or — decision 32, supervisors matched by role and project — the newest live Waykeeper of the Steward's task's project, or the live Harmonizer for a standalone task's Steward (`maySupervise`); `tell_steward` still checks the recorded supervisor only. Without `agentId` the list covers the caller's live Stewards and those that ended in the last 24 h, from one `ps` snapshot (`StewardProcesses.listMany`). A stop without pids is refused while the Steward runs; a `held` process (in use by its session) is stopped only when named. Which processes are a Steward's is always the server's attribution (§2.2).

**Promotions (P2-12, `brains/promotion.ts`).** `PromotionFlow` subscribes to the log: a task promotion's `proposal.created` pauses the task's live Stewards (as `system:promotion:<card>`), `proposal.rejected` resumes the ones it paused, `proposal.approved` runs deterministic, idempotent steps — `createPromotedProject` (`meta.proposalId`, `meta.promotedFrom`), a Waykeeper when the project has none living, `moveTask` / `transferTask` / `moveProject` — then resumes the Stewards and records the outcome (`Proposal.meta.carriedOut` + `promotion.carried_out`, which wakes the card's owner and the Waykeeper of the work's project). `start()` carries out approved cards left unrecorded and re-pauses the Stewards whose last pause was a still-undecided card's; `index.ts` starts the flow after the Harmonizer and stops it on shutdown. No running supervisor or a refused Waykeeper spawn throws `RetryLater`: the card stays unrecorded (Stewards paused) and is retried on agent lifecycle events and every `retryMs` (1 min) for `giveUpMs` (2 h); a Waykeeper started meanwhile by someone else is adopted. A final failure fails the card's new project if it holds nothing and frees its body. Hand-overs (`domain/handover.ts`): `moveTask`, `transferTask` and a `parentProjectId` change re-parent the affected agents inside the same command (the newest living candidate, else the newest row; none → `conflict`, rolled back).

**Closure (P2-13).** The Harmonizer's `ask_closure` asks "Anything else on <project>?" (the question id kept in `meta.closureRequest`), `close_project` closes (which stops the Waykeeper and its Stewards and withdraws the open question), `finished_work` archives, pins, unpins or reopens. Both check the user's word in code (decision 11): the closure question answered by the user with "Close …", or an `ask_confirmation` question (options `[<the action on the target>, 'No']`, opened by this Harmonizer) answered by the user with its first option or yes; otherwise `invalid`.

**Conversational control map (P5-05, P5-07; SPEC §4.3's last line, decision 83).** What the Harmonizer does with the same command the panel's `POST /api/control` calls (`DomainCommands.control` / `stopAgent` / `pauseAgent` / `resumeAgent`, so the log records it as `by` and `concernedSupervisors` wakes the same supervisors): an agent below it — `pause_agent`, `resume_agent`, `stop_agent` (an agent-level cancel is a stop); a task or project — `control_work` (pause, resume and edit at once; stop and cancel on the user's answered confirmation); a project — `close_project`; finished work — `finished_work` (archive, pin, unpin, reopen); the confirmed ones only on the user's answer (decision 42). `control_work` (`brains/harmonizer/control-tools.ts`) parses its input with zod; an edit takes a task's `description` / `priority` or a project's `name` only — the trust level and permission table are refused (decision 49), as is any field the panel cannot edit; it refuses scene-harness rows (`meta.harness`, decision 79); stop and cancel pass `checkUserAgreed` (an `ask_confirmation` this Harmonizer asked for exactly that action and target, answered by the user with its first choice or yes) and the answer is used up in the control's own transaction — `control(target, action, by, {confirmation: questionId})` runs `consumeConfirmation` (the question row's `meta.usedFor`, e.g. "stop task t-1") and `recordControl` in one `runCommand`, so two calls with one answer never both pass, a control the domain refuses leaves the answer unused, and the work's history shows no extra edit. It writes with the turn's origin: `BrainToolContext.origin` is `originOfMessages` over the `message.sent` events folded into the current turn (P2-21), so the remote floor applies as for a panel request (today no `control_work` edit writes a policy; the origin is there for defence in depth). A project task's close stays the Waykeeper's (`close_task`, decision 11). Status of anything is `report_status` (read-only, the same projections as the panel's status views). Proposal, plan and promotion decisions stay the user's alone (decisions 34, 35, 40); the Harmonizer only proposes. The whole map is tested in `brains/harmonizer/control-mapping.test.ts`, `control-work.test.ts`, `closure.test.ts` and `harmonizer.test.ts`.

### 5.4 Hard caps (decision 18)

Enforced by the process supervisor, not by prompts, from Phase 1: per-agent wall-clock (working time: `CapsEnforcer.pause/resume` stop the clock while the agent is paused, any pause; Stewards and free-form agents only, see below) and tool-call caps; global maximum of concurrent agents; per-Waykeeper maximum of Stewards; maximum spawns per hour. Hitting a cap stops the agent with a `failed(reason: cap)` event the supervisor sees and reports. Dollar budgets per project and task (warn, refuse new agents) are described below (P8-01). Processes a Steward leaves behind outside its process group are swept by the server when the Steward ends or its task closes (P4-14, §2.2 "Process cleanup"), not left to the prompts alone.

**Wall-clock cap for Stewards only (P8-10; SPEC decision 103).** `wallClockCapApplies({role, meta})` (`packages/protocol`, `domain.ts`) is the one rule: true for a Steward, an agent of unknown role and any agent whose row has `meta.freeForm` (set by `AgentRuntime.spawn` when `SpawnRequest.freeForm` is passed, which only the ops console's `POST /api/agents` does, so it survives a restart); false for the Harmonizer and the Waykeepers. `CapsEnforcer.track` takes `TrackedInfo.freeForm` from the row and arms no wall-clock timer for a brain (nor on `resume`); the brain's clock still counts, and `usage()` reports it with `capped: false`. The tool-call cap, the spawn caps and budgets apply to brains as before. The status store (`wallClockCapped` option, wired in `compose.ts` with the same rule) keeps `StatusRecord.caps.wallClockMs.limit` (the configured cap, still required, so a page of the previous build parses the record) and adds `capped: false` for a brain (optional; absent means capped). Clients read the limit through `wallClockLimit(usage)` (`packages/protocol`), `undefined` when uncapped: the panel's cards and status views then show the brain's time alone and never flag it near the cap. (The first cut made `limit` optional and left it out — a page of the previous build then failed to parse every snapshot holding the Harmonizer and looped on 4002; P8 wave 2 review.)

**Spawn caps (P2-17).** `CapsEnforcer.canSpawn({role, supervisorId, supervisorRole, again})` runs in `AgentRuntime.spawn`, below `Commands.spawnAgent`, so every path meets the caps (brain tools, the panel, REST, the promotion flow). A refusal writes nothing and is `DomainError('conflict', reason)` naming the cap, the count, the limit and when it frees. `maxConcurrentAgents` counts non-brain agents only and the Harmonizer is never refused nor counted in the per-hour windows (SPEC decision 43; its restarts have their own window, P5-06); `maxStewardsPerWaykeeper` counts the live Stewards whose row names that Waykeeper; `maxSpawnsPerHour` / `maxSpawnsPerHourPerBrain` use a sliding hour over new agent rows, per brain by the new agent's `supervisorId`, seeded on startup from the `agents` rows created in the last hour. Resume and `renewSession` check with `again` (no rate check). Configured in `config.json` `caps` or `GALACTIC_MAX_CONCURRENT_AGENTS`, `GALACTIC_MAX_STEWARDS_PER_WAYKEEPER`, `GALACTIC_MAX_SPAWNS_PER_HOUR`, `GALACTIC_MAX_SPAWNS_PER_HOUR_PER_BRAIN`; defaults 4 / 3 / 30 / 20. `spawn_steward` removes the worktree (and a branch it created) when the spawn is refused. `tell_steward` allows 20 messages per Steward per sliding hour (in memory), bounding a text-only report/reply loop the tool-call cap would not see.

**Dollar budgets (P8-01; notes `docs/tasks/notes/P8-01-budgets.md`).** `projects.budget_usd` / `tasks.budget_usd` (migration 0012) hold the budgets; `setBudget` changes them (`project.updated`/`task.updated` + `budget.set`; raising or removing needs a local origin or `autoApproveRemote`). The event log projects every inserted event, in its transaction, through `projectBudgetEvent`: the per-agent spend (`agent_spend`, the protocol's `foldSpend`: per session the highest cumulative `cost.usd` reported, summed over sessions; a session is the last `agent.spawned`'s `sessionRef`) and the notices already given (`budget_notices`); a log written before migration 0012 is folded once at the next start (settings key `budget_projection`). `Commands.spawnAgent` refuses a Waykeeper or Steward whose task or any project above it has spent its budget (`budget.spawn_refused` + `DomainError('conflict')`); the Harmonizer, resumes and renewals are never refused. The `BudgetWatcher` (`runtime/budgets.ts`) logs `budget.warning` (at `budgets.warnAtPercent` — `config.json` or `GALACTIC_BUDGET_WARN_PERCENT`, 1–100, default 80; local requests only on the settings page) and `budget.exceeded` once per budget value and posts them, and the first refused spawn per budget, in the running Harmonizer's chat as system messages. `budget.spawn_refused` itself is logged once an hour per work, role and budget value (`SPAWN_REFUSED_EVERY_MS`; the caller is refused every time). The Harmonizer's `control_work` edit sets or lowers a budget at once but raises or removes one only on an `ask_confirmation` question for that change (action `budget` with the new `budgetUsd`; the first choice names the amount), used up in the control's transaction like a stop's (SPEC decision 98). The wake table's `budget.set` rule also wakes the Harmonizer when a budget is raised or removed for work nobody runs — an active project without a running Waykeeper, an active asteroid task without a running Steward (`WakeScope.unattended`, from the status store) — and its line asks it to `spawn_waykeeper` / `spawn_steward` if the budget now allows it. A runtime that reports no dollar figure counts as nothing, so a budget is not enforced on work none of whose agents reported a cost. Running agents are never stopped for money.

### 5.5 Control cascades (P2-14)

`domain/cascade.ts`: a panel or Harmonizer control on a task or project records `control.*` and sets the state with `by` (SPEC decision 28) in one command, then the `Commands` surface pauses, resumes or stops the item's live agents through the runtime, each logging its own `control.*{agent}`; a refusal by some agents rejects with `conflict` and the rest stands. Exposed as `POST /api/control {target, action}` (bearer-guarded, zod-validated: 400 / 404 / 409) and as `DomainCommands.control`. `retentionDue(tables)` lists finished, unpinned projects for P2-13's archival job.

---

## 6. Persistence, event log and API protocol

### 6.1 One write rule

Every command (a brain tool, a panel action, an adapter event fold) performs its **domain mutation and its event insert in one `better-sqlite3` transaction**; the event ID is the `events` rowid (monotonic, server-assigned); in-memory subscribers are notified **after commit**, strictly in `seq` order even when a subscriber runs a command of its own while being notified (its events are committed at once and published after the current batch). Events are buffered per outermost command and published only when it commits: a nested command that succeeded inside an outer one that then threw is rolled back with it and never published (P1-35). A crash can therefore never leave the domain tables and the event log disagreeing. **A command reads the clock once** (P2-01): every event it appends carries that reading as `at`, and every row it writes uses it for `createdAt` / `updatedAt`, so a client that folds `updatedAt = at` holds exactly the server's rows. Clients never trust their own timestamps for ordering. A migration that rebuilds a table (SQLite's only way to change a constraint) declares `-- migrate: foreign_keys=off`; the runner disables foreign keys around its transaction and refuses to commit if `foreign_key_check` reports anything (P2-02, migration 0005). The runner also refuses a database whose `schema_migrations` names an applied version differently from the file of that number (a database a branch with another `0011` migrated), with a message naming both (P8-01 review). The build copies the migrations into `dist` first and removes stale files after, so the folder is never empty (`scripts/copy-migrations.mjs`).

**Crashes and storage failures (P8-04, drills in `apps/server/src/test/drills/`, log in `docs/tasks/notes/P8-04-drills.md`).** SQLite runs in WAL mode with `synchronous=NORMAL`: a process kill loses nothing committed; a power loss or OS crash may roll back the last transactions, never half of one. A command the storage refuses (`isStorageFailure`: SQLITE_FULL, SQLITE_IOERR\*, BUSY, LOCKED, READONLY, CANTOPEN, PROTOCOL) rolls back whole and its error reaches the caller. The runtime's own writes — adapter events, `agent.spawned`, an agent's or the system's `message.sent` / `message.delivered`, an answer the agent already has — cannot be refused (the runtime has acted on them), so `AgentRuntime` holds what the storage refuses in an in-memory queue, every later runtime write waits behind it, and it is retried every second (`storageRetryMs`) and written in order once writes work again, each with the `at` of the moment it happened (`EventLog.runCommand(fn, {at})`); one console line when writes start failing, one when they work again. **Nothing commits ahead of the held events** (P8-04 review): the runtime registers an `EventLog.addBeforeCommand` hook, run before every outermost command (domain commands, the budget watcher, `createAgent` in a spawn), that writes the held queue first — and when it cannot, the command fails with the storage error, refused whole. **The user's actions through the runtime** (pause, resume, stop, an answer, a chat line — `by`/`sender` of type `user`) are refused the same way while anything is held, and are thrown rather than held when their own write fails (SPEC decision 99); agent-side writes keep being queued. The queue is bounded (`heldLimits`, default 10 000 events / 64 MiB of event JSON): past it the oldest agent output (`agent.tool_call`, `agent.progress`, `agent.stalled`) is dropped first, logged once per episode, and only when none is left the oldest write of any kind. SQLITE_READONLY and SQLITE_CANTOPEN are logged as permanent (`storage (permanent)`): the database will not take writes until the file is fixed; the queue stays bounded meanwhile. `AgentRuntime.storageState()` (`{held, bytes, dropped, since?, error?, code?, permanent}`) feeds the panel's storage banner. **Storage health in the panel (P8-09; notes `docs/tasks/notes/P8-09-storage.md`).** `StorageMonitor` (`runtime/storage-health.ts`, built by the composition) folds two sources into the protocol's `StorageHealth` (`{condition: ok | full | unavailable | unwritable, held, dropped, since?, error?}`, `packages/protocol/src/storage.ts`): the runtime's held queue, and every outermost command through `EventLog.addStorageObserver` — a command the storage refused (`isStorageFailure`, including the before-command hook's refusal) marks the storage refusing even when nothing is held (the user's action on a full disk with no agent running), the next command that commits an event clears that. The condition comes from the SQLite code (`storageConditionOf`: SQLITE_FULL → `full`; READONLY / CANTOPEN → `unwritable`; IOERR, BUSY, LOCKED, PROTOCOL → `unavailable`). It is **runtime state, never an event** — a full disk could not log "the disk is full", and a persisted state would have to be written when writes work again, which says nothing — so it rides on the WebSocket (§6.3): in the snapshot and in `status` messages. A change of condition is published at once, a change of the counts alone at most once a second; while refusing, the queue is read every 250 ms (held events change without a command). **REST refusals:** a route whose action the storage refuses answers 507 `storage_full`, 503 `storage_unavailable` or 503 `storage_unwritable` with `{error, message}` in the user's words (`STORAGE_ERROR`; `http/storage-refusal.ts`): the agent routes' `attempt` (formerly a 409 for any throw) and `refuse` (formerly a 500), the Harmonizer's restart, and a root Fastify error handler for anything that escapes a route. The WebSocket carries no actions (only `transcript.subscribe`, which writes nothing), so there is nothing to refuse there. The panel shows the server's message on the refused control; the offline answer queue (P7-05) does not treat a storage refusal as a passing 5xx — the answer is refused, not queued (decision 99). A `agent.spawned` lost here used to make the next start treat a running agent as never started (restarted fresh, its spend's session key missing); held, it is written once space returns and a restart resumes the session. A shutdown tries once more and what is still held is lost with the process (the next start treats it as a crash). Transcript chunks whose write fails are dropped (never delivered). Startup recovery also takes agents created but never started (an `agent.created` without `agent.spawned` or an end in the log: killed while the CLI was starting): it logs `agent.interrupted` for them and they start fresh — a brain re-seeded through its runner, a Steward from its saved first prompt — unless a stop was logged for them meanwhile (their own `control.stop`, or a `control.stop` / `control.cancel` of their task or project after their `agent.created`): then `agent.stopped{by}`, as for a live agent with a stop under way. `Workspaces.createWorktree` removes and re-adds a task worktree left locked "initializing" (a `worktree add` the machine went down in). The kill drills run a real child server (`drill-server.ts`) and kill it; `pnpm drills` adds seeded random kills (`GALACTIC_DRILLS`, `GALACTIC_DRILLS_SEED`, `GALACTIC_DRILLS_WINDOW`).

### 6.2 Two streams

- `events` — domain, status, question, proposal and lifecycle events, `message.sent` (what the user, a supervisor or the system sent to an agent — logged before it is queued, so a restart re-delivers what was pending and a brain's mailbox is fed from the log; P1-22) and `message.delivered` (the adapter accepted it; P1-33): small, ID-ordered, resumable, retained for a long time. This is the log that corner case 33 ("reconstruct any state") and the timeline scrubber replay.
- `transcript_chunks(agentId, seq, chunk)` — agent output (text, tool calls, thinking), written in batches (≈10 Hz), pruned by retention policy, redacted for known secret patterns **before** persisting and before sending to the UI. Redaction is boundary-aware (P1-27): the previous chunk's tail is matched together with the new text, a secret's head moves into the new chunk, and the newest chunk of an agent is held for 250 ms so a key split over streamed deltas is caught whole. Since the phase review (P1-31): the pipeline receives the **raw** output and redacts it itself (a chunk redacted on its own loses the context a split key needs); only consecutive chunks of the same kind are joined; after a gap longer than the hold, a key's head is kept only if it was safe on its own and the rest is masked; a private-key block is masked from its `BEGIN … PRIVATE KEY` line to its `END` line whatever the chunking (per-agent state, 16 KB cap); a subscribe backlog holds persisted rows only and subscribers are notified after the write succeeded; an agent's `seq` keeps increasing across restarts: the pipeline numbers from the larger of its newest chunk and the high-water mark retention stored when it removed chunks (P8-02, below).

The status store (§4) and the task/project states are projections over `events`; they are rebuilt on startup and never written back as truth.

**Work states (P2-15).** `WorkStateDeriver` (`runtime/work-state-deriver.ts`) is a log subscriber placed after the status store. Once every event of a commit is folded, it recomputes the tasks and projects the commit concerns with an ordered rule table (`domain/work-states.ts`, SPEC §4.4) and writes any difference through `setTaskState` / `setProjectState` as `*.state_changed` **without `by`**, so clients receive derived states as ordinary row changes and the conformance test still holds. It derives per commit, not per event: a command that sets a task and its project explicitly (a cancel) has both holds recorded before either is re-derived. A task's change re-triggers only its project, a project's change nothing. On restart it rebuilds its tables from the log without writing, then corrects only rows a crash left stale.

**Bodies (P2-02).** The catalogue is seeded by migration 0005 (`domain/catalogue.ts`: `freePlanets`, `freeMoons`, `chooseBody` with decision 25's preferences, returning the occupied planets when none is free; seeded asteroid generator `b-asteroid-n`). The "moved to …" marker is a first-class `Body.movedTo{bodyId, host, at}` column: `body.moved` writes it with the event's `at` (with `intoProjectId` when a standalone task joins a project's body, whose host is unchanged), `body.freed` clears it, and both sides fold it alike. `sweepMarkers(retentionMs)` frees markers by the log's clock (default 7 days until P2-13 reads the setting). **Demo bodies retire (P7-07, `domain/demo.ts`, `bodies.ts` `retireBody`).** `/dev/seed`'s planet and project carry `meta.demo` (`isDemoBody` / `isDemoProject` in `packages/protocol/src/dev.ts`; rows made before the flag are recognised by `/dev/seed`'s fixed shapes: an orbit-less, parent-less, unreserved planet named exactly `Demo planet n`, and a project named `Demo project n` in `/tmp/galactic-demo-n`). The catalogue hands out only catalogued bodies (`isCatalogued` in `@galactic/protocol`, also used by the proposal card's body list: a numeric `meta.orbit`, not demo, not retired), so `freePlanets` / `freeMoons` / `chooseBody` and a card's named body never offer a demo or orbit-less planet; `checkAssignable` refuses a retired body and a demo body for anything but the demo project it hosts. Archiving a project whose body is a demo body frees it and then retires it: `meta.retired: true` and **`body.retired{bodyId}`**. Bodies are never deleted — `projects.body_id` is a foreign key and archived work keeps naming its body (history, inspector) — so the retired row is a tombstone: `EventLog.snapshot()` leaves it out and the web reducer deletes the body on `body.retired`, which keeps the conformance test's "fold == snapshot" true. The timeline replay gets `body.created` … `body.retired` for the project's body (`touches` treats `body.retired` like `body.freed`), so a scrub shows the demo planet while the project lived and not after. A reopened demo project whose planet retired takes a catalogue body (`reopenProject`'s old body is not free when retired).

**Patch rule (P2-20).** `project.updated` / `task.updated` carry `changes` validated by `ProjectChanges` / `TaskChanges` (`packages/protocol`): absent or `undefined` is unchanged, an explicit `null` removes the field — allowed only on `CLEARABLE_PROJECT_FIELDS` (`repoPath`, `parentProjectId`, `permissionPolicy`) and `CLEARABLE_TASK_FIELDS` (`worktreePath`, `branch`), refused elsewhere. The commands and the web reducer apply changes with the same `applyChanges`, so a cleared key is absent on both sides, never stored as `null`; clearing an absent field emits nothing. `agent.updated` has no clearable fields.

**Retention (P2-13).** The retention clock is derived from the event log, not stored: for a project or standalone task it is the later of its last `*.state_changed` into `completed`/`failed` from a non-finished state and its last `*.updated{pinned: false}`, falling back to `updatedAt` for a row created finished or a pruned log (`domain/retention.ts`: `retained`, `sweepRetention`). `runtime/archival.ts` runs it hourly from the composition (once at start; injectable timers; off in tests), archiving with the ordinary `archiveProject` / `archiveTask` by `{type: 'system'}`, so there is no new event and no client fold. The period is `config.retentionMs` (`retentionDays`, `GALACTIC_RETENTION_DAYS`, default 7). A stored `retained_since` would be needed once events are pruned, or for a client countdown.

**Transcript retention (P8-02; notes `docs/tasks/notes/P8-02-retention.md`).** Only `transcript_chunks` are compacted; `events` is never pruned, so resume from `lastEventId`, the timeline replay, the history and the chat's messages are unaffected. Compaction **removes chunks** (no summary is kept: the log already holds the facts — messages, tool calls, states, questions), always an agent's oldest ones, so what is left is a contiguous tail. The policy is the `transcriptRetention` setting (`{maxAgeDays, archivedDays}`, days, `null` = keep; defaults 90 and 30; `config.json`, the settings page, local requests only — decisions 51, 90): an agent whose work is archived (its task, or a project in its chain; the archive time from the last `*.state_changed` into `archived`, else `updatedAt`) loses its whole transcript `archivedDays` after that; any other agent, the Harmonizer included, loses the chunks older than `maxAgeDays`; an agent whose task or any project in its chain is **pinned** is never compacted. `runtime/transcript-retention.ts` (`TranscriptRetention`) runs hourly from the composition (once at start; injectable timers and clock; off in tests), enumerates agents by index seeks on the primary key, and deletes in batches of 500 chunks, one transaction each, awaiting the next event-loop turn between batches; each batch also upserts `transcript_compactions(agent_id, through_seq, through_at, last_seq, removed_at, removed_chunks, reason)` (migration 0011), so a crash mid-run leaves a marker covering what is gone. `last_seq` is the agent's high-water `seq`. Both REST pages report the marker as `compacted{throughSeq, throughAt, removedAt, reason}` (optional): the Steward view says "Older transcript … removed on <date> by retention" above the oldest chunk once nothing older is left to load, and the chat says its replies up to a date were removed (its messages are kept). No event describes a compaction (storage bookkeeping, like `agent_processes`), so the conformance test is unaffected. **Space** (`db/vacuum.ts`): `openDatabase` sets `auto_vacuum = INCREMENTAL` before anything else (it takes effect only before the first table exists); a database made earlier is converted once at startup by a full `VACUUM` (`startupConversion`, before the server listens). In WAL mode the VACUUM writes the whole database through the WAL, so it is skipped (with the sizes logged) unless the disk has 2.2 × the database free (`fs.statfsSync` on its folder); after any attempt the WAL is truncated (`wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`), and `openDatabase` sets `journal_size_limit` to 64 MiB so a big write never leaves a WAL of that size behind. A failure (a full disk) is counted in `settings` (`vacuum_conversion_failures`); after three the conversion is no longer tried at startup and the log says to start once with `GALACTIC_VACUUM_RETRY=1` — freed pages are still reused meanwhile, the file just does not shrink. The retention settings are whole days, at least 1 (or keep forever). After a run that removed chunks, `vacuumIncrementally` releases the free list 256 pages (1 MiB) at a time, yielding between steps; the WAL is left to SQLite's auto-checkpoint. Measured: 404 000 chunks / 213 MB → 71 MB in 1.7 s, longest delete batch 36 ms, typical vacuum step 3–30 ms with occasional 0.1–0.4 s steps where the auto-checkpoint's fsync lands.

Two consequences for the client, decided with P0-18:

- **Domain state is derived twice by design** — the server's commands mutate the tables, the web client's reducer folds the same events — so the two must agree or a resuming client and a snapshotting one would show different worlds. Every command therefore emits events that fully describe its mutation (`body.created` for a new body, `project.created` with the whole project, …), and a **conformance test** (`apps/web/src/state/conformance.test.ts`, P2-01) drives the real commands and runtime through every domain event type and checks that replaying the recorded log through the client reducer equals the server's snapshot after every commit. Status records are exempt: they are server-owned (§4). A row's placement in the hierarchy changes with `agent.updated{changes: supervisorId | bodyId | taskId | projectId}`.
- **The per-project `PermissionPolicy`** (SPEC §3.6, decision 7) is stored in a `permission_policy` JSON column on `projects` (validated by the protocol schema; absent = default policy), added by migration with P2-01 and exposed as `Project.permissionPolicy?`. It is never parked in `meta`. The same migration (0004) adds `pinned` to projects and tasks (decision 11b).

### 6.3 WebSocket protocol (defined in Phase 0, used by every client from Phase 1)

1. Client opens the socket and sends `hello{token, lastEventId?, logId?}` as the **first frame** (browsers cannot set headers on a WebSocket upgrade). `logId` is the identity of the log its state came from.
2. Server replies with `snapshot{logId, seq, agents, bodies, projects, tasks, questions, proposals, statuses, spend?}` when `lastEventId` is absent, `logId` is not this log's (database deleted or replaced), or `lastEventId` is too old to replay; otherwise with the `events` since `lastEventId` followed by `caught_up{seq}`. Both `snapshot` and `caught_up` carry an optional `serverTime` (the server's clock when sent): the client keeps its offset to it, so anything that asks "how old is this event?" (event toasts) uses the server's clock, not the page's (P4-06 review). `spend` (P8-01; optional, an older server sends none) is the per-agent `AgentSpend` the server projects into `agent_spend`; the client folds it on with the same `foldSpend` (`agent.spawned`, `agent.idle`, `agent.finished`, `agent.failed`), and the web's replay conformance test checks the two agree.
3. Thereafter a single ordered stream of `{seq, event}`; the client derives all state from it and remembers `seq` + `logId` for the next reconnect. `seq` is never reused (`AUTOINCREMENT`). Transcript chunks are a separate, subscribable stream per agent.
4. The derived state lives in memory only. A client that has no state (a fresh page load) sends no `lastEventId` and takes a snapshot; resuming from a persisted `seq` onto an empty world would replay only the tail (P0-12). Persisting the world together with its `seq` + `logId` is possible later (a phone PWA), never `seq` alone.
5. **Protocol evolution** (review of P7 wave 2, 2026-10-05). New event types are added without a protocol version bump, so a page loaded from an older build may receive one it cannot parse. Rule: an `event` frame whose envelope is valid (`seq`, `at`, an `event.type` string) but whose event type the build does not know (`unknownEventFrame`, `packages/protocol/src/ws.ts`) is **skipped**: the client keeps the ordering checks (duplicate ignored, gap → resume), advances `seq` past it (and persists it), logs a warning and does not hand it to the store. Anything else that fails to parse — a malformed event of a *known* type, a broken envelope, an unknown message kind — is still an unreadable message (close 4002, resume): that is a server bug, not a newer protocol, and skipping it would silently fork the folded world. Consequences: a page skipping an event it does not know may miss its effect until it reloads (the P7-01 update banner prompts that, and a reload takes a fresh snapshot); and the rule protects builds from this one on — a bundle deployed before it still loops on an unknown event (close 4002, resume from the same `seq`) until the tab is reloaded. New fields on known events and messages must stay optional (zod strips unknown keys, so an added field is already safe). **Every message must stay readable by the previous build's parser**: never drop or stop sending a field the previous build requires, and never make a required field optional and then omit it — the old parser still requires it and refuses the whole message (for a snapshot: every reconnect, close 4002, a loop). To say "this value does not apply", keep sending the old field with a meaningful value and add an optional flag the new build reads (as `StatusRecord.caps.wallClockMs.capped`, P8-10).

Two more message kinds ride on the same socket (P1-05, P1-06): `status{statuses}` carries the status store's batched diffs (≈10 Hz; each record replaces the client's copy for that agent) and follows a replay's `caught_up` with the full current set, since records cannot be derived from the replayed events. `status.storage` and `snapshot.storage` (P8-09, optional) carry the database's write health (§6.1); the client keeps the latest (`ok` when a snapshot has none). A change of storage goes out as a **replacing** status message — the complete record set, `replace: true`, plus `storage` — never as a new message kind (a page built before it would close on an unknown kind, rule 5) nor as a storage-only status message (such a page's schema refuses a status message with no records, no removals and no `replace`); this build accepts `storage` alone, so a later server may drop the set once no such page is left; `transcript.subscribe{agentId, since?}` / `transcript.unsubscribe{agentId}` open and close the per-agent transcript stream, whose `transcript{agentId, chunks}` batches arrive in per-agent `seq` order (history after `since` first, then live). A client that reconnects re-subscribes with the last `seq` it has. A socket too slow for any of the three streams is cut and resumes on reconnect. A fourth, per-run stream (`run.subscribe` / `run_output`, P9) follows the transcript stream's rules (§9.8).

**Chat history (P2-18).** The console's chat boxes fold `message.sent` from the stream into a per-agent list beside the folded world, not in it (the snapshot carries no messages and the conformance test compares the tables only); after a reload or a snapshot catch-up (the store counts snapshots; a chat box refetches when the count changes) the page backfills from `GET /api/agents/:id/messages` (the log's last 200 `message.sent` of that agent, an indexed read on `events(type, json_extract(payload, '$.agentId'))`, migration 0006), deduplicated by `messageId`. The route returns the text as the stream carries it: user-written text is not redacted (§8), and a Steward's report was redacted when it was made. The promotion flow's start-time scans of the whole log stay (once per boot). The other REST surface the console uses: `POST /api/proposals/:id/decide` (approve / edit / reject / park), `POST /api/questions/:id/answer` with the question's `version` (409 `stale`, "already answered by …"), `POST /api/questions/:id/dismiss` (P9-14, same `version` rule), `POST /api/control`, `POST /api/agents/:id/send`, `GET /api/runtimes`.

**Panel endpoints (P25-01).** All in `http/panel-routes.ts`, typed in `packages/protocol/src/panel.ts`, bearer-guarded:

- `GET /api/agents/:id/conversation?before=&after=&limit=`: `message.sent` to the agent merged with its `text` transcript chunks by (`at`, messages first, `id` / `seq`), paged by an opaque cursor (`<at>~m<id>` / `<at>~r<seq>`) in both directions, newest page by default; entry ids `m:<messageId>` / `r:<agentId>:<seq>` match the stream for dedup. Each source is read through a partial index of migration 0007 — `events(json_extract(payload,'$.agentId'), at) WHERE type = 'message.sent'` and `transcript_chunks(agent_id, at, seq) WHERE json_valid(chunk) AND json_extract(chunk,'$.kind') = 'text'` — with row-value cursor bounds, so a page reads only its rows (no sort of the agent's history, no JSON parse of every chunk). `lastSeq` is the newest *persisted* chunk when the page was read: `transcript.subscribe{since: lastSeq}` continues it and delivers a reply that was still buffered.
- `GET /api/agents/:id/transcript?before=&after=&limit=&kinds=`: chunks by `seq`; `after` and `lastSeq` (persisted, as above) are `transcript.subscribe`'s `since`. Both this and `/conversation` carry `compacted` when retention removed part of the agent's transcript (P8-02, §6.2); `lastSeq` is then at least the stored high-water mark.
- `GET` / `PATCH /api/settings`: `SettingsService` (§2.2); 400 `invalid_body` / `invalid`, 403 `remote_settings` (§8), 409 `env_override`.
- `GET /api/preflight[?refresh=1]`: each adapter's optional `preflight()` (never throws), cached 30 s, concurrent callers share a run, a refresh reruns it at most once per 5 s. The Claude adapter runs `claude --version` and `claude auth status` (JSON, read even on a non-zero exit) with the agents' allow-listed environment (`INHERITED_ENV`) and a 10 s timeout, reports the SDK version and the bundled CLI's (`manifest.json`); without a global `claude` it asks the SDK's bundled binary and reports a warning, not an error. The email and organisation are not passed on.
- `GET /api/notifications?after=&limit=`: the log through the protocol's pure `notificationOf`, which the client also folds over the live stream, so ids match and dedup across reconnects is by id. The scan reads only the notifying shapes (indexed `type` plus the payload fields that decide); a backfilled question that is no longer open is left out; the returned `seq` (the next `after`) is never below the `after` given. "Work interrupted by a restart" keys on `recovery.needs_attention{agentId, reason}`, which `AgentRuntime` logs once per interruption when recovery passes an agent over or cannot resume it, when a resumed session is lost before its first turn with no fresh fallback, or when that fallback fails — never on `agent.interrupted`, which every graceful shutdown and session reset logs.
- `GET /api/history/work?before=&q=&kind=&state=&limit=` and `GET /api/history/questions?before=&q=&kind=&status=&limit=` (P25-10, SPEC decision 61; `runtime/history-view.ts`): finished and archived projects and tasks (`state IN ('completed', 'failed', 'archived')`) and answered or cancelled questions as the domain tables hold them (a cancelled question also gets the `reason` of its last `question.cancelled`). Newest first by (`updated_at`, `id`), paged back by an opaque cursor `<updatedAt>~p|t|q<id>`; work merges the two tables by (`updated_at`, `t` before `p`, `id`), like the conversation endpoint. Each list reads a partial index of migration 0008, `(updated_at, id) WHERE <the list's term>`, named with `INDEXED BY` (SQLite otherwise picked `questions_status` and sorted; a query-plan test guards it), and a cancelled question's reason is read through 0008's `events_type_question` (`events(type, json_extract(payload,'$.questionId'))`); so a page without a search reads only its rows. A search is a `LIKE … ESCAPE '\'` substring match (case-insensitive for ASCII). Text comes back as the stream carries it: agent text was redacted when logged, user text is not (§8). The client renders the stream's current copy of a row's item when it has one. A malformed or foreign cursor (here and on `/conversation`) is a `CursorError`, answered 400 `invalid_query`. **Trails and holder chains (P5-03).** Each work row also carries `bodyName` and a `trail` derived from the log for the whole page in one read: `closedAt` (the last move into `completed`, `failed` or `moved`, while the work is still finished), `archivedAt` (while archived), `reopenedAt` (the last move out of a finished state), `moves` (each `body.moved` of the item, with `intoProjectId` for a task that joined a project, and a project task handed to another project via `task.updated` with a new `projectId` — the `task.updated` and `body.moved` of one command are folded into one move; body and project names resolved by the server), `promotedTo` (the project whose `meta.promotedFrom.taskId` is this task) and `promotedFrom` (a project's `meta.promotedFrom`). Each past question carries `holders`: the holder it was routed to when asked (the first `question.escalated` / `question.reassigned`'s `from`, else the row's holder), then one step per escalation or reassignment (with its reason), from 0008's `events_type_question`. Both fields are optional in the schema, so a client reading an older server still parses. Migration 0009 indexes these reads: `events_work_subject`, a partial expression index on `coalesce($.projectId, $.taskId, $.host.id)` `WHERE type IN ('project.state_changed', 'task.state_changed', 'task.updated', 'body.moved')` (other events pay nothing; the read names it with `INDEXED BY`, since the planner otherwise takes `events_type_question` for the `type IN` term), plus `projects(parent_project_id)`, `projects(json_extract(meta, '$.promotedFrom.taskId'))`, `agents(project_id)`, `agents(task_id)` and `questions(origin_agent_id, updated_at)`. A page's ids are bound as one JSON array (`IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?))`), so one prepared statement serves every page size; a query-plan test checks each statement searches its index and scans no table.
- `GET /api/history/work/:kind/:id` (P5-03, SPEC decision 61; `HistoryView.detail`): one project or task inspected from the history, in any state (a moved-to link may lead to live work). Returns the row, `bodyName`, the trail, every state change oldest first (`{at, from, to, by?}` from its `*.state_changed`), its project (a task's, or a sub-project's parent), a project's tasks and sub-projects, the agents that ran it (a project's: its own by `agents.project_id` plus every agent of its current tasks by `agents.task_id` — a Steward of a standalone task that joined the project may carry no `project_id`; a task's: `agents.task_id`) and the questions those agents asked, newest first, at most 50 (`moreQuestions` when there are more), each with its cancel reason and holder chain. 400 `invalid_query` for a kind other than `project` / `task`, 404 `not_found` for no such row; bearer-guarded; rows as the snapshot holds them (redaction parity, tested).
- `GET /api/history/events?projectId=&from=&to=&after=&limit=` (P4-07; `runtime/history-events.ts`, typed in `packages/protocol/src/replay.ts`): the log's events that touch one project, oldest first, paged forward by `seq` (`{events, seq, hasMore, membership}`; `seq` is the next `after`). Membership is decided from the tables as they are when a read starts — the first page caches the members under the `membership` token (32 slots, 10 min) and later pages that pass it back reuse them, so one read's pages agree; work that has since *left* the project is not a member, so its past there is missing from the replay (known limit): the project and its sub-projects (transitively), their tasks, agents working on them, those agents' questions, the plan cards whose `meta.plan.projectId` is one of them, and the bodies they live on plus every body the log ever assigned them or moved them from or to (`body.assigned` / `body.moved`, read through `events_type`). An exhaustive switch (`touches`) keeps each event type to the right ids (`body.freed` by body, `question.*` by question, `agent.*` by agent…); `REPLAY_SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES` leaves out what draws nothing (`message.*`, `control.*`, `permission.decided`, `recovery.needs_attention`, `agent.processes_stopped`). No server-side snapshot: the client folds from the log's start, because a project's events are a small slice and one fold implementation (the client's, held to the tables by the conformance test) is better than two. `from` / `to` only filter by `at`. Each request reads one rowid window (`id > after AND id <= after + 20 000`, `SEARCH events USING INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`, a query-plan test guards it) and parses only rows of kept types, so a request is bounded and a whole project walks the log once: 1 000 000 events, 5 % of them the project's, read in 51 pages, 0.8 s in all, 19 ms the slowest page — no index added. Rows are returned as the stream sends them (redacted when logged). 404 `not_found` for an unknown project.
- First-run marker (P25-08, SPEC decision 60): `SettingsPatch.setupCompleted: true` stamps `setupCompletedAt` (ISO) in `config.json` once; it is never moved or cleared. `SettingsResponse.setupCompletedAt` reports it, and `ConfigFile` / `ServerConfig` carry it. Only local requests may set it (it is not in the remote-writable list), so a remote client gets 403 `remote_settings`. `SettingsResponse.writable` (`all` | `limited`, from the same `writableFor` rule the PATCH applies; absent from an older server means `all`) tells the client whether it may finish the setup. The web client shows its wizard only for a valid settings reply without the marker and with `writable: 'all'`; a hand-edited marker is read leniently (any non-empty string; reported as ISO) so it never stops the server.

**The chat (P25-03; SPEC decisions 54, 55).** The client keeps its threads in their own store (`apps/web/src/state/conversation.ts`), a REST-filled cache beside the stream-derived `useAppStore`. It merges the history pages with the live `message.sent` and `text` chunks by entry id. The transcript is subscribed after the first page, from its `lastSeq` (the session's `transcriptSince` takes the max of the buffer's last chunk and the open chat's `lastSeq`). After a snapshot catch-up, or when a chat is reopened or the log changes, the thread pages forward from its newest history cursor — or rereads the newest page when it has none (a thread with only live entries); past 20 forward pages it restarts from the newest page, dropping older held lines (paged back in on demand), so it never leaves a hole. `message.sent.turn` marks a brain's turn prompt (`AgentRuntime.send({turn})`, set by the runner); the conversation endpoint's SQL leaves those out (`json_extract(payload,'$.turn') IS NULL`, still served by migration 0007's partial index) and so does the client. Cards are placed in a thread by `createdAt`; a Steward view that has paged back is exempt from the live transcript cap (`MAX_TRANSCRIPT`) until it reads its newest page again; a Steward's question is routed to its supervisor by role and project, not by its `supervisorId`, so a replacement Waykeeper's thread shows it. The Harmonizer's thread also shows a project's clarification the user holds or answered (`reachedUser`).

**Replies (P8-17; SPEC decision 139).** `SendBody.replyTo: ReplyRef {kind: message | reply | question | proposal, id, version?, answerId?}` names the line or card a message replies to (a `messageId`, a conversation reply id `r:<agentId>:<chunkSeq>`, a `questionId`, a `proposalId`).
