The send route resolves it (`runtime/reply-target.ts`): a `message.sent` to that agent that is not a turn prompt (migration 0007's index), a non-blank `text` chunk of its own transcript, or a card its chat shows — `threadShowsQuestion` / `threadShowsProposal` / `questionOwner` in `packages/protocol/src/reply.ts`, the rules the panel's `threadCards` uses too (moved there from the web). Unknown or foreign → 400 `reply_target`; a chunk under the agent's compaction marker → 409 `reply_target_gone`; a slash command with a quote → 400; nothing is sent. The quote is the log's text (never the client's), `redactSecrets`, cut to `REPLY_QUOTE_MAX` (2,000) characters with "…", stored as `message.sent.replyTo: ReplyQuote {kind, id, sender, text, answered?}` and carried on `ChatMessage` and `ConversationEntry` — an optional field in the event's JSON payload, so no migration (no table or projection stores it; replay skips `message.sent`). A clarification still open, or one the client saw open (it sent `version`), is answered first through `DomainCommands.answerQuestion` as the user, with `version`/`answerId` (the adapter path when a live session waits on it); a refusal (409 `stale`…) returns before anything is logged, and so does a chat agent that could not take the message (`AgentRuntime.sendRefusal`: not running, stopping, ended — 409 `rejected`, checked before the answer; only a stop that starts during the adapter's answer can still leave an answer without its message, which the retry below completes); then `message.sent` with `replyTo.answered: true`, after its `question.answered`. The send is idempotent on `answerId` (P8-17 review): a retry whose answer is already recorded under it returns the chat's message that answered the question (`answeredReplyOf`) instead of logging a second one, or sends it when the first try's message failed; the panel keeps sending `version`/`answerId` for a clarification its draft answered (`meta.answerId` on the row). Two tries in flight at once may still both log the message (the known limit of the in-flight case). That answer carries `question.answered.repliedIn: <agentId>` (`AnswerQuestionInput.repliedIn`, passed down to `AgentRuntime.answer`): the `question.answered` wake rule skips the brain whose chat it was, so it reads one line, the message's — `the user answered q-… (<whose> question q-…: "<quote>") by replying: "<text>"`; other holders hear the answer as before. Otherwise the line is `the user said (replying to <your reply | your message | their own earlier message | agent X's message | agent X's question q-… | agent X's permission request q-… — this does not decide the request: only the user's buttons on its card do | your proposal p-…>: "<quote, ≤ 1,000 chars>"): "<text>"`. A permission request is never answered by a reply. An agent without a mailbox (a Steward, a free-form agent) gets `(replying to <who>: "<quote>")` before the text in its inbox — also when a restart redelivers it; the logged text is the user's. Supersession (decision 135) runs after the send as for any message. In the web, `panel/chat/Reply.tsx` holds the Reply button (`.pnl-reply`: opacity 0 until hover/focus-within with a fine pointer, always shown and ≥ 44 px under `(pointer: coarse), (hover: none)`), the composer's quote bar (the draft in `useConversationStore.replies`, beside `drafts`; Esc cancels after the slash list's own Esc) and the quote block; the jump finds `[data-entry]` / `[data-card]` in the thread, else pages back (`loadOlder`, at most 40 pages, one at a time; a page that fails or moves no cursor ends the search, and a failed history is not called "no longer in the history"; the search stops when the chat unmounts, and the highlight timer is cleared) until found or no older page, then `scrollIntoView` (smooth unless `prefers-reduced-motion`), focuses (lines and cards are `tabIndex=-1`) and highlights it for 2 s, or shows "The original is no longer in the history".

**Shared cards (P25-06).** Question and proposal cards are store-driven components keyed by id (`QuestionView`, `ProposalView` in `apps/web/src/panel/cards/`), rendered by the inbox, the chat thread and the ops console over `world.questions` / `world.proposals`, so an answer anywhere resolves everywhere with no client-side coordination: `question.answered` (or the proposal's decision) on the stream is the only signal. The Steward view renders permission questions through the same `QuestionCard` and its clarification box with the shared stale line and banner. Every card needs an `ApiContext`; the panel also provides `CardLinksContext` so agent names carry an "open agent" link.

**Desktop notifications (P25-07; SPEC decision 59).** In `apps/web/src/panel/notifications/`. `createNotifier` is wired in `startSession`, next to the store, and folds each message after the store applies it, through the protocol's `notificationOf` with lookups into the client world (the same function as `GET /api/notifications`, so ids match). A per-browser ledger in `localStorage` (`galactic.notifications.ledger`: `logId`, last `seq`, the last 500 ids) dedupes by `seq` and by id across reloads and tabs; the `Notification` `tag` is the id. A snapshot of a log the ledger has not seen sets the baseline and backfills nothing; a snapshot of the same log past the ledger's `seq` backfills `GET /api/notifications?after=` (at most 10 pages of 100, items at most 24 h old, 5 shown plus one summary). A tab decides on the current stored preferences (re-read at decision time, `storage` events) before it claims an id, so a tab with stale preferences cannot swallow a notification; once a ledger write throws (quota, private mode) the ledger lives in memory for that page. Dedup between tabs open at the same moment is best effort; the shared `tag` collapses a duplicate in the OS. Preferences (`galactic.notifications.prefs`: `enabled`, per-kind booleans) are per device and never sent to the server. `Notification.requestPermission` is called only from the Enable button's click.

**Settings page and first-run wizard (P25-08; SPEC decisions 51, 60).** In `apps/web/src/panel/settings/`. The page and the wizard share one `SettingsProvider` per panel and one spec per setting (editor, client check, patch), so both validate and word refusals the same way; the server stays the authority. `ApiError` carries the error body, which holds the zod `issues` of a 400.

**Managed agents, controls strip and project rows (P4-09; SPEC decision 69).** The side column's managed-agents section can be hidden per browser: `useManagedAgentsVisibility` (`apps/web/src/panel/managed-agents-visibility.ts`) keeps the choice in `localStorage['galactic.panel.managedAgents.visible']` (`'0'` hidden, anything else shown), every access in try/catch like `galactic.scene.visible`. Hidden means unmounted; the grid gets `pnl-grid-no-agents` and reflows, the inbox always stays. The control actions are shared UI in `apps/web/src/panel/controls.tsx` (`useControls`: `POST /api/control`, the inline confirmation of the destructive ones, the edit form, the refusal as an alert): the cards use it, and so does the **controls strip**, shown whether or not the cards are, which keeps the selected agent's own controls in the main area: in a Waykeeper's header its project's work actions and its agent actions, in a Steward's view its task's work actions beside the view's agent controls. Pause / resume are inline, the rest in a "More" menu. A project row's Steward count (`stewardCount` in `nav-model.ts`) counts by task: the project's own non-archived tasks that have had a Steward (total), and those whose newest Steward (`primaryAgent`) is live and not paused (active), shown "N active, M in total". Phone-menu items that close the menu after acting carry `data-closes-menu`.

**Hidable inbox, closed work and row actions (P4-12).** The inbox hides per browser like the managed agents, in `localStorage['galactic.panel.inbox.visible']` (`'0'` hidden, try/catch). Its state is a small module store (`apps/web/src/panel/inbox-visibility.ts`, `useSyncExternalStore`; cached only while something is subscribed, so a fresh mount reads storage again) rather than component state, because `revealInbox` / `revealQuestion` (`inbox-reveal.ts`; the top bar's "N waiting for you", a button in both bars, and the scene's beacons) are not React: they call `showInbox()` first, then look for the inbox or the card over a few frames as before. The grid gets `pnl-grid-no-inbox` with only the managed agents left; with both sections hidden the side column is not rendered and the grid is `pnl-grid-no-side` (`nav | centre`; stacked: scene, then the navigation full width). The wide bar offers "Show inbox" only while it is hidden; the phone menu always has the toggle (`data-closes-menu`). The navigation (`buildNav`) builds the whole tree, then prunes **closed** items — `completed` and `archived` (`CLOSED` in `nav-model.ts`; `failed` is not closed) — with everything nested in them, unless "Show closed" (`localStorage['galactic.panel.nav.showClosed']`, `'1'` shown) is on; the item holding the selected agent survives the pruning with its path (a deep link); `hiddenClosed` counts the pruned roots for the toggle's "(N hidden)". Closed rows carry `is-closed` (dimmed). Each row has a "⋯" button (shown on hover/focus, always on `(hover: none)`) that opens `NavRowActions` (`nav-row-actions.tsx`) inline under the row — inline, not a popover, because the navigation is a narrow scroll container: `workActions` minus edit through `useControls` (`POST /api/control` on the row's task or project, the same confirmations, the server's refusal as an alert), plus a note when a project has unfinished tasks (the server archives a project only when its tasks are finished; closing leaves them as they are, cancelling marks them failed). The Steward count's option is now `showClosed` (archived tasks still drop out of the total without it; completed ones count).

**Panel layout and the docked conversation (P4-10).** The shell (`.pnl`) is exactly the viewport (`height: 100dvh`, `overflow: hidden`): the top bar, then `.pnl-body` (`flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0; overflow-y: auto`, where the settings, history and first-run pages scroll), then the grid (`flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0`, one `minmax(0, 1fr)` row — no grid row has a fixed length). The page never scrolls; every column does. Every scroll container is `position: relative`, so an absolutely positioned descendant (a visually hidden label) clips and scrolls with its column instead of stretching the document from its static position. Above `STACKED_LAYOUT_QUERY` (`(max-width: 1100px)`, in `use-media-query.ts`) the grid is `nav | centre | side`; the centre (`.pnl-centre`, a flex column) holds the scene and the main area, which is the **dock** (`<main class="pnl-main pnl-dock">`): a bar (title, state, "N new" replies counted from the live transcript while minimised, the status record's current activity — not a typing indicator —, the Waykeeper's controls strip, a minimise/restore button with `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`), a `role="separator"` handle on its top edge (pointer drag, arrow keys ±5, Page Up/Down ±10, Home/End; `aria-valuenow` in percent), and the body. The dock **splits** the centre with the scene (its height is `max(--pnl-dock-size, min(160px, 75%))`: a percentage of the centre, clamped 25–75, with a 160 px floor where that fits). A drag writes `--pnl-dock-size` and `aria-valuenow` straight onto the elements on each pointer move and sets React state once, when it ends; the scene area, the navigation and the side column are memoised, and the R3F `Canvas` debounces its resize (`resize={{ debounce: { resize: 50, scroll: 50 } }}`), so a drag neither re-renders the panel nor resizes the renderer and composer on every frame. Minimised, only the bar is left: the body stays mounted and laid out, collapsed (`height: 0; visibility: hidden`, never `display: none`, which drops a scroller's position) and `inert` + `aria-hidden`, so the thread, its scroll position (the thread also restores the last position the user left it at when its box regains height) and its subscription survive. Composer drafts live in the conversation store per agent (`drafts`, cleared on send), so switching agents or remounting the chat keeps them. The landmark is named after who it is about ("Conversation with Harmonizer", "Steward · …"). The Waykeeper controls strip's "More" menu measures where it fits when it opens: below its row unless there is less room below than its height and more above (a minimised bar at the bottom, the stacked layout), capped to that room and shifted sideways to stay on screen (`placeMenu` in `controls.tsx`, `data-placement`). The scene's overlays are children of `.pnl-scene`, so they stay in the scene's (smaller) box. In list mode on a wide screen the dock is the whole centre and does not minimise. At or below 1100 px React renders a different tree: one scrolling `.pnl-stack` (scene, navigation, side column) above the dock, which minimises there in list mode too (its bar sits above the bottom safe area the shell pads for); crossing the breakpoint remounts the scene and the chat (a draft survives, being in the store). Open/minimised and the height are per browser in `localStorage['galactic.panel.dock']` (`{open, size}`, read and written in try/catch, validated and clamped on read; a drag is saved when it ends). The chat thread is `flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0` inside the dock (the old `max-height: calc(100vh - 290px)` assumed a full-height column and pushed the composer out of sight in a shorter one), and keeps to the bottom when its box changes height (a `ResizeObserver`), as when the dock is resized or restored.

A phone that slept reconnects with its last `seq` and catches up instead of reloading everything; answers given offline are sent with the question's version so an "already answered by …" reply is possible.

**Offline answers (P7-05; corner case 28; notes `docs/tasks/notes/P7-05-offline.md`).** Every answer the panel gives (inbox, chat thread, Steward composer, permission cards) goes through `apps/web/src/client/answer-queue.ts` with the question's `version` and a fresh `answerId` (`AnswerBody.answerId`, the idempotency key). Online it is sent at once; while the stream is `disconnected` / `connecting` / `catching_up`, when `navigator.onLine` is false, or when the send fails without a reply (a network error, a proxy's non-JSON page) or with a passing failure (5xx, 408, 429 — not a decision about the answer), it is queued in `localStorage['galactic.answers.queue']` (`{v: 1, scope, entries}`: answer text, question id, version and text, state; `scope` is an FNV-1a fingerprint of the token, never the token) and sent when the stream goes `live` again — after the snapshot or `caught_up` was applied — or on the window's `online` event, oldest first, one at a time; a network failure or a passing 5xx/408/429 retries after 5 s with the same `answerId`. A session with another token clears the queue when it goes live; forgetting the token clears it. One queue per browser: tabs read each other's changes (`storage`) and a live tab sends what an offline one queued (idempotent); another tab's `sending` entry is left to that tab (only a page load turns a stored `sending` into `queued`, recovering what a closed tab left mid-send). The server: `question.answered.answerId?` is folded into the row as `meta.answerId` (no migration; the client reducer does the same, so the conformance tables agree); an answer whose `answerId` is already recorded is `200 {ok, duplicate: true}` with nothing logged, and a retry while the first copy is still being handed to the adapter joins it (`Commands.answering`). Any other answer to a closed or changed question is refused as before (409 `stale`, "already answered by …"; a withdrawn one 409 `rejected`). The page words the outcome from the folded world: "sent", or "not sent: already answered by you on another device or tab / by <supervisor> (its supervisor, from context) / by the permission policy", "the question changed while you were offline … answer it again" (with Send again at the new version), "withdrawn", "no longer exists". Card decisions are not queued: proposals have no version to detect a stale view and an approval starts work, so while the stream is down the cards disable their decision buttons and say so. The service worker is not involved.

### 6.4 Backups, export and import (P8-03)

Procedures in `docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md`; notes and measurements in `docs/tasks/notes/P8-03-backups.md`.

**Backups (`db/backup.ts`, `BackupService`).** A backup is a full copy of the database through SQLite's online backup API (better-sqlite3 `db.backup`, 1024 pages per step, awaiting the event loop between steps; writes made meanwhile through the server's own connection are carried into the copy). It is written as `<name>.partial`, created 0600 before SQLite opens it in a 0700 `<dataDir>/backups/`, switched to `journal_mode = DELETE` so the backup is one file, checked with `PRAGMA quick_check`, then renamed to `galactic-<UTC yyyymmddThhmmssmmmZ>-<auto|manual|pre-import>.sqlite`. A failure removes the partial file, and each backup first removes any `*.partial*` left in the folder by a backup the process died in (only one backup runs at a time; the CLI's pre-import copies run under the data lock). The shutdown awaits a backup under way (`BackupService.idle()`) before the process exits. The list, the newest automatic backup and each backup's time come from the file names, so nothing is stored in the database and a restart keeps the schedule. **Schedule:** the composition (`index.ts`) starts a 15-minute check once the server listens. The daily backup is due 24 h after the newest `auto` file and starts when the newest event is at least 2 min old, or regardless once it is 6 h overdue. It is skipped, logged once per episode, when `statfs` free space (the P8-02 `diskFree`) is below the database's size plus `reserveBytes` (default 1 GiB): a backup must never fill the disk under the live database (P8-04). **Rotation** after each backup (`backupsToRemove`): the newest `auto` of each of the last `keepDaily` days (7) and of each of the last `keepWeekly` ISO weeks (4, UTC), and the newest `keepManual` (5) `manual` and `pre-import` each; whatever the numbers (0 included), the newest backup of each kind is kept, so the copy just taken and the only `pre-import` copy are never deleted. **Settings:** `config.json` `backups {enabled, keepDaily, keepWeekly, keepManual, reserveMiB}`, `GALACTIC_BACKUPS=0|1`; not editable from the panel. **REST** (`http/backup-routes.ts`): `GET /api/backups` (`BackupsResponse`: files, folder, rotation, `running`, `last` outcome), `POST /api/backups` ("Back up now"; concurrent calls share one backup) and `GET /api/export?transcripts=1` (an attachment, `no-store`). All three are local only unless `autoApproveRemote` (403 `remote_control`; SPEC decision 90). The settings page's Backups section (`panel/settings/Backups.tsx`) lists them, offers "Back up now" and "Export the map", and tells a remote client to use the Mac. Measured on a 221 MB database: the backup takes 1.5 s in all, the longest event-loop block is about 0.1 s (the `quick_check`), and copy steps take ≤ 50 ms. `quick_check` is one synchronous pass over the copy (≈ 50 ms for 96 MiB, measured on an M-series Mac with the file cached); at several GiB it would need a worker thread.

**Export (`db/map-export.ts`, `MapExport` in `packages/protocol/src/backup.ts`).** It is one JSON document `{format: 'galactic-map-export', formatVersion: 1, exportedAt, app, schema {version, migrations[{version, name}]}, source {logId, seq}, includes {transcripts}, events[{seq, at, event}], rows {bodies, projects, tasks, agents, questions, proposals}, transcripts? {chunks, compactions}}`, read in one transaction. The event log is exported whole, because everything else is a projection over it (§6.1). Event payloads are kept as written, not re-validated, so an old log stays importable. `rows` are `Table.all()` (retired demo bodies' tombstones included) and serve only as the import's check. Never exported: `settings` (log id, vacuum counters), `agent_processes`, `push_subscriptions`, the config file, the token, VAPID keys and runtime sessions. The export refuses a database this build has not migrated, or one another build migrated (the same name check as the runner). `galactic export` opens the database read-only, so it runs beside a live server, and writes the file 0600. The export takes 3 ms for 600 events. **`GET /api/export` streams** (`exportMapText`, P8 wave 2 review): it opens its own read-only connection on the database file and holds one read transaction on it (a WAL snapshot: events, rows and transcripts of one moment while the server writes through its own connection), runs the schema check before answering (409 `export_refused`), then writes the head and rows, the events and transcript chunks 2 000 at a time (each event's payload as stored, not parsed and re-stringified), yielding to the event loop between pages. A large export therefore neither blocks the server for the whole read and stringify (formerly ≈ 0.55 s for 200 MB of transcripts) nor sits in memory whole. An in-memory database (tests) falls back to `exportMap`. The CLI still builds the document whole (it is its own process).

**Import and rebuild (`db/map-import.ts`).** The world fold moved from the web client into `packages/protocol/src/world.ts` (`applyEvent`, `emptyWorld`, `WorldState`; `apps/web/src/state/reduce.ts` re-exports it). The import therefore rebuilds with the very function whose agreement with the server's commands the conformance test checks (§6.2 "Domain state is derived twice"). `importMap(dbPath, file, {replace, preferRows})` works as follows:

- It checks the format and that every exported migration exists here under the same name: a newer build's or another branch's export is refused.
- It refuses a target holding events, work, agents or transcripts unless `replace`. With `replace` it first copies the target into `backups/` (`pre-import`) and carries its `push_subscriptions` over (`ATTACH`).
- It builds a fresh database at `<db>.import-<pid>` (migrations seed the catalogue). In one transaction with `defer_foreign_keys`, it inserts the events with their `seq`, `at` and payload, then folds them from the seeded bodies (`foldRows`). A `body.retired` keeps the body's tombstone with `meta.retired`, as `retireBody` does, and an unknown event type folds to nothing. It compares the result with the exported rows field by field (`diffRows`), and refuses with the differences (`ImportError('drift')`), writing nothing, unless `preferRows` writes the exported rows instead. It writes the rows in the export's order, so `rowid` order (snapshot order) matches, and the transcripts if present.
- Then `new EventLog(db)` creates a **new log id** and folds the budget projections (`agent_spend`, `budget_notices`) and the run history (`deliverable_runs`, P9-01) from the log. It runs `foreign_key_check` and `quick_check`, checkpoints, closes, and renames over the target (stale `-wal`/`-shm` removed first), mode 0600.
- Status records and work states are rebuilt from the log at the next start, as after any restart. Agents live at the export are resumed or flagged by startup recovery (decision 8).

**The data lock (`db/data-lock.ts`, P8 wave 2 review).** `<db>.lock` (`galactic.sqlite.lock`), created with `O_EXCL`, holds `{pid, role: server|import|restore, processStart (ps lstart, ms), bootAt, since, port?, host?}`. `index.ts` takes it (role `server`) before it opens the database and refuses to start, exit 1, while a live holder has it (two servers on one database); it records the port and host once it listens and removes the lock on exit (`process.on('exit')`, only if the file is still its own). `galactic import` / `restore` refuse while a live holder has it — whatever port (`galactic start --port` sets the port only in the child's environment), address, or a server still in its startup vacuum or recovery before it listens — then keep `GET /health` on the configured port as a second check, and hold the lock themselves while they replace the file, so no server starts under them. A lock is **stale** — taken over silently — when its pid is not alive, or the process with that pid started more than `START_TIME_TOLERANCE_MS` from `processStart` (a recycled pid), or it was written in another boot, or it cannot be parsed. Both the server start and the CLI remove `<db>.import-<pid>` / `<db>.restore-<pid>` (and `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal`) whose pid is not alive (`removeStaleTempDatabases`), and the CLI also `backups/*.partial*`. **Restore (`db/restore.ts`, `galactic restore <file|name>`):** it runs `quick_check` on the backup and `checkSchema(…, {allowOlder: true})` (from `map-export.ts`): a backup with a migration this build does not know, or one of the same number under another name, is refused; an older one is migrated at the next start. It copies the target into `backups/` unless empty, copies the backup in with the backup API, writes a **new `log_id`** and switches the copy back to WAL. The new id matters because the restored log's later `seq`s will be reissued for different events, so a client resuming with its old `lastEventId` would skip them. The acceptance test (`db/map-export.test.ts`) runs export → wipe → import on a log covering cards, sub-projects, moves, archives, a retired demo body, questions, agents and spend. Afterwards the snapshot (log id aside), the log row by row, the status records rebuilt from it, the history and inspector outputs, the timeline replay and the chats (with `--transcripts`) are all equal, and `logDrift` (P8-04) reports nothing.

---

## 7. Repository layout (pnpm monorepo, kept small)

```
galactic-agents/
  apps/
    server/        Fastify + ws + SQLite + agent runtime
      src/adapters/   claude-sdk/, fake/, (later) acp/, pty/
      src/brains/     Harmonizer & Waykeeper prompts, tool schemas, handlers
      src/domain/     commands, projections, status store
      src/deliverables/ deliverables: paths, detection, copies, file serving, opener, runs (P9)
    web/           Vite + React + R3F (WebGL), PWA
  packages/
    protocol/      zod schemas shared by server and web: domain model, events,
                   the AgentAdapter interface, the DomainCommands interface
  e2e/             Playwright browser smoke tests (P8-05): test-only, no runtime code
  docs/            SPEC.md, TECHNOLOGY.md, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md
```

**Browser smoke tests (P8-05).** `e2e/` is a test-only workspace (`@galactic/e2e`, no `test` script, so `pnpm test` skips it; run with `pnpm e2e`, which builds first). It starts the real server from the production build (`apps/server/dist`, serving `apps/web/dist`) in development mode with `GALACTIC_HARMONIZER=fake`, a fresh `GALACTIC_HOME` and projects root under the system temp directory and `GALACTIC_PORT=0` (the OS picks a free port); the world is made with the scene harness (`/dev/harness/*`, decision 79). Playwright's pinned Chromium (`pnpm e2e:install`, or `E2E_CHANNEL=chrome`) renders WebGL on SwiftShader on every machine, so the scene's baselines (canvas only, DOM overlays made transparent) do not depend on the GPU or the fonts. The scene is asserted through a hidden test hook (`scene/probe.tsx`: `data-bodies`, `data-satellites`, `data-camera`) rather than pixels where possible. Details: `docs/tasks/notes/P8-05-smoke.md`.

Three workspaces instead of five (plus the test-only `e2e/`, P8-05): adapters and brains live inside the server until a second consumer exists (extraction is cheap; a `server ↔ brains` dependency cycle is not). `packages/protocol` is the **single vocabulary**: `Body{id, kind: star|planet|moon|asteroid, parentId?, host?: {kind: project|task, id}, movedTo?: {bodyId, host, at}, meta}` (catalogue data in `meta`: `reserved`, `orbit`, an asteroid's `number` / `seed`), `Project{…, workspace, repoPath?, parentProjectId?, budgetUsd?}`, `Task{…, projectId?, bodyId, worktreePath?, branch?, blockedBy[], budgetUsd?}`, `Agent{…, role, runtime, sessionRef?, supervisorId?, bodyId, taskId?, projectId?, caps}`, `Proposal`, `Question` (§5.3, with a `version` bumped on every holder change or answer), `StatusRecord` (§4), the `agent.*` / `question.*` / `permission.decided` (the policy decided without the user; no question row) / `message.sent.origin?` (`RequestOrigin`: local or remote, P2-21) / `message.sent.turn?` and `ChatMessage.turn?` (a brain's turn prompt, left out of the conversation, P25-03) / `recovery.needs_attention{agentId, reason}` (recovery left the agent interrupted for the user, P25-01) / `proposal.unparked` (a freed body handed to a parked card, P2-09) / `promotion.carried_out{proposalId, outcome}` (folded into `Proposal.meta.carriedOut`, P2-12) / `proposal.*` / `project.*` / `task.*` / `body.*` (`created` for a body that was not in the seeded catalogue — a procedural asteroid — then `assigned` / `moved` (with `intoProjectId` when a standalone task joins a project's body; the old body gets the "moved to …" marker) / `freed` (clears host and marker)) / `budget.set` / `budget.warning` / `budget.exceeded` / `budget.spawn_refused` (P8-01) / `control.*` / `deliverable.*` and `run.*` (P9, §9.1: `Deliverable`, `RunSummary`, `deliverables.ts`) events. Budgets (P8-01, `budget.ts`): `BudgetUsd`, `BudgetTarget{kind: project|task, id}`, `AgentSpend` and `foldSpend`, `budgetUsage`, `formatUsd`, `DEFAULT_BUDGET_WARN_PERCENT`, `Snapshot.spend?`, `DomainCommands.setBudget`, `Settings.budgets?{warnAtPercent}`; the fake adapter has a `cost` step for tests.

---

## 8. Security

- The server can start processes on your machine. It must require a bearer token even on localhost, sent in the first WebSocket frame (§6.3) or an `Authorization` header for REST, and it must bind to `127.0.0.1` by default; Tailscale Serve terminates TLS and forwards to it.
- **`Host` allow-list** (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, the `*.ts.net` name) and an **`Origin` check** on every WebSocket upgrade, so a web page in the same browser cannot reach the server via DNS rebinding or cross-origin sockets.
- The token is printed once by `galactic start` and pasted, or scanned as a QR code, into the phone PWA (P7-02). The settings page draws the QR code in the browser (`uqr`) after its own reveal. The code holds `https://<remoteHost>/#token=…`: the token sits in the fragment, which no browser sends, so it reaches no request log, and the page takes it and strips it from the address bar and history (`takeTokenFromFragment`). It is saved only once a session with it goes live (`confirmToken`); a refused one falls back to the token saved before (`refuseToken`), so a stale QR code never replaces a working token. The browser's global history or synced tabs may still have recorded the `#token=` address (`docs/REMOTE_ACCESS.md` §2). `IDENTITY_TOKEN` (`'tailscale-identity'`, in `packages/protocol`) is refused as a configured token (`config.json`, `GALACTIC_TOKEN`) and never taken from a fragment. The `Host` allow-list takes the exact Serve name from `remoteHost` (`config.json` or `GALACTIC_REMOTE_HOST`; never a wildcard). A `*.ts.net` wildcard in `allowedHosts` still works but is warned about at startup, because it also admits every Funnel page's `Origin`. **Tailscale identity headers** can replace the token (`tailscaleIdentityLogins`, off by default). `tailscaleIdentityOf` accepts a listed `Tailscale-User-Login` only over a loopback connection whose `Host` is exactly `remoteHost`, whose `Origin` (if any) is `https://<remoteHost>`, and which carries no `Tailscale-Funnel-Request`. Serve deletes client-sent identity headers before setting its own, so a tailnet device cannot forge one. A local process can, though — but it can already ask `/health` for the token, so the opt-in adds no new local exposure: every process on the Mac is trusted either way (trust model in `docs/REMOTE_ACCESS.md` §4; SPEC §8 2026-10-05). An identity-authenticated request passes the bearer guard and the WebSocket `hello` whatever its token. It is still remote, and `/health` never gives it the token: it reports `identity` and `origin` instead. The client then connects with a stand-in token (`IDENTITY_TOKEN`) that is never stored. `GET /health` hands it out only to a request `isRemoteRequest` calls local: from loopback, with a loopback `Host`, no forwarding or Tailscale headers, and an `Origin` that is absent or a loopback name. A proxy on this machine (Tailscale Serve, `vite preview --host`) connects from loopback too, and a plain GET or curl through it sends no `Origin`, so the remote `Host` is what withholds the token (P3-08 review: the Origin check alone handed it to any LAN client). It also requires `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` (the page's own `fetch`) or `none` (a typed navigation): browsers set it and no script in a page can change it, while `curl` and most scripts do not send it by default (`isSameOriginFetch`, P7-02 review). A speed bump for an agent's casual `curl`, not a boundary — any local process can add the header. The Vite dev proxy passes the header through. **Only HTTP-mode Serve may front the port** (`tailscale serve --https=443 http://127.0.0.1:<port>`): it always adds `X-Forwarded-For`, the load-bearing signal of `isRemoteRequest`; a TCP Serve, `ssh -L/-R`, an IDE's port forwarding or any tunnel that adds no proxy header makes its users local. `loadConfig` warns about it whenever `remoteHost` is set.
- Development-only routes (`/dev/*`) exist only with `GALACTIC_DEV=1`; they are bearer-guarded and refused to remote clients (403 `remote_dev`, `isRemoteRequest`: like a settings write, a development tool belongs to this machine, P4-06); they never write domain rows without the matching events, so a replaying client and a snapshotting one always agree. The scene harness (`/dev/harness/*`, P4-06) writes only through `Commands` and the runtime's agent events, flags what it creates `meta.harness`, refuses anything else (409 `not_harness`), and its "clear" stops its fake agents and archives its tasks and projects, never touching real ones. "Clear demo projects" (`POST /dev/demo/clear`, P7-07, `runtime/demo-clear.ts`, the ops console's status bar) closes or cancels and archives every non-archived demo project (`isDemoProject`, never a `meta.harness` row) and its tasks through `Commands` — archiving retires each demo planet — then retires every free demo body left over from earlier archives (`retireFreeDemoBodies`); a demo body still hosting real work, or a step a domain rule refuses (a running agent), is listed in `skipped`.
- **Agent processes get an allow-listed environment** (`INHERITED_ENV` in the supervisor: PATH, HOME, locale, proxies, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, …) plus what the spawn passes explicitly; the server's own variables — its token, any API key — never reach an agent unless a spec hands them over (bare mode does, for its key).
- **REST authentication runs on the decoded route** (P1-28): the `/api` and `/dev` routes live in encapsulated Fastify scopes whose `onRequest` hook checks the bearer token, so no spelling of the path (`/%61pi/…`) reaches a handler without it.
- Permission requests from agents follow the per-project policy table (SPEC §3.6); anything not auto-allowed is surfaced to the user and never auto-approved for a remote client unless explicitly configured. **The policy engine is lexical, a guard rail rather than a sandbox** (P1-30): `domain/permissions.ts` lexes a subset of bash (quotes removed as the shell does, `&`/`;`/`|`/newlines, glued and fd-prefixed redirections, command substitutions classified as commands, heredoc bodies as data, brace expansion capped at 64 alternatives); a command name or git subcommand built at run time, `$'…'` quoting, an interpreter reading from stdin or a heredoc, and `git -c` keys git executes are opaque. An opaque command — like an unknown tool — is *unclassified* (`Classification.unclassified`): it gets the policy's `unclassified` decision (absent or unknown = `ask`; `allow` only in the `trusted` preset, SPEC decision 49) on top of the categories the engine still saw in it, and its raw text is searched for a push (`mentionsPush`: `git` followed anywhere by the word `push`, also with quotes and backslashes removed, plus the push rules), so a trusted project still asks before `sh -c "git push"` or `python - <<EOF … subprocess.run(['git','push'])`. By design it does not follow symlinks and does not see what a script written in one call does when run in the next (`Write x.sh` → `bash x.sh`, `npm test` after editing `package.json`); an OS-level sandbox (Claude Code's sandbox, seatbelt) is the real boundary if one is needed. The gate never throws (an exception is a `deny`), an invalid policy makes every action `ask`, and a cwd outside the workspace (or a workspace of `/`) makes every call `ask`.
- **"Allow similar" rules (P9-17, SPEC decision 138).** A project's rules live on its row (`Project.permissionRules`, column `permission_rules`, migration 0015), changed only by `permission.rule_added` / `permission.rule_removed` (never by a project update: `ProjectChanges` omits the field) and folded alike by the web client. `domain/permission-rules.ts` is pure apart from an injectable real-path lookup: `similarOptions` drafts the three scopes from a request (`PermissionRule.match`: `text` — a command as written, a file tool's real paths, a web address, stable JSON of other input; `words` — a command prefix as the lexer's dequoted words; `folder` — a real path; `categories`), with a preview line each; `matchingRule` finds the first rule that allows a call. The gate (`permission-gate.ts`, option `rules`, read on every check like the table) consults the rules only when the table's verdict is `ask` and the policy parsed: a rule never overrides `deny`, never matches a call whose classification has `push` or `guarded` (a new `Classification` flag set by `guardedWriteReason` for every edit path and every command write target — write redirections, and every argument of a command that is not read-only — **wherever it is** (P9-17 review B1): a `.claude`, `.git` or `.husky` segment or a `.mcp.json`/`.claude.json` name below the cwd or the workspace, or anywhere on the absolute path outside them; the deliverables folder; the shell start-up files `~/.zshrc`, `~/.zprofile`, `~/.zshenv`, `~/.zlogin`, `~/.bashrc`, `~/.bash_profile`, `~/.bash_login`, `~/.profile`; `~/Library/LaunchAgents`, `/Library/LaunchAgents`, `/Library/LaunchDaemons`; `~/.ssh`; `~/.gitconfig` and `~/.config/git`; someone else's `~user` — case-folded, `$HOME` read as `~`), never an unclassified call unless the rule is an exact one made from an unclassified call; and every category the table asks about in the call must be among the rule's `categories` (those it asked about when the rule was made). An edit folder rule also never matches when a call's **physical** path is guarded (a symlink inside the folder that leads into `.git/`). **Outside the workspace** (review B2): when the categories the table asks about include `outside_workspace`, only an exact rule or a folder rule (reading and writing apart) matches, and the chooser offers neither a prefix nor the category — a prefix or category made from `cat /tmp/x` or `git -C / status` would cover the whole computer; `allow-similar` refuses those scopes (400) since it drafts from the same options. **Shells and interpreters** (review S3): no prefix is made or matched for a command that runs whatever code it is given (`prefixRefusal`: `sh`, `bash`, `zsh`, `fish`, …, `python*`, `pypy*`, `node`, `deno`, `bun`, `perl`, `ruby`, `php`, `lua`, `osascript`, `tclsh`, `source`, `.`, `eval`, and `npx`/`pnpx`/`bunx`/`uvx` without a package); an `npm`/`pnpm`/`yarn`/`make`/`gmake`/`just` prefix is offered with a warning in its preview that it runs scripts from package.json or the Makefile, which the agent can change. **curl uploads** (review S2): the files curl reads and sends (`-d`/`--data`/`--data-binary`/`--data-ascii`/`--json @file`, `--data-urlencode [name]@file`, `-F`/`--form name=@file` or `name=<file`, `-T`/`--upload-file`, `-K`/`--config`; glued or as the next word; `curlUploads`) are read targets: one outside the workspace adds `outside_workspace` (one with a variable is unresolvable), and the card names every file curl sends, inside the workspace too. A prefix matches only a line the lexer reads fully (no `$'…'`, no opaque command, no heredoc) whose every simple command — list and pipeline parts and command substitutions included — starts with exactly those words, with no redirection other than an fd duplication or a harmless device. A folder matches when every path of the call (and every location of its glob patterns) lies under the folder on its physical path: walked segment by segment from the root, each existing segment replaced by its real path, so a symlink is followed and a `..` after it leaves the link's target, as the kernel does; a pattern whose locations cannot be told never matches; read tools and write tools never share a folder rule; a folder that is the root or contains the home folder is never offered. An allow by a rule is logged as `permission.decided{decision: allow, ruleId}` (from the adapters' `check()` through `onRuleAllow`, from the runtime's own decision in `rulePermission`). Routes (`http/agent-routes.ts`): `POST /api/questions/:id/similar-preview` (anyone; `allowed` says whether this client may make one), `POST /api/questions/:id/allow-similar {scope, digest, version?, answerId?}` (`Commands.allowSimilar`: the user only, local or `autoApproveRemote` — 403 `remote_control` otherwise —, checked like an answer, the rule drafted again on the server from the stored request and **bound to the preview** (review S1): each previewed option carries `digest` (`draftDigest`, a SHA-256 of the draft's scope, tool, match, categories and `unclassified`), sent back with the choice, and a re-drafted rule with another digest is refused 409 `stale` before anything is answered; the project's state and the rule are checked (`checkRuleAllowed`) before the answer (review S6); with no session waiting on the request the answer and the rule are one transaction, with one the adapter gets the answer first and the rule is stored after it — should that fail, the reply is still 200 with `ruleNotStored` ("allowed once; the rule was not stored: …"), which the card shows) and `DELETE /api/projects/:id/permission-rules/:ruleId`; both writes go through the agent speed bump (`rulesPeer`, an `agentPeerGuard`: 403 `agent_peer`), and so do answering a permission request (`POST /api/questions/:id/answer` when the question is a permission) and dismissing any question (`answersPeer`, review S4; a clarification's answer is not guarded). The desktop app is Mission Control's parent, not below it, and carries no marker, so its menu bar passes; so does the panel's browser; an unidentified peer is allowed and logged. A speed bump, not a boundary (SPEC §8). One resolver gives an agent's project for its table, its workspace and its rules (`AgentRuntime.projectIdOf`: the agent's project, else its task's, else the spawn's; review S7), and a preview for an agent not running here builds the gate's context from the rows — workspace, the task's worktree, `aliasesOf`, `userFoldersOf` (review S8) — so it judges the call as the gate would. No brain tool makes or removes a rule, and the protocol's `DomainCommands` does not carry these commands. Rules are removed (by the system) when the project becomes `completed` or `archived` (`setProjectState`) or is cancelled (`cascade.ts`); a project the deriver marks `failed` keeps them. Known limits: the rule is drafted from the request as the log keeps it — redacted — so a command carrying a secret gets an exact rule that never matches (it asks again: fails closed). A folder rule judges the paths and patterns a call names; **Grep and Glob then walk the folder themselves** and follow a symlink inside it that leads elsewhere (ripgrep and glob follow links), which the gate never sees — a read folder rule covers what its links reach. And the gate decides **before** the tool opens the file (time of check vs. time of use): a link swapped in between — by the agent's own earlier command — is followed. Both are the lexical engine's limits; an OS sandbox is the boundary for them.
- **Credentials reach the CLI's tools.** The `claude-sdk` adapter passes `CLAUDE_PASS_ENV` (login and API routing: `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, Bedrock/Vertex keys, …) to the CLI because it needs them; every shell child of the agent can therefore read them. Accepted for a local single-user tool; revisit with a sandbox.
- Known secret patterns (API keys, tokens, private-key blocks, case-insensitive `password`/`secret`/`api_key`/`token`-style assignments, Basic auth, Slack, GitLab and Hugging Face tokens) are redacted from transcripts before persisting and before sending to any client, from every string of the other agent events, and from the error messages of `/api` 409 responses (P1-31, P1-38); the routes over `DomainCommands` (`/api/control`, `…/decide`, `…/answer`) answer an error that is not a domain refusal with a 500 `{error: internal}`, logged and never echoed. **Text the user (or a supervisor) writes is not redacted**: `message.sent.text`, `question.answered.answer` and the spawn prompt in `Agent.meta` are stored and broadcast as written — a key pasted into a message stays in the log.
- Store API keys in the OS keychain or a `.env` outside the repo; never send them to the browser.
- **Web Push (P7-04).** The VAPID private key stays in `<dataDir>/vapid.json` (0600) and only its public half leaves the server. Push endpoints are capability URLs: they are never listed, logged or sent back to a client (devices are named by a hash). A registered endpoint must be a known browser push service (no SSRF through `PUT /api/push/subscription`). Payloads are encrypted to the device, carry no token or credential URL, and hold names or question text only for a device that opted in ("visible on the lock screen").
- **Deliverables (P9, §9.9).** Files are served only by deliverable id, re-checked on their real path inside the project on every read, with `nosniff`, a sandboxing CSP and `attachment` for anything not previewable; downloads use one-time tickets, never the token in an address; opening, launching and starting runs are local only, even with `autoApproveRemote` (the phone may stop a run).
- **Backups and exports (P8-03, §6.4).** Backup files (0600 in a 0700 folder) and export files (0600) hold the whole history, including what the user wrote as written, so they are as sensitive as the database. The export never carries the token, `config.json`, VAPID keys, push subscriptions, the process table or runtime sessions, and transcripts only on request. Listing, taking backups and exporting are local only unless `autoApproveRemote` (decision 90). Import and restore exist only on the command line, with the server stopped (enforced by the data lock, §6.4).

---

**Server-run git (P2-05).** The server runs git only through `workspace/git.ts`: `execFile` with argument arrays (never a shell string), `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false`, an allow-listed environment, no prompts (`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`) and a 60 s timeout. Hooks are off because the server's own `worktree add`, merge and initial commit would otherwise run repository hooks on the server's behalf, outside any agent and permission gate; Stewards' own commits run in their shell, with hooks, under the project's policy. Git work on one repository is serialised in-process. `mergeTask` tells a conflict with the project's own work from one with external changes (SPEC decision 30): the base side is `rev-list --full-history --no-merges <merge-base>..HEAD -- <conflicting files>`, and a commit is the project's own when a merge of one of its tasks brought it in (any `before..commit` of the task's `Task.meta.merges`, appended on every successful merge; `meta.merge` is the last); the rest are `externalCommits` (short id, subject, author; at most 20, `externalCount` in all). It fails closed: a base side git cannot list makes the conflict external (`externalUnknown` says why), so nobody is sent back on a guess. Known limit: merge commits are skipped (`--no-merges`), so a hand edit made inside a merge commit on the base branch is not seen as external by itself. Workspace paths are canonicalised (symlinks resolved) and refused when they are `/`, the home directory, the projects root, or touch the data directory. Values a brain can write (`Task.branch`, `Task.meta`) never reach git unchecked: the worktree root is always derived from the workspace and the task id, and a stored branch must be a valid `galactic/…` ref name. Known gap: filter drivers configured in the repository's shared `.git/config` (which a Steward's shell can edit) still run for the server's `git status` / `add`; the permission table counts anything under `.git/` as outside the workspace (decision 22), which is the guard for now.

**Remote clients (P2-04).** `isRemoteRequest(req)` counts a request as local only over a loopback connection with a loopback `Host`, no non-loopback `Origin`, no forwarding header (`Forwarded`, `X-Forwarded-*`, `X-Real-IP`) and no Tailscale identity header; everything else is remote — headers can only make a request look more remote. `autoApproveRemote` (`config.json` or `GALACTIC_AUTO_APPROVE_REMOTE`) is off by default; while off, a policy a remote client chooses is floored at the global default with `noLooserThan` (the stricter of the two per category) — on every `permissionPolicy` write: `policyForWrite(policy, {origin, autoApproveRemote})` (`domain/permissions.ts`) guards `createProject`, `updateProject`, a project `control` edit, the `POST /api/agents` presets (P2-21) and the trust level an approved proposal card gives its new project (`decideProposal(…, write)`, P2-22). The floor covers `unclassified` too (the stricter of the two, `ask` when either is absent), so a remote client can never make a project `trusted`; the card's level itself (`meta.trust`, folded by `foldProposalMeta` on server and clients alike) is set only by a user decision. The command surface takes an optional `WriteOptions {origin: 'local' | 'remote'}` last argument and a missing origin counts as remote; the REST routes pass `originOf(req)`, and `POST /api/agents/:id/send` records it on `message.sent.origin`, so a brain acting on a user's message writes with that origin (`originOfMessages` over the turn: local only if every message is). A remote answer to a permission question is an explicit user decision and is accepted. **Remote controls (P7-02; SPEC decision 90):** a free-form spawn (`POST /api/agents`, the ops console's development tool, decision 24), stopping a Steward's leftover processes (`POST /api/agents/:id/processes/stop`) and restarting the Harmonizer (`POST /api/harmonizer/restart`) are local-only unless `autoApproveRemote` (403 `remote_control`, nothing done). The work and agent controls (`/api/control`, pause / resume / recover / stop of an agent), answers, dismissals of questions (P9-14) and card decisions stay open to remote clients: they are what the phone is for. Work-driven spawns (a Waykeeper's Stewards, the Harmonizer's) never go through `POST /api/agents` and are unaffected; the spawn's `policyForWrite` floor stays as a second line. The Harmonizer's `control_work` follows the panel's controls. The detection rule also counts every `X-Forwarded-*` and `Tailscale-*` header and `Via` as a proxy header. `/health` reports `origin`, and the gateway logs a remote session's credential kind (token or identity), never its value. The global default table is a setting (P25-01, SPEC decision 50): it lives in `domain/permissions.ts` (`globalDefaultPolicy` / `setGlobalDefaultPolicy`, process-wide, set at startup from `config.json` and by `PATCH /api/settings`), and every merge (`resolvePolicy`) and floor (`noLooserThan`) reads it. A remote `PATCH /api/settings` of it goes through `policyForWrite` against the current default, so it can only tighten (reply `floored: true`); a remote reset to the SPEC table is floored too. Every other setting is local-only (decision 51): unless `autoApproveRemote`, a remote or unknown-origin patch touching anything but `qualityTier` and `defaultPermissionPolicy` is refused with 403 `remote_settings` before anything is written.

## 9. Deliverables (Phase 9)

What a project or task produced, reachable from the panel (SPEC §4.7, decisions 118–134; tasks in `docs/tasks/PHASE_9_DELIVERABLES.md`). Everything lives in a new server folder, `apps/server/src/deliverables/` (the service, path checks, detection, copies, file serving, the opener, managed runs), with its vocabulary in `packages/protocol/src/deliverables.ts`; the panel's side is `apps/web/src/panel/deliverables/`. No runtime SDK is imported there: the Steward's tool reaches the server through the adapter's existing `tools` option (§3.1).

### 9.1 Data model, events and projections

**Tables (migration 0014, `0014_deliverables.sql`).**

- `deliverables(id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, project_id REFERENCES projects(id), task_id REFERENCES tasks(id), kind, title, description, source, added_by JSON, location JSON, command JSON, url, detected_key, version INTEGER, removed_at, removed_by JSON, removed_reason, last_run JSON, created_at, updated_at, meta JSON)`, `CHECK (project_id IS NOT NULL OR task_id IS NOT NULL)`, `kind IN ('file','folder','app','image','command','url')`, `source IN ('agent','detected','user')`; indexes on `project_id`, `task_id` and a unique partial index on `(project_id, detected_key) WHERE detected_key IS NOT NULL` (one row per detected script, also once removed, so a scan never re-adds what the user removed — decision 119).
- `deliverable_runs(id, deliverable_id REFERENCES deliverables(id), line, cwd, state, started_at, ended_at, exit_code, signal, outcome, reason, ports JSON, started_by JSON, stopped_by JSON, meta JSON)` — every run, for the run history (`GET …/runs`). A projection of the `run.*` events (`projectRunEvent`, `domain/deliverables.ts`), written by `EventLog` in the transaction of each event like the budget projections (P8-01), and folded once from an existing log (settings key `run_projection`) — so an import (§6.4) rebuilds it from the exported events.
- `run_output(run_id, seq, at, chunk)` — a run's output, like `transcript_chunks` (§6.2): runtime bookkeeping with no event, pruned by retention, never exported. `PRIMARY KEY (run_id, seq)`.

**Vocabulary (`packages/protocol/src/deliverables.ts`).** `DeliverableKind`, `DeliverableSource`, `DeliverableLocation{storage: 'in_place' | 'worktree' | 'copy', path (absolute, canonical at write), originalPath?, missing?{reason, at}}` (`copy`: in the project's `deliverables/` folder, §9.4), `DeliverableCommand{line, cwd (absolute, canonical), detectedFrom?}`, `Deliverable{id, projectId?, taskId?, kind, title, description?, source, addedBy: Actor, location?, command?, url?, detectedKey?, version, removedAt?, removedBy?, removedReason?, lastRun?: RunSummary, createdAt, updatedAt, meta?}` (a project task's deliverable carries both `taskId` and the task's `projectId`; a standalone task's only `taskId`), `DeliverableRun` (a `deliverable_runs` row), `RunSummary{runId, state: 'starting' | 'running' | 'stopping' | 'ended', startedAt, endedAt?, outcome?: 'exited' | 'failed' | 'stopped', exitCode?, signal?, reason?, ports: number[]}`, and the REST bodies of §9.6–§9.8. `meta` holds what is not worth a column: `size`, `mtime` at declaration, a detected script's `important` rank, `confirmedOpen{fingerprint, at, by}` (decision 128), `copy{bytes}`. A project's `deliverables/` folder, once made, is recorded as `Project.meta.deliverablesFolder` (absolute, canonical; a scratch task's as `Task.meta.deliverablesFolder` — a standalone task in a project's folder uses that project's) through `project.updated` / `task.updated`, so a rename never moves it (decision 122). `version` increases on every change of the row's own fields (`deliverable.updated` / `removed` / `restored`, each carrying the new `version`); it is the stale check of a run's confirmation (decision 129), like a question's `version` (§5.3). `lastRun` is folded from `run.*` and changes neither `version` nor `updatedAt`, so a run ending while the dialog is open does not make it stale.

**Events (one write rule, §6.1).** `deliverable.added{deliverable}` (the whole row), `deliverable.updated{deliverableId, changes, version}` (re-pointed, copied, missing, edited, `confirmedOpen`, moved with its work; `changes` follows the P2-20 patch rule — an absent key is unchanged, `null` clears `description`, `location`, `command`, `url` or `meta`, and `meta` is the whole new value), `deliverable.removed{deliverableId, by, reason?, version}` and `deliverable.restored{deliverableId, by, version}`; `deliverable.opened{deliverableId, action: 'open' | 'reveal' | 'ide' | 'app', by}` (the audit of decision 127; folds to nothing); `run.started{run}`, `run.ports{runId, deliverableId, ports}` and `run.ended{runId, deliverableId, outcome, exitCode?, signal?, reason?, by?}`. `lastRun` on the deliverable row is folded from the `run.*` events, so the world carries one summary per deliverable and the run history stays a REST read. `run.ports` and `run.ended` fold into `lastRun` only when it is that run. Moves fold too: `moveTask` and `transferTask` (P2-12) update the `project_id` of every deliverable of the moved task, removed ones included, in the same command (`deliverable.updated{changes: {projectId}}`), so decision 124's roll-up follows the work. A promotion is covered by them: `createPromotedProject` only creates the new project, and the flow then moves the task with `moveTask` (a standalone task) or `transferTask` (a project task).

**Projections.** `WorldState.deliverables` (by id) in `packages/protocol/src/world.ts`, folded by `applyEvent` — the server's import (§6.4) and the web client share it; `Snapshot.deliverables?` (optional: a page of the previous build ignores it, rule 5 of §6.3). The conformance test (`apps/web/src/state/conformance.test.ts`) drives every `deliverable.*` and `run.*` event through the real commands. Removed rows stay (`removedAt`), so the history keeps them (decision 132); the panel hides them unless "Show removed".

**Commands.** `DomainCommands.addDeliverable(input, by, write?)`, `updateDeliverable(id, changes, by, write?)`, `removeDeliverable(id, by, reason?, write?)` (never deletes a file, decision 122), `restoreDeliverable(id, by, write?)` (`domain/deliverables.ts`) — synchronous under the one write rule, `DomainError` on a refusal; the file-system work (checking a path, copying) is done by `DeliverableService` before the command and its outcome recorded by it, never inside a transaction. `write` (`DeliverableWriteOptions`) carries the request's `origin` — a user's add, edit, remove or restore is refused with `DomainError('remote_control')` (REST 403 `remote_control`, the code the routes' own `requireLocal` answers first) unless it is `local` or the server sets `autoApproveRemote` (decision 131) — and `expectedVersion` (a changed row is `stale`). Refusals: an unknown target or row; a task of another project; a shape that does not fit the kind (a path kind needs an absolute `location.path`, a command a `command` with an absolute `cwd`, a `url` an http(s) address, and nothing of the others); a second detected row for the same project and key (removed or not); an agent editing, removing or restoring a row it did not declare (`source` not `agent`, decision 120); an agent restoring a row the user removed (`removedBy` the user); an agent writing outside its own work — the supervision rule's second line under the tools' `maySupervise` (P9-04): a Steward reaches only its task's rows, a Waykeeper its project's (the project's own and its tasks'), the Harmonizer standalone tasks', and an agent the log does not know nothing; an edit of a removed row by anyone but the system (restore it first); an owner, source or key change (those move only with the task). `updateDeliverable` merges `meta` (a `null` value drops a key) and logs nothing for a change that changes nothing. The run commands, for `RunService` (P9-08): `Commands.recordRunStarted({runId?, deliverableId, line, cwd, meta?}, by)` (refused for a removed or non-command row, while one run of it is live, and to an agent — the user starts runs, decision 129), `recordRunPorts(runId, ports)` (sorted, unique; the same set logs nothing) and `recordRunEnded(runId, {outcome, exitCode?, signal?, reason?, by?})` (idempotent); `recordDeliverableOpened(id, action, by)` is the audit of §9.7.

### 9.2 Paths: where a deliverable may point (`deliverables/paths.ts`)

Pure checks over a small `fs` facade (`PathFs`), so tests run on temporary folders and can swap a file between the check and the open. API: `deliverableRoots(input, {dataDir, home})` → the canonical roots; `checkPath(path, roots, {cwd?, secretNames?, stray?, outside?})` → `{ok, path (real), where: 'worktree' | 'deliverables' | 'workspace' | 'repository' | 'stray' | 'outside', type, stat}` (`outside`: a path outside every root accepted for a copy, the user's addition by hand, §9.3) or a refusal `{code: 'invalid' | 'missing' | 'outside' | 'refused_area' | 'secret' | 'stray_refused' | 'hard_link' | 'changed', reason}`; `openChecked` (below); `identityOf` / `sameIdentity` for download tickets. **Real paths are on-disk names:** roots and paths go through `realpathSync.native`, which on macOS returns a name as it is on disk (its case and its Unicode normalisation); `fs.realpathSync` and `canonical()` keep the spelling they were given, so `Proj/FILE.txt` and `proj/file.txt` would differ on a case-insensitive APFS volume. Containment in a root compares exact on-disk strings (a mismatch refuses); the refused areas and secret names compare case-folded NFC names, so `.GIT/config` is refused too. **Hard links (agents only):** a hard link inside the project to a file elsewhere has the inside path as its real path, so `realpath` cannot tell; with `secretNames` (an agent's declaration and every re-check of an agent's row) a file with `nlink > 1` is refused (`hard_link`, "declare a copy of it instead"). The user's own additions and detected rows may point at one — refusing them would refuse every file pnpm links into `node_modules`. What an agent can still do is copy a secret into an ordinary file (§9.9).

- **Allowed roots** for a task's or project's deliverable: the project's `workspace`, its `meta.workspace.repoRoot` when the workspace is inside a repository, the task's worktree root (`worktreeRoot(workspace, taskId)`, never `Task.worktreePath` as written), a standalone task's workspace, and the project's (or standalone task's) `deliverables/` folder, which lies inside the workspace anyway (§9.4). **A link never becomes a root** (P9 wave-3 review S1): `deliverableRoots` keeps the recorded deliverables folder only when its real path is the recorded path itself (compared case-folded) and `lstat` says it is no symlink (`trustedFolder`; a folder not made yet only when the part of it that exists holds no link), and the task's worktree only when its real path is `<real workspace>/.galactic/worktrees/<taskId>` — a folder, a parent or a worktree replaced by a symlink would otherwise make the link's target an allowed root. Every user of the roots (declarations, copies, reads, the runs' folder check, the folder's Reveal) goes through the same computation. A path is resolved against the agent's cwd, then `realpath`ed (every symlink followed) and must be inside one of them after resolution. Refused inside: any `.git` directory, `<workspace>/.galactic/` except the task's own worktree, the server's data folder (`dataDir`), `/`, the home directory itself.
- **Secret names (agents only):** a basename matching `.env`, `.env.*`, `.envrc`, `*.pem`, `*.key`, `*.p12`, `*.pfx`, `AuthKey_*.p8` (App Store Connect keys), `*.ppk`, `*.jks`, `*.keystore`, `*.keychain*`, `*.kdbx`, `*.ovpn`, `*.tfstate`, `*.tfstate.backup`, `id_rsa*`, `id_dsa*`, `id_ecdsa*`, `id_ed25519*`, `.npmrc`, `.netrc`, `.pypirc`, `.pgpass`, `.htpasswd`, `.vault-token`, `.git-credentials`, `credentials*`, or any component named `.ssh`, `.aws`, `.azure`, `.gnupg`, `.docker` (its `config.json`) or `.kube`, or the pair `.config/gcloud`. A folder deliverable is refused when it *is* one of these; a folder containing them is allowed (the folder is opened on the Mac, never served whole — decision 125).
- **Strays (decision 122):** a path outside the roots is accepted from an agent only when its real path is inside a temporary folder — `os.tmpdir()`'s real path (`/private/var/folders/…/T`) or `/private/tmp` — owned by the server's uid, not a symlink itself, with a birth time (`stat.birthtimeMs`; `ctimeMs` where the file system has none) no earlier than the agent's first `agent.spawned` minus one second; it is then copied at once (§9.4). Known limit of the fallback: `ctime` changes with any change of the file's metadata (a `chmod`, a `touch`, a new link, a rename), so on a file system without birth times an older file the server's user owns can be made to look new by the agent; APFS (every macOS temporary folder) has birth times, so it applies only elsewhere. Anything else outside the roots is refused with the reason.
- **Reading safely (every read, view, download, open):** re-resolve and re-check the stored path; the real path holds no symlink by construction, so nothing after `realpath` follows one: the check `lstat`s the real path (not `stat`) and reads its real path again (a parent swapped for a link meanwhile no longer resolves to it: `changed`), and the open is `open(real, O_RDONLY | O_NONBLOCK | O_NOFOLLOW_ANY)` — macOS's flag (`0x20000000`, not exported by Node; it replaces `O_NOFOLLOW`, the two together are `EINVAL`) that answers `ELOOP` for a symlink in *any* component, so a parent folder swapped for a link out of the project between the check and the open cannot redirect it (`O_NONBLOCK`: a FIFO swapped in never hangs the server). Then `fstat` the descriptor and compare `(dev, ino)` with the checked `stat`; read only through that descriptor. A mismatch or an `ELOOP` (the file or a folder above it was swapped) is a 409 `changed`, read again by the client. Off macOS (`checkedOpenFlags(platform)`) the open falls back to `O_NOFOLLOW`, which guards the final component only: a parent swapped for a link before the `lstat` and the open both then reach the same outside file, so the guarantee there is weaker (Linux would need `openat2` with `RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS`, not reachable from Node); Mission Control runs on macOS.
- **File facts** (`GET` responses, never stored): exists, size, mtime, `kindGuess` (text / image / pdf / binary: no NUL in the first 8 KiB and valid UTF-8 → text), the preview content type by extension (a fixed table; anything else `application/octet-stream`), whether it would run code when opened (`APP_EXTENSIONS`: decision 128's extensions — programs and scripts, installers and system add-ons, disk images, location files —, an `.app` directory; and a file with the execute bit that starts with a Mach-O magic or `#!`, or has no extension at all, since the Finder runs any extensionless executable in Terminal — the latter become a command suggestion, not an app). `deliverables/facts.ts`: `factsOf(name, stat, head)` is pure; `fileFacts(path, roots, opts)` reads through `openChecked` and answers `exists: false` with the refusal for a missing or refused file, so a list never fails for one row. The application fingerprint (decision 128) is the opener's (§9.7).

### 9.3 Declaration: agents, detection, the user (`deliverables/service.ts`, `deliverables/detect.ts`)

**`DeliverableService`** (built by `composeRuntime`, like `StewardProcesses`) is the one entry: `declare(by, target, input)` checks the target (an agent may declare for its own task — a Steward —, or for its project and tasks — a Waykeeper, by `maySupervise` (§5.3) —, or for standalone tasks — the Harmonizer), the limits (decision 120, settings `deliverables.declarations{perTask: 50, perProject: 200, perAgentPerHour: 30, titleChars: 120, descriptionChars: 2000}` — active agent-declared rows per task and project, a sliding hour in memory like `tell_steward`'s window; they count declarations, never bytes), the path (§9.2), copies a stray (§9.4), then runs `addDeliverable`. Agent-written text goes through the redaction of §8 like any agent event; a command line or an address that holds what looks like a secret is refused rather than redacted (redacting it would break it), as is an address with a user name or password. The same thing declared again for the same target (same kind and path, line or address; a stray by its original path) returns the existing row and costs no declaration. The service is built by `composeRuntime` when a data folder is known (`ComposeOptions.deliverables`, `index.ts` passes `config.dataDir`, `limits: () => declarationLimits(config)` and the backups' `reserveBytes`, read at every declaration so a changed setting applies at once); brains reach it as `BrainToolContext.deliverables` and write through their own command surface (their writes never wake them). **Strays and copies:** a stray must be born after the agent's first session — a Steward's own; for a brain declaring for a task, the task's earliest Steward (a brain's own start may long precede the work, so a file it did not make would pass); a brain declaring for a project (or a task with no Steward yet) gets no stray at all (refused `outside`, saying so). An accepted stray is handed to the composition's `DeliverableCopies.copyIn(source, {projectId?, taskId?}, rules)` with the rules it was checked with (the agents' and the stray rule, applied again right before the copy and to every entry, §9.4) (the `DeliverableCopier` interface of `copies.ts`, §9.4) — a task's stray into the task's subfolder, a project's at the folder's top, a scratch task's into its own folder — which makes and records the folder, checks the space and copies; the row is then the returned `location` (`storage: 'copy'`, `originalPath`) with `meta.copy` (`bytes`, and what was left out). Without copies (a composition without `workspaces`) the service refuses a stray with the reason ("save it inside your working folder"); a refused copy refuses the declaration with the copy's reason (both sizes for the disk). `ComposeOptions.deliverables` is one shape for both: `{dataDir?, home?, limits?, tmpDirs?, reserveBytes?, freeBytes?}`. **Roots:** every place that needs a project's or a task's roots — the declarations, the copies and the merge hook, the routes that serve, open and add by hand — reads them through `deliverables/roots.ts` (`rootsOf(tables, {projectId?, taskId?}, places)`; a standalone task's folder by `standaloneWorkspace`: `Task.meta.workspace`, else its newest Steward's `meta.workspace` or `meta.cwd`).

**Steward tool.** A Steward spawned on `claude-sdk` (and on the fake runtime of tests and dev mode — `TOOL_RUNTIMES`, `runtime/tool-runtimes.ts`) gets the server's tools through `SpawnOptions.tools`, attached by the runtime itself at every start of a Steward's session that has a task — spawn, resume, fresh restart — through the hook the composition registers (`AgentRuntime.setStewardTools`, `brains/deliverable-tools.ts` `stewardDeliverableTools`), so a resumed Steward keeps them — `declare_deliverable{kind, title, path?, command?, url?, description?}` and `list_deliverables{}` (its own task's) — served by the in-process MCP server as `mcp__galactic__…` (§3.1). The permission gate allows the server's own `mcp__galactic__*` tools without a card (they are the server's code, not the agent's), and the adapter must keep every other tool behind `canUseTool` and the PreToolUse hook as today (the SDK's `allowedTools` lists only these two; a test checks that a Bash call still reaches the gate). The ACP adapter refuses `tools` (§3.2) and a terminal agent has none, so on those runtimes `brains/steward-prompt.ts` asks for a "Deliverables:" list in the "Done:" report and the supervisor declares them. The format is one line per deliverable under a line `Deliverables:` — `- <kind> | <path, command or address> | <title>`, a path relative to the Steward's working directory or absolute (e.g. `- image | screenshots/home.png | Home page screenshot`, `- command | pnpm dev | Dev server`). `deliverables/report-block.ts` reads it (Markdown emphasis, headings and numbered items allowed; at most 20 items; the block ends at the first line that is not an item) from the whole report before it is cut, and `runtime/steward-reports.ts` adds under the report the supervisor reads one ready `declare_deliverable` input per item (`{taskId, kind, title, path | command | url}`, paths resolved against the Steward's folder) plus the lines it could not read; nothing is declared without the supervisor's call, so every path is still checked as the supervisor's declaration. The etiquette line for every Steward: declare what the user will want to open (an app, a report, a screenshot, a script to run), not every file changed.

**Brain tools** (`brains/deliverable-tools.ts`, shared by both brains): `declare_deliverable{taskId?, projectId?, kind, title, path?, command?, url?, description?}`, `remove_deliverable{deliverableId, reason}` (agent-declared rows only; never `source: user` or `detected`), `list_deliverables{projectId?, taskId?, includeRemoved?}`. Reach: the Waykeeper's own project and its tasks; the Harmonizer's standalone tasks (and any project, read-only). Relative paths resolve against the task's worktree (or the project's workspace, a standalone task's folder); a command's folder defaults to the same. A refusal comes back as the tool's result (`{error, message}`) with the reason. The Waykeeper's prompt: when closing a task, read the Steward's report and its declared deliverables, declare those a non-Claude Steward listed, remove obvious scratch; prefer declaring a dev command as a deliverable to leaving a dev server running (decision 76's exception, §8).

**Detection (`detect.ts`, pure parsers over file text; nothing is executed; the scan service is `deliverables/detection.ts`, `ProjectDetection`).** The project folder deliverable (`detected_key = 'folder'`, kind `folder`, title "Project folder", `location{storage: 'in_place', path: <the workspace's real path>}`) is added by the first scan, which `Workspaces.prepareProject` triggers. Script scanners read the workspace's top level only — never a subfolder, never through a symlink (`O_NOFOLLOW`), regular files only, each at most 1 MiB (a larger one adds nothing) — and only for a prepared project (`meta.workspace`) that is not archived: `package.json` `scripts` (the package manager from the lockfile present — `pnpm-lock.yaml` → `pnpm run <s>`, `yarn.lock` → `yarn run <s>` (never `yarn <s>`: a Yarn built-in such as `yarn add` or `yarn exec` would shadow a script of that name), `bun.lock`/`bun.lockb` → `bun run <s>`, else `npm run <s>`; npm's lifecycle scripts (`install`, `prepare`, `postinstall`, …) and `pre<x>`/`post<x>` hooks of another script are left out; the script's text is the row's description, redacted); `Makefile` / `makefile` / `GNUmakefile` rule targets (`^name:` lines, not `.PHONY` or other dot-targets, patterns with `%`, variables, assignments (`:=`, `::=`), lines inside `define … endef` or after a `\` continuation) → `make <t>`; `justfile` / `.justfile` recipes (not assignments, `set`, `alias`, `import`, `mod`, nor private recipes — `_name` or `[private]`) → `just <r>`; `pyproject.toml` `[project.scripts]`, `[tool.poetry.scripts]` → `uv run <s>` with `uv.lock`, `poetry run <s>` with `poetry.lock`, `pdm run <s>` with `pdm.lock`, else the script name, and `[tool.pdm.scripts]` → `pdm run <s>`; `Cargo.toml` → `cargo run --bin <b>` per named `[[bin]]` (key `Cargo.toml#run:<b>`), or `cargo run` for a single package (`Cargo.toml#run`), and `cargo test` for a package or a workspace; `compose.yaml` / `compose.yml` / `docker-compose.yaml` / `docker-compose.yml` (not empty) → `docker compose up` (`<file>#up`); `deno.json(c)` `tasks` → `deno task <t>` (JSONC comments and trailing commas read). Where a tool reads the first of several names (make, just, Compose, deno), only that one is read. A name is one shell word: as is when it holds only `[A-Za-z0-9_.:@/+=,%^-]`, else single-quoted; a name starting with `-`, holding a control character (C0 or C1), a format character (`\p{Cf}`: bidi overrides, zero-width characters that make a name read as another) or a line or paragraph separator (`\p{Zl}`, `\p{Zp}`), or over 200 characters is skipped. **What a command runs** is kept with it: `DetectedCommand.body` — a `package.json` script's text, a `deno.json` task's `command`, a pyproject script's target or pdm command, a Makefile rule's recipe lines (tab-indented, with their continuations) and a just recipe's indented lines; Cargo and Compose have none — redacted with `redactSecrets` (like the description: a token written into a script is never stored, logged or shown) and capped at 4 000 characters, stored as `meta.detected.body`. A change of the body alone (the same line) updates the row, so the run confirmation (§9.8) can show what the line will execute and flag a body that changed since the last run; the stored body is for display only — a run executes the command line (`pnpm run dev`), which the tool resolves from the file itself. TOML is parsed with `smol-toml` (BSD-3-Clause, no dependencies); a file that does not parse adds nothing and is logged once per project, file and reason. Keys are `<file>#<name>` (`package.json#dev`); `meta.important` ranks `dev`, `start`, `serve`, `preview`, `build`, `test` first (0–5), then the files in the order above. At most 50 detected commands per project (the rest are counted in the scan's result, not listed). **The diff** (`detectProject(projectId)` → `ScanResult{scanned, skipped?, added, updated, removed, restored, overflow, problems}`, all writes `by: system` through the domain commands): a new key `deliverable.added`; a key whose detected line, title, description or body differs from what the previous scan wrote (`meta.detected`) `deliverable.updated` — so the user's own edit of a detected row stays until the file itself changes; a key no longer found `deliverable.removed` with `reason: 'no longer in <file>'` (`more than 50 commands detected` past the cap); a key the *scan* had removed and finds again `deliverable.restored` — the same row, its run history kept; a row **the user removed** is never added, restored or changed by a scan (the unique `(project_id, detected_key)` index keeps it; "Show removed" → Restore is the user's way back, decision 119). A file present that cannot be read or parsed keeps the rows it gave before (a half-saved `package.json` does not empty the list); a deleted file removes them.
