Triggers: `Workspaces.onFolderChanged(projectId, 'prepared' | 'merged')` after `prepareProject` and after every successful `mergeTask` (after §9.4's hook, outside the repository lock), wired by `composeRuntime` (on whenever it composes `workspaces`; `ComposedRuntime.detection`), the panel's Rescan (`POST /api/projects/:id/deliverables/rescan` → `detectProject`; registered only by `http/deliverable-routes.ts`, when `buildApp` gets `rescanDeliverables` — `index.ts` passes `composed.detection.detectProject`), and **once at startup** (decision 119): after recovery `index.ts` starts `detection.detectAll()` in the background — every project that is prepared (`meta.workspace`) and not archived (`scannable()`), one at a time with `STARTUP_GAP_MS` (250 ms) between two scans, so a map of many projects never holds the first seconds of the server (a scan reads a handful of top-level files); it stops at shutdown, and what changed in a folder while the server was down is listed without a Rescan. At most one scan per project per 10 s: a call inside the window waits for its end, and calls made meanwhile share that one scan; a call while a scan runs queues one more (the running one may have read the files before the change). A scan never throws; shutdown wakes a waiting scan as skipped.

**By hand.** `POST /api/deliverables` (decision 131: local only unless `autoApproveRemote`; a path from outside the project — copied in, below — local only **always**, 403 `remote_control` even with `autoApproveRemote`, since the copy is then served to every client: a remote client could otherwise copy `~/.ssh` in and download it) with a target and `path | command | url`; the same roots, without the secret-name rule (the panel warns); a path outside the roots is copied into the project's `deliverables/` folder (§9.4; the task's subfolder for a task, the top for the project) rather than refused (decision 122): `checkPath(path, roots, {outside: true})` applies the user's rules first — `/`, the home folder itself, the data folder or a folder holding it, anything inside `.git` or `.galactic` are still refused (409 `refused_area`) — and answers `where: 'outside'`; the route then calls `DeliverableCopies.copyIn(path, target, {outside: true})` (passed to the routes as `copies` by `buildApp`'s `deliverableCopies`), which checks the path and every entry again with those rules (§9.4), and records the copy (`storage: 'copy'`, `originalPath`, `meta.copy`); a copy whose recording the command then refuses stays in the folder, unrecorded (never deleted, decision 122); a refused copy (the disk, too many files, a folder holding the project) is answered with its reason. Without copies (an app built without them) such a path stays refused (409 `outside`). A command's folder is never copied: it must be inside the roots. The dialog says the file will be copied.

### 9.4 The project's `deliverables/` folder and merged worktrees (`deliverables/copies.ts`)

**Where (decision 122).** Copies go inside the project's own folder: `<workspace>/deliverables/`, project-level copies at its top and a task's in `<workspace>/deliverables/<task-slug>/` (`slugify(task.description, 40)`, plus `-<first 6 of the task id>` when another task's copies, or anything not the task's own, already have that name; a task whose copies are already in a subfolder keeps it). Always the project's main folder — the workspace checkout the Waykeeper merges into — never a task's worktree. A standalone task uses its own workspace the same way (decision 34): `<projects root>/scratch/<slug>/deliverables/` for a scratch task (its copies at the top: the folder is the task's own), or `<related project's workspace>/deliverables/<task-slug>/` when its workspace (`Task.meta.workspace`) is a project's — its `relatedProjectId` first, else any unarchived project with that workspace. There is no separate setting: the parent is the project's workspace, by default under the projects root (`projectsRoot`, `~/GalacticProjects`, already a settings-page field changed from local requests only — decisions 27, 51). The folder is made when the first copy needs it (never before), with `mkdir` under a parent re-checked with `canonical` (a `deliverables` that is a symlink, or not a directory, is refused with the reason), mode 0700 (the task's subfolder the same way, a symlink or a file refused), and recorded as `Project.meta.deliverablesFolder` (a scratch task's as `Task.meta.deliverablesFolder`; an absolute path) by a `project.updated` / `task.updated` written when the folder is made, before the command that records the deliverable which needed it — a copy is file-system work done before its command (§9.1 "Commands"), so it cannot share that command's transaction; a folder made but not recorded (the server stopped in between) is found again by the same rule and recorded on its next copy. A rename never moves it. A copied file keeps its basename; a name already taken in that folder gets ` (2)`, ` (3)` … before the extension (`report (2).pdf`).

**The API** (`DeliverableCopies`, composed with the workspaces as `ComposedRuntime.deliverableCopies`): `copyIn(source, {projectId?, taskId?}, rules)` — the declaration tools' stray copies (P9-04, `rules` = the agents' rules and the stray rule they were checked with), the user's additions from outside the project (`{outside: true}`) and the merge hook (the row's own rules: the agents' for an agent's row) call it with a path §9.2 already checked and the options it was checked with; it makes the folder, checks the source again (below) and returns `{location: {storage: 'copy', path, originalPath}, bytes, folder, skipped}` to record (`copyMeta`: `meta.copy = {bytes, skipped?, skippedCount?}`), or throws the `DomainError` to show as is. A stray copied for a declaration the domain then refuses (a limit reached meanwhile) is left in the folder, unrecorded — never deleted (decision 122); the user sees it in the folder, not in the list. `placeOf(target)` says where a target's copies go without making anything; `ensureFolder(target)` makes, excludes and records it; `beforeWorktreeRemoved` is the merge hook below; `usage()` answers `GET /api/deliverables/usage` (`http/deliverables-usage-route.ts`). The pure part — the folder's names, `recordedFolder`, `userFoldersOf` for the permission gate — is `deliverables/folder.ts`.

**Out of git, locally.** When the workspace is in a git repository, the server appends `/deliverables/` — the path relative to the repository's root when the workspace is a subfolder of it — to `<git common dir>/info/exclude` (shared by every worktree) — appended, never rewritten (so a line git or the user adds meanwhile is kept), one writer per file at a time (a per-file lock in the server), the path written as a pattern with the glob characters `[ ] * ? \` escaped and a trailing space kept with `\` (the line starts with `/`, so a `!` or `#` in a name is literal); a path holding a line break cannot be a pattern and the copy is refused — as `excludeGalactic` does for `.galactic/` (§8 "Server-run git", decision 27): never `.gitignore`, never a tracked file, never a commit. Excluded files do not show in `git status`, so the merge's "workspace is dirty" check (decision 27) is unaffected. If the repository already tracks anything under `deliverables/` (`git ls-files -- deliverables` is not empty) the folder is `galactic-deliverables/` instead, excluded the same way; the choice is recorded with the folder (its name is in the recorded path). A workspace that is not a repository just gets the folder. Known limit: `info/exclude` is shared by the worktrees, so a `deliverables/` folder a Steward makes inside its own worktree is ignored there too (its files are then kept by a copy at the merge, not by git); committing a folder of that name takes `git add -f` once, and the tracked-folder rule then sends copies to `galactic-deliverables/` for a project whose folder is not recorded yet — a recorded folder is never moved.

**Agents do not write there.** The permission engine (`domain/permissions.ts`) counts a write or edit under the recorded deliverables folder (and under `<workspace>/deliverables/` before one is recorded) as outside the workspace, like the agent configuration paths of decision 22 (`isAgentConfigPath`), so an agent's own write asks; only `DeliverableService`'s copies put files there. Mechanics: `PolicyContext.userFolders` (lexical, like the rest of the engine, and case-folded — NFC, lower case — since APFS is case-insensitive by default: `Deliverables/x` asks too; on a case-sensitive volume this only asks more often); an edit tool's path under one, and a shell line's write redirection or any argument of a command that is not read-only (`cp x deliverables/`, `rm -rf …/deliverables`), add `outside_workspace` with the reason "the project's deliverables folder, which belongs to the user"; reading it is a workspace read. The gate reads the folders on every check (`userFolders: () => …`, like the workspace) from `userFoldersOf`: the agent's project's recorded folder, a standalone task's own recorded folder plus that of any project with the same workspace, else the folder it would be made as — `AgentRuntime.setDeliverablesFolderLookup` (wired by `composeRuntime` to `DeliverableCopies.placeOf`) asks once per spawn, without holding the spawn up, so a repository that tracks a `deliverables/` source folder has `galactic-deliverables/` guarded instead and its own `deliverables/` stays a workspace folder — and `<workspace>/deliverables` until that answer arrives. Declaring a file already inside it is an in-place deliverable (§9.2).

**Copying, never through a symlink** (`copyInto(source, dir, {roots, rules})`). Immediately before the copy the source is checked again — `checkPath(source, roots, rules)` with the options it was accepted with — and must still be its own real path: a parent swapped for a symlink since the declaration (a Steward declares `x/Documents/`, then replaces `x` with a link to the home folder and commits; at the merge the old `fs.cp` followed the parent) resolves elsewhere and is refused (`changed`, or `outside` when it now leads out of the roots). A file is read only through `openChecked` (§9.2: macOS `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY`, `ELOOP` for a link in any component, `(dev, ino)` compared with the check) and written with `wx` into the partial name. A folder is walked with `lstat`, entry by entry — never `fs.cp`: symlinks and anything not a regular file or directory are left out, and every folder and file is checked with the same `rules` (decision 122 per entry, not only at the top: for an agent's source secret names and secret folders and files with `nlink > 1`; for a stray the owner and the birth-after-the-first-session rule; refused areas everywhere) and must be its own real path — so under the source's real path — and every file is read through `openChecked`. Each entry left out is listed (`skipped: [{path, reason}]`, `reason` `symlink`, `special`, `missing`, `changed` or the refusal code — `secret`, `hard_link`, `stray_refused`, `refused_area`), never a reason to refuse the copy; the row records the first 50 and the count in `meta.copy` (`copyMeta`) and the panel says what was left out (P9-09). The user's own additions copy secrets and hard-linked files as they are (their rules). Without `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` (not macOS) a file open falls back to `O_NOFOLLOW` plus the `(dev, ino)` comparison, the weaker guarantee of §9.2. A `.git` entry below the copied folder is left out silently (a worktree's link back into the repository); copied files and folders keep their mode bits minus group/other write and the set-uid, set-gid and sticky bits (`copiedMode`: `mode & 0o755`, owner-readable files, owner-`rwx` folders) and their modification time. A symlink as the source itself is refused. Before copying, the size is summed (an `lstat` walk) and compared with the free space of the destination's file system (`statfs`, the P8-02 `diskFree`; `deliverables/space.ts`: `treeSize` — asynchronous, `lstat` only, at most 100 000 entries (`{bytes, entries, complete}`; an incomplete walk refuses the copy as too many files) — and `checkFreeSpace(bytes, dir)`, which measures the nearest existing parent of a folder not made yet): a copy that would leave less than the backups' reserve free (`backups.reserveMiB`, 1 GiB, §6.4) is refused with `DomainError('invalid', 'not enough space: needs <n>, <m> free')` — the agent's tool result or the panel shows it as is. **No other size limit** (decision 122). A copy is written to a unique partial name inside the destination folder (`.<name>.<8 hex>.partial`, so a leftover of a crash is never reused or removed) and moved into place under a free name without replacing anything: a file is hard-linked to its name (`EEXIST` → the next free name) and the partial name unlinked; for a folder the name is first claimed with an exclusive `mkdir` (`EEXIST` → the next free name), so the `rename` only ever replaces that empty folder of the copy's own — a name someone else made meanwhile is never replaced (`ENOTEMPTY` → the next name). A failure removes the partial copy, never anything else.

**Worktree merges (decision 123).** `Workspaces.mergeTask` and `removeWorktree` gain a hook, `beforeWorktreeRemoved(taskId, root, {mergedCommit?})` (`WorkspacesOptions.beforeWorktreeRemoved` or `setBeforeWorktreeRemoved`; the composition sets `DeliverableCopies.beforeWorktreeRemoved`; it must not call back into `Workspaces`, whose lock it runs under; a throw goes to `onError` and the removal goes on), called inside the repository's serial lock (`serial(repoRoot, …)`) after a successful merge (or before a forced removal) and before `git worktree remove --force`. For each active deliverable of the task whose real path is inside `root`: `rel = relative(root, path)`; it is **carried** when `git --literal-pathspecs ls-tree -r --name-only <mergedCommit> -- <rel>` lists it (a folder: lists anything under it; literal, so a `*` or `:(…)` in a name is not a pathspec) and `git --literal-pathspecs status --porcelain --ignored -- <rel>` in the worktree reports nothing (no uncommitted or ignored change under it) — then it is re-pointed to `<repoRoot>/<rel>` (checked by §9.2 with the row's rules; `storage: 'in_place'`); otherwise it is copied into the task's subfolder (with the row's rules — the agents' for an agent's row — checked again right before the copy and per entry: a declared path whose parent became a symlink is marked missing, never followed) of the project's `deliverables/` folder in the main workspace (`storage: 'copy'`, `originalPath`), or marked `missing{reason}` when the copy is refused (the disk), and the task's supervisor gets a system `message.sent` naming what could not be kept (`runtime.send` with the system as sender: the Steward's `supervisorId`, else the project's Waykeeper, else the Harmonizer; not sent when that agent is not running — the row still says missing). The worktree's root itself (`rel` empty: the Steward declared its whole checkout) counts as carried after a merge. The hook records its `deliverable.updated` events (by the system) after each copy and before the removal; it never fails the merge (an error marks that deliverable missing, is logged, and the others are still kept). A merge that conflicts or is refused changes nothing. Commands declared in the worktree are re-pointed the same way (their `cwd` → `<repoRoot>/<rel>` when that folder exists; otherwise left and named in the message).

**Never deleted.** Nothing in the server deletes a file or folder under a project's `deliverables/` folder: `removeDeliverable` only marks the row removed, and archival, retention, cancel and close leave the folder alone (decision 122). A file the user deletes there makes its deliverable "missing". `deliverables/never-deletes.test.ts` holds both halves: it drives every removal, merge, forced worktree removal, close, cancel, archive, archival sweep and transcript retention through the composed server and compares the folder after each, and it lists every deletion call site in the server's code (`rm`, `unlink`, `rmdir`, `git worktree remove` …) with why it cannot reach a deliverables folder — a new one fails the test until it is reviewed. The project's Deliverables view shows the folder (Reveal in Finder, Mac only); `GET /api/deliverables/usage` reports each project's `deliverables/` size for the settings page.

### 9.5 Viewing in the panel

`apps/web/src/panel/deliverables/`: the list (`DeliverablesList.tsx` — the Waykeeper dock's third view, the Steward view's section, the history inspector's list — with one row per deliverable in `DeliverableRow.tsx`, and a pure model `deliverables-model.ts` for grouping — project, tasks newest first, sub-projects folded and read when unfolded — ordering, the row's words and which actions a client gets), the data hooks (`use-deliverables.ts`: the REST list re-read whenever the stream changes one of its rows, the client's rules, the effective IDE's name), the viewer (`DeliverableViewer.tsx`, `highlight.ts`) and the run view (§9.8). The dock's switch (`panel/status/dock-view.ts`) stores `deliverables` as a third value; the Harmonizer, which has no Deliverables view, shows its chat meanwhile. **Who may do what on this page** (decisions 131, 134): the opening actions (Open, Reveal in Finder, Open in IDE, an application's Open, the deliverables folder's Reveal) are offered on a loopback page or inside the desktop shell, and shown as "on the Mac" elsewhere; add, edit, remove, restore and Rescan follow the settings' `writable` (`all`: local, or `autoApproveRemote`); a path from outside the project is offered on the Mac only; Download is hidden inside the desktop shell. The server enforces all of it whatever the page shows (§9.9). New dependencies, loaded lazily with the viewer so the panel's first load does not grow: `react-markdown` (raw HTML is not rendered by default; `skipHtml`, `urlTransform` keeping only `http(s)`/`mailto` links, every link `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`, images replaced by links — decision 126) and `highlight.js` (core plus a fixed set of languages; output is escaped HTML from the library, the only `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` in the viewer, and a test checks a `<script>` in a source file comes out as text). Bytes are fetched with the bearer token (`fetch`, as every `/api` call) and shown from a `blob:` URL created with an explicit, allow-listed raster type (`image/png`, `image/jpeg`, … for `<img>`; text is decoded and rendered as text): a blob is never given an HTML type and never put in a frame. **An SVG is never given a `blob:` URL** (P9 wave-3 review B1): a blob URL has the panel's origin, so "Open image in new tab" would render the SVG as a top-level document of that origin, whose script could read the token from `localStorage`. The viewer rasterises it instead (`svg-image.ts`: decoded as an image from a `data:` URL — an image context runs no script and loads nothing — drawn on a canvas, shown as a PNG `blob:`); when the canvas cannot (no 2D context, a canvas tainted by the SVG as older Safari does, no decode within 5 s) it shows the `data:image/svg+xml;base64,…` URL, whose document has an opaque origin (no storage of the panel's) and which Chrome, Firefox and Safari refuse as a top-level navigation from a page. The page itself carries a Content-Security-Policy (§9.9). Preview limits (the page's, not the files'): text 2 MiB (the server sends the first 2 MiB with `X-Truncated: 1`), images 20 MiB; over those, and for binary files and PDFs, the viewer shows no preview. Open (Mac) and Download are offered for every file whatever its size (decision 125).

### 9.6 Serving files (`deliverables/serve.ts`, routes in `http/deliverable-routes.ts`)

- `GET /api/deliverables?projectId=&taskId=&removed=` — the rows plus live file facts (§9.2: `{exists, problem?, isDirectory, size, mtime, kindGuess, contentType, runsCode}`, computed through the checked descriptor; a row whose file is gone or no longer passes says so in `problem`, the list never fails for one row) and the project's (or standalone task's) `deliverables/` folder once made; removed rows only with `removed=1`; 404 for an unknown project or task. Bearer-guarded, open to remote clients. Roots are the deliverable's own (§9.2); a standalone task's workspace is `Task.meta.workspace` or else its newest Steward's `meta.workspace` / `cwd`. An agent's row is re-checked with the agents' rules (secret names, hard links) on every read, the user's and detected rows without them.
- `GET /api/deliverables/:id/content` — a file deliverable's bytes for the viewer (wherever it is, the `deliverables/` folder included): only by id, never by a path in the request; the path checks of §9.2 on every request; 404 for an unknown or removed id, 409 `changed` (or the rule it now breaks: `outside`, `refused_area`, `secret`, `hard_link`), 409 `folder` (folders are never served), 410 `missing`, 413 `too_large` over the preview limits. Text (HTML and Markdown included) is its first 2 MiB, cut at a character boundary, with `X-Truncated: 1` past them; an image of the preview table inline up to 20 MiB; anything else (a PDF, a binary) as an `attachment` up to the same 20 MiB — past it 413, and Download has no limit. Implemented in `deliverables/serve.ts` (`contentPlan`, `openForServing`, `fileHeaders`) and `http/deliverable-routes.ts` (P9-07). No `HEAD` route is exposed for the file routes (a `HEAD` must never use up a ticket) and file responses say `Accept-Ranges: none`: a ticket serves one request, so a resumed range could not be answered. Headers on every file response: `Content-Type` from the preview table (`text/plain; charset=utf-8` for every text file, HTML and Markdown included; `image/svg+xml` for SVG), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; img-src data:; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `Content-Disposition: inline` only for the preview types and `attachment; filename*=UTF-8''<name>` otherwise. Even if a response were opened directly, the CSP's `sandbox` keeps an SVG or HTML from running script on the server's origin.
- `POST /api/deliverables/:id/download` → `{url: '/files/<ticket>'}`: a one-time ticket (32 random bytes, base64url) held in memory for 60 s, bound to the deliverable and to the file's `(dev, ino, size, mtime)` at minting. `GET /files/:ticket` lives outside the bearer scope (the ticket is the credential), answers once with `Content-Disposition: attachment` and the headers above, streams through the checked descriptor with no size limit (decision 125), and refuses a used, expired or changed one (410 `expired` / `changed`; a malformed ticket is 404). At most 1 024 live tickets are kept, the oldest dropped first, and at most 64 per client (`MAX_TICKETS_PER_CLIENT`; the local panel is one client, a remote one is keyed by its Tailscale identity, else as the token's), that client's oldest dropped first — so one client in a loop cannot crowd the others' tickets out. The global `Host` allow-list still applies. Open to remote clients (decision 131). The service worker (§2.4) must not intercept `/files/` (added to its never-intercepted list explicitly, since an `<a download>` click can arrive as a navigation).
- Folders are never served (decision 125); a folder, the project's `deliverables/` folder included, is opened on the Mac only. `POST /api/deliverables/folder/reveal {projectId | taskId}` (P9-09) is Reveal in Finder for the project's (or standalone task's) recorded `deliverables/` folder, which is not a deliverable of its own: the folder is the one recorded on the work (never a path from the request), refused (409 `changed`) unless it is its own real path, re-checked against the work's roots, local only like every open (403 `remote_control` even with `autoApproveRemote`); 404 before the folder exists.
- **Direct navigation is safe too.** The content route needs the bearer token, so a page cannot be pointed at it; and were a response opened directly anyway (a download link, an `<a>` in a rendered page), the CSP's `sandbox` gives the document an opaque origin with no script, so an SVG or an HTML file served as text cannot run or reach the server's origin; `nosniff` keeps the browser from guessing a type that would.
- **By hand** (`POST /api/deliverables`, `PATCH /api/deliverables/:id {version, …}`, `POST …/:id/remove {reason?}`, `POST …/:id/restore`): a path is checked against the target's roots without the agents' rules and must fit the kind (a folder, a file, an `.app` or decision-128 name); a relative path is 400 `invalid`; one outside the roots is copied into the `deliverables/` folder (§9.3 "By hand", P9-06) — local only always, 403 `remote_control` to a remote client whatever `autoApproveRemote` says — or 409 `outside` without copies; a command's folder defaults to the workspace and is checked the same way; an edit's `command.line` keeps the stored `cwd`; a changed row is 409 `stale` with the current row (`deliverable`). `POST /api/projects/:id/deliverables/rescan` is registered when the server passes detection's scan (`AppOptions.rescanDeliverables`, P9-05).

### 9.7 Opening on the Mac (`deliverables/opener.ts`)

macOS only (`process.platform === 'darwin'`; elsewhere 501 `not_mac`). Everything goes through `execFile('/usr/bin/open', args)` with argument arrays and the allow-listed environment, a 10 s timeout, never a shell: **Open** `open <file>` — refused (409 `use_app`) by the opener itself for what would run code (an application name of §9.2's `APP_EXTENSIONS`, an executable file without an extension), whatever the route checked; **Reveal in Finder** `open -R <path>`; **Open in IDE** `open -a <the detected app's absolute path> <folder>`; **Open application** `open <path.app | file>`. Paths are always the canonical absolute paths §9.2 just re-checked (they start with `/`, so never read as an option). The IDE is the setting `deliverables.ide` (`'auto'` or a bundle id from the detected list; never a name or path from a request; `SettingsService` refuses any other value with 400 `invalid`, and the `PATCH` route runs a detection first so the list is current). Detection (`Opener.ides()`, cached 60 s, one detection in flight shared; `GET /api/ide` → `{supported, installed, setting, effective?}`, open to every client): the bundle ids of `KNOWN_IDES` (`packages/protocol`) in decision 127's order — `com.jetbrains.WebStorm`, `com.jetbrains.intellij`, `com.jetbrains.intellij.ce`, `com.todesktop.230313mzl4w4u92` (Cursor), `com.microsoft.VSCode`, `dev.zed.Zed` — each looked up with `mdfind "kMDItemCFBundleIdentifier == '<id>'"` (`/usr/bin/mdfind`, argument array) and, in parallel, by a scan of `/Applications`, `~/Applications` and `~/Applications/JetBrains Toolbox` (and one level of sub-folders) reading each app's `Contents/Info.plist` (`CFBundleIdentifier`; a binary plist is converted with `/usr/bin/plutil -convert xml1` only when it mentions a wanted id). Scanned paths first, then Spotlight's hits (Spotlight may be off or stale); the first candidate that passes both checks wins. **Where:** its real path (`realpath`, links followed) must be an existing `.app` strictly inside `/Applications` or `~/Applications` (JetBrains Toolbox installs live under the latter) — a project, a worktree, another volume or the Trash may hold an app with the right bundle id (an agent can write a fake `WebStorm.app`), and a link from `~/Applications` into a project does not count. **Who signed it:** `codesign --verify -R='anchor apple generic and identifier "<bundleId>" and certificate leaf[subject.OU] = "<Team ID>"' <app>` (`/usr/bin/codesign`, argument array) — an Apple-anchored signature (Developer ID or App Store) for that bundle id by its maker; Team IDs `IDE_TEAM_IDS`, read on Cosmin's Mac (2026-10-07): JetBrains `2ZEFAR8TH3` (WebStorm; IntelliJ IDEA shares it), Microsoft `UBF8T346G9` (VS Code); Cursor and Zed are not installed there, so they are checked without a Team ID (anchor and identifier only) until theirs are recorded. A check takes 0.3–0.9 s, so its result is cached per real path while the bundle, `Contents`, `Contents/MacOS`, `Contents/Info.plist` and `Contents/_CodeSignature` keep their `(dev, ino, size, mtime, ctime)`; the open re-checks the detected app (from the cache when unchanged) and answers `ide_not_installed` if it is gone or no longer passes. A detection with the checks takes about 0.5 s on Cosmin's Mac (WebStorm and VS Code found). `open -a <path>` launches exactly the app the settings page shows — never `open -b`, which lets Launch Services pick any registered copy of the bundle id, wherever it is; a CLI launcher (`webstorm`, `code`) is not used — it may be missing from the server's PATH. Errors (`OpenerError`): 501 `not_mac`, 400 `invalid_path` (not absolute, or a NUL or line break), 409 `no_ide` / `ide_not_installed` (the chosen IDE is gone or fails its checks), 409 `use_app` (Open on what would run code), 502 `open_failed` (the first line of `open`'s error: the app removed, no logged-in window session). Tests inject `execFile` and the file system (`OpenerFs`), so nothing is opened; `opener-real.test.ts` (`GALACTIC_REAL_IDE=1`, `GALACTIC_REAL_IDE_OPEN=<folder>`) is the tagged local check.

**Applications (decision 128).** `POST /api/deliverables/:id/open {action: 'open' | 'reveal' | 'ide' | 'app', fingerprint?}`. For an app (an `.app` bundle or a decision-128 extension) the server computes a fingerprint (`Opener.fingerprint`) — for a bundle, SHA-256 over `Contents/Info.plist` and an `lstat` walk of the whole bundle (each entry's relative path, type, size, mtime and ctime, sorted, links not followed), so a file added, replaced or touched anywhere in it changes it; past 50 000 entries the walk stops and the fingerprint is marked `toolarge:`, which a stored confirmation never covers (`confirmationCovers`: such an app asks at every open); for a file, its `(dev, ino, size, mtime, ctime)` — and answers 409 `confirm{fingerprint, addedBy, name}` unless the request carries that fingerprint (the user's confirmation) or `meta.confirmedOpen.fingerprint` already covers it; a confirmed open records `confirmedOpen` (before `open` runs) and `deliverable.opened`. `open` on a file whose facts say it would run code is refused (`use_app`; `use_command` for a program, decision 128). The route re-checks the stored path (§9.2) before any action and answers 400 `invalid` for a kind with nothing to open (a command, an address), 409 `not_folder` for Open in IDE on anything but a folder, 409 `not_app` for `app` on a file that is not an application, 501 `not_mac` off macOS, and the `OpenerError` codes; `deliverable.opened` is logged after a successful open only. Opened apps are not tracked: `isUserFacingApp` (P4-14) already keeps a bundle's processes away from any sweep.

**Who may.** These routes are local only, **never** remote — `autoApproveRemote` does not lift it (decision 131, like the terminal typing of decision 113): `requireLocal(req, {always: true})` (`http/security.ts`; it returns the 403 body, or nothing when the request may go on) answers 403 `remote_control` — the code decision 113's typing answers too: one code for "not from a remote client", the message saying it is never —; without `always` it applies the decision-51 rule (the same 403 `remote_control`, lifted by `autoApproveRemote`). The desktop app's window is a local origin (§2.3), so it opens exactly as a browser on the Mac does; the server, not the shell, runs `open`.

### 9.8 Managed runs (`deliverables/runs.ts`, `RunService`)

**Starting.** `POST /api/deliverables/:id/runs {version, line?, confirmation}` (local only, never remote): the deliverable's `version` must be the current one (409 `stale` with the current row — the dialog shows the new command, decision 129); `line` is the command as the user confirmed or edited it, else the stored one; one live run per deliverable (409 `running`); at most `deliverables.runs.maxConcurrent` (default 4) live runs (409 `run_cap`); the cwd must still pass §9.2 and be the project's folder or a standalone task's workspace — a worktree cwd is 409 `after_merge`. The confirmation dialog's warnings come from the permission engine's classification of the line (`classifyCommand` and `mentionsPush` in `domain/permissions.ts`: destructive, network, push, outside the workspace, unclassified) via `GET /api/deliverables/:id/run-preview`, which also returns the exact environment keys (§8's `INHERITED_ENV`) — never their values. **For a detected command** the dialog also shows what the line will execute as the file says it (`meta.detected.body`, §9.3: the script, rule or recipe body, redacted) and flags it when it changed since the deliverable's last run (P9-08: a run of a detected command keeps the body it was confirmed with, in its `run.started` event and so `deliverable_runs.meta.body`; the preview compares the current body with the last run's) — `pnpm run dev` reads the same however `dev` was rewritten. **As built (P9-08).** The preview also returns the engine's own lines (`reasons`), `detectedFrom`, `bodyChanged` and `lastRunBody` (the body the last run was confirmed with, when it differs), and `refusal{code, message}` when a start now would be refused (`after_merge`, `running`, `run_cap`, `folder`), so the dialog says why instead of offering Run. Warnings are worded per category: destructive → "deletes or overwrites files", network → "uses the network", push → "may push", outside the workspace → "reaches outside the project folder", an opaque line → "could not read all of it". The folder check is `checkPath` over the row's roots (`roots.ts`) with the user's rules: `workspace` and `deliverables` pass (decision 129: the project's folder — never the rest of the repository it is in, P9 wave-3 review N6), `worktree` is `after_merge`, anything else `folder`. **The confirmation covers the exact line** (wave-3 review S3): the preview answers a `confirmation` token — `<issued ms>.<HMAC-SHA256>` over the deliverable, its version and the SHA-256 of the line, with a key made at each server start, good for 15 minutes — and a start (or restart) whose token does not cover the line it would run (the stored one, or `line`) is 400 `unconfirmed`. The dialog previews an edited line with `POST /api/deliverables/:id/run-preview {line}` (read only, remote clients too): its own warnings, its own token. **An edited line** is one line (wave-3 review S2): a newline or another control character (C0 but tab, DEL, C1, U+2028/2029) is 400 `invalid`, in the preview and the start; so is a line still holding a `[redacted:…]` mask (the preview shows a stored secret masked — an edit types it again). **The event log never holds a secret of the line**: `run.started.line` (and so `deliverable_runs.line`) and the preview's `line` are `redactSecrets(line)`; the process runs the real line. A start answers `201 {run}`; an agent's call is refused by the service before the domain's own refusal. `POST /api/runs/:id/restart {version, line?, confirmation}` (local only, never remote) runs every check a start makes first — version, confirmation, line, folder, one run per command and the cap, this run not counted (`RunService.restart`, wave-3 review S6) — so a restart that could not start again stops nothing; then it stops the run and starts a new one (which checks again). The run history is `GET /api/deliverables/:id/runs` (newest 50; a live run shows the service's `starting` / `stopping`).

**The process.** `ProcessSupervisor.spawn` with a run owner: `ProcessSpec` gains `owner?: {kind: 'agent' | 'run', id}` (default the agent). For a run the PID table row (`agent_processes`, no foreign key) is keyed `run:<runId>` with `meta.kind = 'run'`, the launch intent (`agent_launches`, P8-11) is written the same way, and the child's environment is the allow-list plus `GALACTIC_RUN_ID=<runId>` and the launch id — **no `GALACTIC_AGENT_ID`**, so process attribution (P4-14) never takes a run's processes for a Steward's (they are also in the server's tree while the server lives). The command is `/bin/sh -c <line>` in its own process group (`detached`), stdin a pipe the service closes at once (the command reads end-of-file), stdout and stderr captured. A launch intent the disk refuses fails the run with 507 (decision 107). `run.started` is logged after the fork, `run.ended` from the exit (`exited` with its code, `failed` for a signal or start failure, `stopped` with `by`). As built: `ProcessSpec.owner = {kind: 'run', id}` with the key `run:<runId>` (`runProcessKey`, `process-attribution.ts`; `RUN_ID_ENV`); the supervisor writes `meta.kind = 'run'` and `runId` on the row and the intent, removes any agent marker from a run's environment (and any run marker from an agent's), and lists a run's group in `agentGroups()` under its key, so attribution sees another owner's group. `reconcile()` takes a run row by its boot and start time only — `/bin/sh -c` may have exec'd its command, so the name proves nothing — and fails closed when the start time cannot be read. A run's graceful step is SIGTERM to the group (its `interrupt`), not the agents' SIGINT. `run.started` carries `meta.pid`, the confirmed body (`meta.body`) and `meta.edited` when the user changed the line for that run.

**Output.** Redacted by the transcript pipeline's boundary-aware redactor (§6.2, `runtime/redact.ts`) per run, written to `run_output` in batches (≈ 10 Hz), streamed over the socket with `run.subscribe{runId, since?}` / `run.unsubscribe{runId}` → `run_output{runId, chunks}` — a page that never subscribes never receives the new kind (§6.3 rule 5); the backlog and each batch go in messages of at most 256 KiB (a larger single chunk alone), and a socket holds at most 16 run subscriptions (`error{code: 'too_many_subscriptions'}` past that; wave-3 review N2) — and paged by `GET /api/runs/:id/output?after=&limit=`. ANSI sequences are kept; the panel renders the output with the read-only terminal view of P8-08 (`TerminalView`, xterm.js), so colours and progress bars look as in a terminal. Limits (settings `deliverables.runs.output`: `maxBytesPerRun` 2 MiB, 64 KiB to 64 MiB, `keepRuns` 5, `maxAgeDays` 30): past the byte limit the oldest chunks of that run are deleted (the output page reports `truncatedBefore`); `TranscriptRetention`'s hourly job (P8-02) also deletes the output of a deliverable's runs beyond the newest five and older than 30 days. As built: `deliverables/run-output.ts` (`RunOutput`) is a `TranscriptPipeline` over its own `ChunkStore` (`runtime/transcripts.ts`; the default store stays `transcript_chunks`), so the redaction is the same code; the byte limit is applied in the write's transaction (the oldest chunks of the run deleted, never the newest), and `truncatedBefore` is derived from the oldest kept chunk (`seq` before it) rather than stored, so `deliverable_runs` stays a pure projection of the events. A batch the storage refuses is dropped and never delivered (the transcript rule of P8-04). Retention's run (`TranscriptRetention`, option `runOutput`) removes the output of each command's runs beyond the newest `keepRuns`, of ended runs started more than `maxAgeDays` ago and of runs the log does not know; never a live run's or pinned work's (`CompactionReport.runOutputRuns`).

**Ports.** From the output (`https?://(localhost|127\.0\.0\.1|0\.0\.0\.0|\[::1\]):(\d+)`) and from the system: `lsof -nP -a -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN -g <pgid>` plus the processes carrying the run's marker (the process table of P4-14), every 2 s for the first minute and every 10 s after; `run.ports` when the set changes (at most once per 2 s). As built: one timer for all runs (every 2 s while a run is in its first minute, else 10 s) takes one process-table snapshot and one `lsof -p` (`ProcessTable.ports`) per tick over each run's group members, every process below them and the processes carrying its marker; the markers — other processes' environments — are read every 15 s only (and at every stop's sweep), and only the matching pids and their start times are kept (re-checked against each snapshot), never an environment (wave-3 review N1). The system's set wins once it has a port — an address in the output may be another program's (a database the dev server connects to); the output's addresses stand in until then, or where `lsof` is missing.

**Stopping.** `POST /api/runs/:id/stop` (open to remote clients, decision 131 — the phone may stop, never start; the panel confirms first): SIGTERM to the group and to marker-carrying processes outside it (identity-checked as `StewardProcesses` does: pid and start time re-read before each signal), SIGKILL after 5 s to the ones still the same; `run.ended{outcome: 'stopped', by}`. A log subscriber stops runs with `reason`: `project_closed` (the project or a parent project enters `completed`, `failed` or `archived`), `task_finished` (a standalone task enters `completed`, `failed` or `archived`), `deliverable_removed`. Shutdown: `RunService.stopAll('server_stopping')` runs beside the agents' graceful stop and is awaited (≤ 6 s). As built: the group goes through `ProcessSupervisor.stop` (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL when the 5 s are up; members left after the leader's exit get what remains of them), the marker carriers — processes of the server's user started after the run, never a reaper, the server's own tree, a user-facing app or anything below one — through single-pid signals, each re-checked by its start time. A run that ends by itself is swept the same way after its `run.ended`: what it left outside its group carrying its marker (a daemon it forked) goes with it, as at a restart. **Shared daemons are never taken by the marker sweep** (wave-3 review S4): a run that happens to start a daemon the user's whole session shares would otherwise kill it with the run. `isSharedDaemon` (`process-attribution.ts`) exempts, by executable name, `ssh-agent`, `gpg-agent`, `keyboxd`, `dirmngr`, `scdaemon`, `watchman`, `sccache`, `buck2d`, and by command line an ssh ControlMaster (`ssh: <socket> [mux]`), the Gradle and Kotlin daemons (`GradleDaemon`, `KotlinCompileDaemon` main classes), `git fsmonitor--daemon`, the nx daemon (`nx/src/daemon/`), `turbo daemon`, the adb server (`adb … fork-server`) and a Bazel server (`A-server.jar`). The rule is about the marker sweep only: such a daemon still inside the run's process group goes with the group (most detach into their own). Residual risk: a shared daemon not on the list that a run starts (a language server's daemon, a database the command launches in the background) is taken with the run — the conservative side for "nothing of a run outlives it untracked" — and a process may evade the sweep by naming itself like one of these (it then outlives the run, as anything the user starts by hand does). A stop of an ended run answers the run as it is. `run.ended` is held and retried every second when the storage refuses it. A run started while the server is stopping is refused.

**Restart.** At start, after `reconcile()`: `run:` PID rows of the previous run are stopped (never resumed — unlike agents, decision 130), `reconcileLaunches()` finds a run's launch by `GALACTIC_RUN_ID` and the launch id as it does for agents (P8-11), and every deliverable whose `lastRun` is not `ended` gets `run.ended{outcome: 'stopped', reason: 'server_restarted', by: system}`. Nothing is started again. As built: `index.ts` runs `RunService.recover({launches})` after `reconcileLaunches()` and before the agents' recovery; `launches` are the runs of the previous life's PID rows (`reconcile()`'s keys) and launch intents (`LaunchReconcileResult.runs`), so the marker carriers of a run killed between its fork and its `run.started` are stopped too, while the log, which never knew that run, shows nothing. The drill is `test/drills/run-crash.test.ts` (in `pnpm drills`).

**Caps.** Runs are not agents: no `CapsEnforcer`, no budget, no status record; `deliverables.runs.maxConcurrent` is their only cap. All `deliverables.*` settings (`ide`, `declarations`, `runs` — `{maxConcurrent, output{maxBytesPerRun, keepRuns, maxAgeDays}}`, merged key by key, `runSettings(config)` read at every start, output write and retention run) are written from local requests only unless `autoApproveRemote` (decisions 51, 131; §9.9).

**The panel (P9-10, as built).** `apps/web/src/panel/deliverables/`: `RunPanel.tsx` under each command row, `RunDialog.tsx`, the pure `runs-model.ts` and `use-run-output.ts`. *The row* shows the run's state from the world's `lastRun` (the stream; the REST row may lag) — "not run yet", "running · :5173", "exited 1", "failed (SIGKILL)", "stopped — Mission Control restarted" — with a dot (green while live, pulsing unless `prefers-reduced-motion`), and a polite live region announcing the state when it changes after the row appeared (never the output). Its actions (`runActions`): Run while no run lives, Stop and Restart while one does; Run and Restart only on a loopback page or in the desktop shell (`ClientRules.local`), shown elsewhere as "Run: on the Mac" / "Restart: on the Mac" (decision 131); Stop everywhere, always after an inline confirmation ("Stop <title>? It ends the command and everything it started."). *Ports* are `http://localhost:<port>` links on the Mac — in a browser `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`, inside the desktop shell a plain link with no target, so the shell's navigation guard (`navigation_allowed`, §2.3) hands it to the default browser (decision 134) — and plain text on the phone with "on the Mac: open it in the Mac's browser" (§8 question on the phone's reach). *The dock's switch* shows "Deliverables ▶" (and "(n commands running)" to screen readers) while a command of the Waykeeper's project or its tasks has a live run (`liveRunCount`). *The Run dialog* is a modal (`role="dialog" aria-modal`, Tab kept inside, Escape cancels, the focus back on the opener): the exact command in an editable one-line field, the folder (`cwd`), who added it ("found in package.json", "added by you", "declared by <agent>"), the environment's keys (never values), the engine's warnings with a "Why" toggle over `reasons`, and for a detected command the body "as <file> says now", its lines not in `lastRunBody` marked and "changed since the last run" when `bodyChanged`, with the last run's body behind a toggle. **Where the focus starts (decided here):** on **Cancel** when the preview has a warning or the body changed since the last run, else on **Run**; Enter in the command field never runs (it only edits) — Run is a deliberate click or key press on the button; the Run button is red when there are warnings. An edited line (`field.trim() !== preview.line`) is read again with `POST …/run-preview {line}` 400 ms after the last keystroke; Run is disabled until the preview for exactly that text has come back, and sends `{version, line, confirmation}` with that preview's token; an unedited line sends `{version, confirmation}` with the stored line's token (so a masked secret in the stored line runs as stored; an edit still holding `[redacted:…]` shows the server's 400 and keeps Run off). A preview `refusal` (`after_merge`, `folder`, `running`, `run_cap`) is shown in words and Run stays off — except `running` and `run_cap` in the Restart dialog, where the replaced run is the live one (the server checks the cap without it). Refusals of a start or restart: 409 `stale` and 400 `unconfirmed` read the preview again — the dialog shows the new command (or a fresh token) with a notice and the focus on Cancel, and nothing is sent until the user presses Run again; a version change seen on the stream while the dialog is open re-reads it the same way; `run_cap`, `invalid` and `start_failed` show the server's words; `after_merge`, `folder`, `remote_control`, 507 (disk full, decision 107) and 503 (database not writable) have the panel's own words (`refusalText`). Restart uses the same dialog ("Restart <title>?") and `POST /api/runs/:id/restart`. *The output* (`RunOutputView`): the REST pages from the start (`after`, 1,000 chunks a page, at most 8 pages), then `run.subscribe{runId, since: newest seq held}` — the stream sends the rest of the backlog and the live batches; the store (`runOutputs`, beside the world, never folded) merges both by `seq` and keeps the newest 4 MiB per run; the client re-subscribes from the newest seq after a reconnect; closing the view unsubscribes and forgets it. Drawn by the read-only terminal view of P8-08, generalised as `XtermScreen` (100×16, `convertEol`, the same http(s)-only link handler asking "Open this link from this run's output?"), "Earlier output dropped" when the page reports `truncatedBefore`; a "Plain text" toggle shows it ANSI-stripped in a focusable region (for reading, copying and screen readers). Opened by itself after a Run from this page. *The history* ("Runs"): `GET …/runs` re-read when the newest run changes; the last 10 with their start, outcome, duration, ports and the edited line when `meta.edited`; "Output" shows that run's output (kept for the last five, decision 130).

**As reviewed (P9-12).** *Stored lines* — every writer's command line (an agent's, the user's by hand, a remote edit) is one visible line (`commandLineProblem` in `packages/protocol`: no control character including tab and newline, no `\p{Cf}`/`\p{Zl}`/`\p{Zp}`, private-use or lone surrogate, no space but the plain one, no `[redacted:…]` mask), checked by the domain's `addDeliverable` / `updateDeliverable` and again at every start and preview (a row from before is refused, never run); a line written by a person or an agent that looks like a secret is refused with "read it from the environment". *The dialog* shows the whole line in a wrapping read-only block above the field, a run of three or more spaces as `␠×n`, a tab as `⇥`, anything invisible as `⟨U+XXXX⟩`; the heading quotes the title, and an agent's title is said to be its words, with who declared it. *What it runs* — for a detected command run as detected, the preview reads the script **now** from its file in the run's folder with the scan's parsers (`commandBodyOf`), with the `pre`/`post` hooks npm, Yarn classic and Bun run (not pnpm or Yarn Berry), shows it redacted and cut at 4 000 characters with "cut here" and "part hidden as a secret — open the file" notices, shows the last scan's body when it differs (`scannedBody`), and classifies every line of it unredacted with the permission engine (warnings merged with the line's; reasons redacted); a line edited by hand (in the row or the dialog) shows no body and says so (`bodyMissing`). *The confirmation* is `<issued>.<body tag>.<HMAC>` over the deliverable, version, line, folder and the body's hash; a start re-reads the body and answers 409 `stale` when it changed, and each confirmation starts one run (remembered until it expires; a restart's own start uses it). *The folder* is checked again right after the fork (`(dev, ino)` of the checked path); a folder swapped in between ends the run `stopped` with `reason: 'folder_changed'` — what the shell did in that instant is not undone (known limit). *The backlog* a `run.subscribe` sends is the newest 1 MiB (`RUN_BACKLOG_BYTES`); the panel reads older output over REST and says when the middle is not shown. *Detection problems* carry no file text (a parse error gives the line and column only) and are redacted. *Copies at a merge* spend at most 100 000 entries and 120 s per merge inside the repository lock (`MERGE_COPY_BUDGET`); what is past it is marked missing with the reason and the supervisor is told.

### 9.9 Security rules (summary)

**The app's page carries a Content-Security-Policy** (P9 wave-3 review B1, defence in depth; `webCsp` in `http/web-cache.ts`, added by an `onSend` hook to every HTML answer that sets none of its own — the `/files/` downloads keep their sandbox policy): `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; worker-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; font-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self' ws://<host> wss://<host>; media-src 'self' blob:; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'self'`. Vite's build has no inline script and no `eval`; the scene's paint worker and the service worker are build files; inline styles stay allowed (React style props, xterm.js's injected sheets); the socket's host is the request's `Host` (named for older Safari, whose `'self'` did not cover `ws:`). The Vite dev server (5173) sends no policy. The e2e suite fails on any CSP violation logged by the page. The desktop shell's injected flag is a WebKit user script, outside the page's policy.

| Route | Local (the Mac) | Remote (phone, tailnet) | With `autoApproveRemote` |
|---|---|---|---|
| list, content (view), download ticket, run history and output, IDE list, stop a run | yes | yes | yes |
| add, edit, remove, restore, rescan; the `deliverables.*` settings | yes | 403 `remote_control` / `remote_settings` | yes |
| add a path from outside the project (copied in, then served to every client) | yes | 403 `remote_control` | **no** (403 `remote_control`) |
| open, reveal, open in IDE, open application; reveal the `deliverables/` folder | yes | 403 `remote_control` | **no** (403 `remote_control`) |
| start, restart a run | yes | 403 `remote_control` | **no** (403 `remote_control`) |
| start, restart, or preview an edited line **from an agent's or a run's process** (the TCP peer) | 403 `agent_peer` (speed bump) | — | — |

**The bearer token can run any command** (P9-12 review S3). The runs routes start whatever line a confirmation covers, and the confirmation is minted by a preview with the same token: the Run dialog is a staleness and "what you saw" guard for the panel, not a boundary. Whoever holds the token — the panel, but also any local process that read `config.json` or got it from `/health` past its speed bump (`docs/REMOTE_ACCESS.md` §4) — can start any command as the user, outside the permission gate (decision 129 keeps the table out of runs). **The speed bump** (`http/peer-guard.ts`): a start, a restart or the preview of an edited line arriving over a loopback connection is refused with 403 `agent_peer` when the process at the other end — found with `lsof` by the connection's ports — or any of its ancestors up to the first reaper carries `GALACTIC_AGENT_ID` or `GALACTIC_RUN_ID` in its environment, or runs below Mission Control itself (everything the server starts is an agent, a run or its own tool). The ancestors count because macOS hides the environment of platform binaries (`/usr/bin/curl`) but not of the Steward above it. A peer that cannot be identified (no `lsof`, a connection already gone) is **allowed and logged**: refusing would refuse the user's own panel whenever `lsof` is slow or missing. Not a boundary — a detached process (double fork, `setsid`, a launchd job) loses both signs; the real boundary is a §8 question (2026-10-08: a run-only capability held by the panel and the desktop shell).

Further rules: bytes are served only for a deliverable id, after §9.2's checks on the real path, through a descriptor checked against it; agent text (titles, descriptions) is redacted when logged and rendered as plain text; the server runs `open` and `git` with argument arrays only; the one shell the server starts is a run's `/bin/sh -c`, for a line the user confirmed on the Mac. Known limit: a deliverable inside the project is readable from the phone like any other file the user can view there — the secret-name rule keeps agents from pointing at the usual secret files, not from writing a secret into an ordinary one. Copies apply the rules per entry (§9.4): an agent's folder copy leaves out secret-named files, secret folders and hard links, a stray's the files it did not make; nothing is ever copied through a symlink. Server tools pass the permission gate only from the host's own in-process `galactic` MCP server, by provenance (`ToolCall.mcpServer.source === 'sdk'`, the Claude CLI's `mcp_server`), never by name alone — a repository's `.mcp.json` server called `galactic` goes through the policy table; brains run with `strictMcpConfig` (no other server is loaded).

### 9.10 Retention, export and import, backups, the desktop app

- **Retention** (P2-13, P8-02): archival never touches deliverables or any file; transcript retention prunes `run_output` (§9.8). Pinned work's run output is kept like its transcripts.
- **Export / import** (§6.4): the events carry everything; `rows.deliverables` joins the import's check (`diffRows`), optional so older exports still import (nothing is checked for them, the rebuild still folds them from the events); rows imported as exported (`--prefer-rows`) that lack a deliverable a `run.started` in the log belongs to are refused with `ImportError('drift')` naming the runs, before anything is written (the run history folded after the import would otherwise fail on its foreign key); `run_output` and the files in the projects' `deliverables/` folders are not exported (like `agent_processes` and repositories). The export's `formatVersion` stays 1. After an import, file facts report missing files; `lastRun` states that were live at the export are ended by the restart pass (§9.8).
- **Backups:** the database only; the `deliverables/` folders are inside the projects' folders, backed up with them like the repositories (Time Machine); `docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md` says so (SPEC §8).
- **Backups and exports in the user docs:** `docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md` §4 (the list, not the files); the user guide is `docs/DELIVERABLES.md`.
- **Desktop app** (§2.3, P8-06, P9-11): the page hides Download inside the shell (`window.__GALACTIC_DESKTOP__`, `clientRules`). A run's port links and URL deliverables are `target="_blank"` links to another origin: WebKit asks the shell for a new window, which wry drops when no handler is set, so the shell sets `on_new_window` — an `http(s)` address goes to the default browser through the same rate limit as the navigation rule's outside links (one a second, five a minute), no second shell window is ever opened, other schemes are refused. The rules are pure in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/links.rs` (`navigation_target`, `new_window_target`, `OpenLimiter`), unit-tested with plain `rustc --test` (`pnpm --filter @galactic/desktop test:rust`, no Tauri build). No IPC capability is added.
- **Notifications** (decision 134, P9-11): `NotificationLookup.deliverables(target)` gives `notificationOf` the work's count (`countDeliverables`: current rows from an agent or the user, a project's own and its tasks'), and a finished task's or project's title becomes "Task finished · 2 deliverables". The count is in the title, not the body, so a push to a device without details (decision 95) shows it too — no name or deliverable title ever reaches the lock screen. The server's feed and push use the tables (`deliverables.of`), the page's notifier and the desktop watcher the world's `deliverables`; a count that changed between the live event and a later backfill only changes the text, never the id.
- **Scene** (decision 133, P9-11): `label-model.ts` folds the world's `deliverables` once per change of that map into a `project:<id>` / `task:<id>` → `{count, running}` index (`deliverableMarks`; counted rows as above, `running` from any command's live `lastRun`, detected ones included) and the label shows "◆ n" / "▶" after its state pill, the arrow's colour pulse stopped under reduced motion. The mark's box is measured with the label (`updateLabel(…, {mark})`), so picking's `labelHitAt` tells a click on the mark from one on the label: `activateMark` selects the work's agent as a body click does and opens its Deliverables (the Waykeeper dock's view or the Steward view's section, `useDeliverablesFocus`), as the navigation row's "⋯ Deliverables" does. Nothing per frame: the projector is unchanged.

---

## 10. Alternatives considered

| Area | Alternative | Why not (for v1) |
|---|---|---|
| Frontend | Angular + Three.js | Much smaller 3D ecosystem; manual scene plumbing; change detection vs render loop. Fine for the side panel, poor for the scene. |
| Frontend | Svelte + Threlte | Excellent, lighter than R3F, but smaller community and fewer ready-made helpers/examples. Good second choice. |
| 3D engine | Babylon.js | Batteries-included engine with good tooling, but weaker React integration and fewer solar-system references. |
| Renderer | WebGPU (+ WebGL2 fallback) | Different material system, fewer ready-made effects/helpers, two render paths to test; nothing in v1 needs it. Revisit for GPU-driven simulation. |
| Desktop | Electron | Heavy; unnecessary because the server is already a separate process. Tauri 2 wraps the same UI (P8-06, §2.3). |
| Backend | Python (FastAPI) | Fine for the brains, but you would lose shared types and the TypeScript Agent SDK / ACP SDK ergonomics. |
| Claude transport | ACP (`claude-agent-acp`) on day one | Second Claude integration next to the SDK the brains need; cost/resume/spawn options depend on what the ACP adapter exposes. Becomes the path for Codex/Gemini later. |
| Agent integration | Prompt-only orchestration (agents talk to each other via files or messages) | Untraceable, fragile, expensive. Tool-armed brains + a real event model are simpler to debug. |
| Remote access | Cloudflare Tunnel + Access | Great if you need public-internet access with SSO; more setup than Tailscale for a single user. Keep as option. |
| Remote access | ngrok / raw port forward | Public URL with weak or no auth in front of a process-spawning server. No. |

---
